import { Aside } from '@astrojs/starlight/components';

{/* NOTE: v1.36.10 and v1.36.11 (sidebar nav / icon work) shipped from a separate line of work in parallel with this session's cross-module DX additions; v1.36.12 continues the latter. */}

This page documents notable changes to the Structure module. Versioning follows the module-starter's [patch-default cadence](/module-starter/versioning/#version-bump-commands) rather than textbook semver:

- **Patch** (1.34.25 → 1.34.26): the default — bug fixes, new templates, new features, admin sections, additive schema columns, refactors.
- **Minor** (1.33.x → 1.34.0): rare — a genuinely new subsystem, or a headline release worth signposting.
- **Major** (1.x → 2.0.0): a deliberate breaking epoch requiring coordinated downstream migration.

Pre-launch, breaking renames (such as v1.33.17's `workflow` → `stage`) have shipped as patches; that changes after launch.

Not every release has an entry. Some gaps are routine patch releases that only touched build tooling or docs; others — including a few that carry real template changes — were simply never written up. Every release is recoverable from `git log --grep 'chore(release)'`; the repo carries no release tags, so commit ranges are the only way to walk it.

<Aside type="tip">
Check your installed version with `npx structure info`.
</Aside>

---

## v1.64.0

**Pill badges, a badge from a select field, a switch for the title icon, and no Rules tab** *(generated-output)*

- **Coloured badges are pills.** A badge with a colour shows as a rounded pill with a soft background in that colour and its text in the colour (mixed toward the theme's text colour so it reads in light and dark). The dot is gone. A badge without a colour keeps the theme's own Badge look.
- **Badges are no longer cut short.** The badge has a fixed width cap instead of a share of the row, so a short name no longer cuts it; on a narrow row it still shrinks first and never pushes the name off.
- **A badge from a select field.** The badge panel's **Show a field's value** picks one of the type's select fields; a table lists its options, each with its own **Badge text** (empty shows the option's label) and colour. Stored as `builtInFieldConfig.badge.fromField`: `field` and `options` keyed by option value, each with optional `text` and `colour`. The badge is worked out as: the rules first, then the field's value, then the box's own text, the same on the server and in the browser. The save refuses a field that isn't a select of the type, an option it doesn't have, a text over 500 characters or a colour not offered.
- **Show the icon beside entry names.** A new switch on a type's Basic tab (`navigation.showTitleIcon`, left out when on). Off: the entry's page header and the section switcher's button show no icon (a profile header card shows the photo or initials); the sidebar, lists and pickers keep it.
- **The Rules tab is gone.** Everything it edited is on the Fields tab: Visible when, Required when, the shown settings, notes, Set automatically, the built-in name, description and slug, and the badge. Stored rules are unchanged. `npx structure update` offers to remove the old `RulesTab.tsx`.

Frontend and backend validation. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

---

## v1.63.2

**Pickers scroll inside dialogs; Merge template replaces a formula** *(generated-output)*

- **Pickers scroll.** A field picker opened inside the Merge fields or Conditions dialog (or a form's side panel) could not be scrolled with the mouse wheel, because the dialog held the scroll. Those popovers are now modal, so their lists scroll. This covers the Merge fields field and reference pickers, the merge-field picker, the record picker (NodeSelector), option lists and the chained-option condition picker.
- **Merge template on a value that is a formula.** Opening the Merge fields dialog on a box that holds a formula and building a merge template used to put the tokens inside the formula, so the preview showed "Unexpected token after expression" and saving stored a broken formula. Now the merge template replaces the formula ("This replaces the formula (the value becomes a merge template):"). A template such as `{focus_parent->name}{other_parents->name|before=" & "}` works the same in the preview and on the server.

Frontend only. `npx structure update` brings it in.

## v1.63.1

**Badge panel: colours only where they apply** *(generated-output)*

- The badge panel's colour swatches show only when its own box has text (the badge shown when no rule is true), labelled **Its colour** or **Its colour, when no rule is true**. Each rule's colour is set in the Conditions dialog. Clearing the box clears its colour.

Frontend only. `npx structure update` brings it in.

## v1.63.0

**New linked entries show their name, the server reads through references, and Linked to / Viewer wording** *(generated-output)*

- **New entries in a reference field show their name.** A person created inside a reference field in the create form shows as "Name (new)" instead of "New Person", worked out from the typed fields when the type builds its name. Labels and tests read it before the form is saved.
- **Set automatically can test through a reference** (for example **Parent › Status**, or **Parent › Linked to › a user account**). The server works it out on every save with one batched read, under the same read rules as copies, and works dependants out again when the linked record changes (automatic, or counted in **Ask first**).
- **Badges can test through a reference**, including **Parent › Viewer › is › the linked user**, with one batched read per list.
- **Wording.** Tests are **Field**, **Linked to** (a user account: the stored link) and **Viewer** (an admin, the linked user, the creator). **Created by** and the word "me" are gone; saved tests reopen under the new names. **Set automatically** offers Field and Linked to only.
- **Safeguards found in review:** data keys holding `->` are dropped on every write (they could otherwise steer a rule into setting a locked field); reference tests only read through a declared reference field, and the type's save refuses others; a rule checks at most 500 linked entries; a list's badge reads stay one query even when every row is in its own section; **Ask first** sees rules whose test reads through a reference.
- See [Linked to, Viewer, and the server reading references](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#admin-ui--conditions). Tests: `tests/reference-reads.test.ts`.

Frontend and backend. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

---

## v1.62.0

**One Conditions component: sentence tests, reading through references, Else, Hidden and Show a note, and Try it** *(generated-output)*

- **The same dialog everywhere.** Field settings, Set automatically, the badge and the Rules tab all open one component: **Target / If / Then**, with **Add else** and, for value actions, **Else if**. See [The Conditions dialog](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#admin-ui--conditions).
- **Tests read as sentences** on three lines: what to test, the test, the value. Linked to, Created by and Viewer use **is / is not** with **me**, **a user account**, **an admin** or **a user (not an admin)** instead of a Yes/No box. **Match all / any of these tests** moved into the If card.
- **Reading through references.** A reference field adds **Which of its…**, so a test like **Parent › Linked to › is › me** reads the picked record. Worked out in the forms (and the detail page); value rules and badges refuse reference tests.
- **New actions:** **Hidden**, **Optional**, **Not in the create form**, **Not on the detail page**, **Set the value** (its **Only set it once** works like a default that depends on the tests) and **Show a note**: a note under the field or a tooltip beside its label, in the create and edit forms, stored as the field's `notes`.
- **Try it** shows, for an entry you pick, what each test read, whether it held, and which branch happens.
- **Saved additively.** Negative actions and Else use a `{ not: … }` wrapper with a `written` marker, so rules reopen exactly as written. Older settings are unchanged.
- **Created by: an admin / a user** isn't offered yet: it would need the creator's role looked up on every read. Created by offers **me**.
- For developers: `useConditionReferenceValues` reads picked records through the node cache, keyed by path (`parent->linkedToMe`); negated and reference tests are never pushed into server data filters.

Frontend and backend validation. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

## v1.61.0

**Dialogs, round 2: dropdowns that pick, Target / Conditions / Then sections, several actions, plain built-in boxes, badge rules in the dialog** *(generated-output)*

- **Fixed: the Conditions dialog's dropdowns couldn't be picked** in v1.60.0. Each pick was reset straight away, because the dialog gave the row editor back the condition it opened with instead of the one being edited.
- **Three sections.** The Conditions dialog is laid out as **Target** (the focus field, and **This field** or **Another field**), **If all / any of these are true** and **Then**, joined by a line with a shadcn **Marker** heading each. The module now installs Marker (`shadcnComponents`), so `npx structure update` adds it. Buttons are smaller in both dialogs. See [The Conditions dialog](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#admin-ui--conditions).
- **Another field.** A condition can be written for another field from here; it is saved on that field, with a warning before it replaces one that's already there.
- **Several actions.** **Then** can list **Visible**, **Required**, **In the create form** and **On the detail page** together, so one set of tests drives them all. Removing an action puts that setting back to its default.
- **Built-ins are plain boxes.** The name, description and slug no longer have a **Computed value** heading: each is a text box with the merge button, and the hint "Leave it empty to let people type it. Use `{ }` to fill it in from other fields." The description box takes several lines, and the line breaks show in the page header.
- **Badge.** The badge panel is one box (shown when no rule is true) with a merge button and a conditions button. The rules move into the Conditions dialog as **If / Else if**, each with its own text and colour. The saved format is unchanged.
- For developers: the row editor's state is in `utils/field-conditions` as plain functions (`conditionRowsState`, `syncedConditionRows`, `editConditionRows`, …), tested without a DOM. `ComputedValueEditor` takes `extraAddon`. New `ValueRuleSyncEditor`.

Frontend only. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in, with the Marker component.

## v1.60.0

**A Conditions dialog and a Merge fields dialog: field-builder cleanup, step 1** *(generated-output)*

The first step of making the field builder easier to follow: conditions and merge fields each get one dialog, opened from a button inside the box it belongs to.

- **Conditions dialog.** **Visible When**, **Required When**, **Set automatically** and the **In the create form** / **On the detail page** switches show a short summary ("When Status is Closed and 1 more", "Always") with a branch button. The dialog names its target at the top ("Field: Mother's name · Required when"), asks **If all / any of these are true**, and lists tests: **Field**, **Linked to**, **Created by** or **Viewer**, then which one, the test, and a value that can be typed, another field, or merge-token text. Set automatically reads **If** / **Else if** / **Then set it to** / **Else**. Nothing changes until **Save**. See [The Conditions dialog](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#admin-ui--conditions).
- **Viewer tests.** **Visible When**, **Required When** and the shown settings may now test **Linked to me**, **Created by me** and the new **Viewer is an admin** (`viewerIsAdmin`). Value rules and reference filters still refuse them, and no saved value may use them as tokens. Hiding a field this way is display only.
- **The shown settings take a condition.** `showOnCreate` and `showOnDetail` can hold a condition as well as `true` or `false`.
- **Comparing with a field or merge text.** A test's value can be another field or merge-token text, marked with `valueKind: "field"` or `"template"`. Values without the marker, including every value saved before, are compared exactly as written.
- **Merge fields dialog.** Every box that takes tokens has a braces button that opens the dialog: **Merge template** (rows with the field, part, format, join, and **Before** / **After** / **If empty, show**, each with its own token list) or **Formula**. **Insert token** goes in at the cursor; inside an existing token it becomes **Update token**. **Advanced** is gone: the box is the raw text. See [The Merge fields dialog](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#admin-ui--merge-templates).
- **Joining words is a merge template again.** Tokens can carry `before`, `after`, `ifEmpty`, `join` and `last` options, so text around a value or a custom join no longer saves as a formula. Old formulas keep working. See [Token options](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#token-options-v1600).
- **Limits.** A condition's test value, a field's label and its merge template can each be at most 2000 characters, checked on every save and import. Token scanning was made linear, so very long text can't slow the server down.
- Fixed during review: rule conditions no longer swap places when a rule is moved or removed, a worked-out value can't depend on who saved it, and a badge reads fields named inside token options.
- For developers: `NodeFieldRenderer` takes a separate `conditionData` prop for the viewer keys; `allData` never carries them. New `findViewerReference` / `viewerTokenMessage` in `utils/merge-fields`; `conditionTemplateTokens` / `conditionTokenBaseId` from the shared conditions core; `MergeFieldPicker` is no longer used. Tests: `tests/conditions-dialog.test.ts`, `tests/merge-dialog.test.ts`.

Frontend and backend. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

## v1.59.0

**Labels fill in merge fields, worked-out fields read like values, and field-group tables fit narrow cards** *(generated-output)*

From Kim's screenshots: a label showed its merge-field token as typed (`Who's in {focus_parent->legal_name.first} household?`), a formula field (Age) showed as a grey input box on the page, and an email table overlapped in a narrow side card.

- **Field labels can show the entry's values.** A label with merge-field tokens, such as `{name}` or `{client->name}`, is filled in on the entry's page, the Info tab, fields cards and the create and edit forms. It uses the same rules as card titles: it reads within the entry's section, never reads a secret field (the entry's own, a field-group cell's, or a new linked entry's typed in the same form), and a token that can't be worked out is left out, never shown raw. While it's being worked out, the label shows without its tokens. On a new record of a type that sits in a section, it reads only shared types. The field editor's Label box says: "The label can show the entry's values, like `{name}` or `{client->name}`: they're filled in on the entry's page and in its forms." Only labels: descriptions and help text show as typed, because there's no way to write a literal `{` in them.
- **Where the label shows without its tokens:** messages and screen-reader names that name a field ("please complete…", "Also required:"), the lock hints under rule-set fields, the "N records copy this" prompt, and table and list column headers. The admin's Fields and Rules tabs show the label as typed.
- **Worked-out fields read like values.** On the page (not editing), formula and computed fields now show as "Label / value" like every other field, with "—" when blank, instead of a greyed-out input. Until the value is worked out (or while it can't be, for example while the formula functions are still loading or a linked type isn't known yet), the stored value shows. The edit forms keep the greyed-out box.
- **Field-group tables fit narrow cards.** A field group's rows go to two columns by the card's width (a container query), not the window's, so a narrow side card shows one column. Values wrap instead of running into the next column.
- "Former names" showing "Close" was stored data, not a bug.
- For developers: `FieldLabelText` / `fieldLabelHasTokens` / `templateDataKeys` (labels resolve with only the data their tokens read, so typing elsewhere costs nothing); `withoutSecretTemplateData` (strips secrets, field-group secret cells and `{ $new }` drafts' secrets); a `sharedOnly` option on the section fetcher; `settled` on `useResolvedTemplateResult` and `useResolvedFormula`. Tests: `tests/field-label-tokens.test.ts`.

Frontend only. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

---

## v1.58.1

**Edit no longer crashes** *(generated-output)*

- Clicking **Edit** on a record whose page has a reference field with **Allow editing** or **Allow deleting** on could crash the page with "Rendered more hooks than during the previous render". The Edit and Delete buttons beside a chosen entry checked whether you are an admin only after the entry had loaded, which React does not allow. They now check first. The bug was there from v1.47.2.
- For developers: `ReferenceEntryActions` calls `useIsAdmin()` before its early returns. A new `tests/rules-of-hooks.test.ts` renders every frontend template and fails on a hook after an early return, in a condition, loop, try, callback or render helper, or at file top level (the one allowed exception is `useIsAdmin`'s `useUserProfile()` inside a try, which never changes the hook count).

Frontend only. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

---

## v1.58.0

**"Values already entered" can read reference fields** *(generated-output)*

Kim found that a select's "Values already entered" options could only come from text fields, and needed them to come from node reference fields. Now the **Field** of a "Values already entered" entry can be a reference field: the options are the records already linked in that field. For example, a **Team member** field can offer every person already listed in the **Members** field of a team in this section. Text fields work exactly as before.

- **Setting it up.** In the field editor's **Add options from…** → **Values already entered**, **Field** ("Choose a field") now lists the type's reference fields too, each marked "(links to records)". Choosing one adds **When picked, store** (**A link to the record**, chosen for you, or **Its text**) and, for a link, **Also connect the chosen entries** (on to start with), the same settings a **Records of a type** entry has. The note reads "The records linked in that field, the most linked first. Only records the person can see count, and never across an isolation section." The row's summary reads "Values already entered: Team › Members (records), anywhere", with ", stored as text" for **Its text**.
- **A link** works exactly as a records entry's link: the pick shows the record's current name, linked to its page, follows a rename, reads through in merge fields (`{team_member->x}`), and is kept to the isolation section, stripped and remapped as a reference is. **Its text** stores the record's name at the time of picking.
- **Connect.** With **Also connect the chosen entries** on, each picked record gets a connection named after the field, as for a records entry. A field's link entries all connect, or none do; the switch's note now ends "It applies to every entry of this field that stores a link."
- **What the picker offers.** The records the field links to on the type's records in the scope, the most linked first, then A to Z, up to 200; past that it says "Showing the first 200. Type to find more." The search matches the start of a record's name before any limit, so a record linked only on older records is still found.
- **Who sees what.** **Where** applies to the records that hold the links. A linked record is offered only when the person can read it, and never across the entry's isolation section. When the reference field lists allowed types (on the type and every subtype that has it), only records of those types are offered or accepted.
- **The limits.** On top of the existing 5,000 most recently changed records, only the first 100 items of each record's list count, for the offer and for the checks on save, and at most 20,000 (record, linked record) pairs are read.
- **On save.** Only changed values are checked. A link must name a record the person can read, inside the isolation section, that the field links to on a record they can read in the scope. A name picked with **Its text** must be the name of such a record.
- **In merge fields.** A select whose "Values already entered" entry stores links can name a record of any type, so the **Insert field...** pickers and the merge-field schema offer `{field->name}` and `{field->x}` for every type's fields, and a computed value that reads a secret field of any type through it is refused, by the admin form and the server alike.
- **Imports.** An imported entry on a reference field with no **When picked, store** offers nothing, and its picks are refused, until an admin chooses one ("Choose what a pick stores.").

Every rule is on [Field Types](/modules/structure/fields/#options-from-other-records-and-values-already-entered-v1550), the editor on [Usage](/modules/structure/usage/#options-from-other-records-v1550), and the endpoint on [Configuration](/modules/structure/configuration/#values-already-entered-v1550).

**For developers**
- **The shape.** A used-values entry gains `store?: 'link' | 'text'` and `connect?: boolean`: `store` is required when `field` is a `node-reference` field and refused on a text field, and `connect` is for `store: 'link'` only (absent: on). The admin saves on both halves refuse the mismatches through the shared `usedValuesTargetProblem`; an import checks the shape only. Export and import round-trip the entry as stored.
- **The shared rules** (`structure.option-sources.ts` and `utils/option-sources.ts`): new `usedValuesStore`, `usedValuesReadsRecords`, `usedValueSourcesOf`, `usedValueLinkSourcesOf`, `optionSourceStoresLink`, `optionSourceStoresText`, `optionSourceConnects`, `valuesFieldKind` and `usedValuesTargetProblem`. `holdsRecordLinks` and `connectsRecordLinks` count a used-values entry that stores links, and `recordLinkTypes` returns `[]` (any type) for one. `optionSourceSummary` adds " (records)" and ", stored as text". In `merge-field-tokens`: new `recordLinksUnrestricted` and `recordLinkTargetSlugs`.
- **The endpoint.** `GET /types/:slug/field-values` takes `store=link|text`, required for a reference field (`400` `"members" links to records: say what a pick stores (store "link" or "text")`, so a text field's suggestions, which send none, never read one), and answers `{ values: [{ value, count, label? }], more }`. `structureService.getFieldValues` takes `store` and returns `more`. On the server the read is `readLinkedRecordValues`, with `MAX_LINKED_PER_RECORD` (100) and `MAX_LINKED_PAIRS_SCAN` (20,000); `typesWithReferenceField` leaves out a subtype whose field of that id isn't a reference field.
- **The write checks.** `assertSourcedOptionValues` checks a link no records entry offers against each used-values link entry (one read per entry), and a name pick in one indexed read. `sourcedValueKind` accepts a link only with an entry that stores links, and other text only with one that stores text.
- **The browser.** `connectingReferencePaths` counts a used-values entry that stores links and connects. `SourcedOptionsInput` asks for a used-values entry's values only as the server answers them. The editor's store and connect controls are one shared component for both kinds of entry.
- **Tests.** `tests/used-values-refs.test.ts`; text guards in two existing test files follow the new code.

Frontend and backend. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

---

## v1.57.0

**The "This is me" link as a built-in, `{linked}`, in merge fields, formulas and Build it** *(generated-output)*

Kim asked how to use the "This is me" tick box in merge fields and in Visible When, as a built-in like Name and Status. Visible When already had it: since the [v1.56.1](#v1561) picker fix, **Visible When** and **Required When** list the link first on every type whose entries can be linked. This release makes it a built-in of merge templates, formulas and **Build it** too. Types whose entries can't be linked work exactly as before.

- **Where it works.** On a [profile type](/modules/structure/usage/#profile-types-v1500) with **Entries can be linked to user accounts** turned on, a template or formula can read `{linked}`: a data field's merge template, a formula field, the computed name, description and slug, a card's title and body, a page layout's cards and table columns, and how options from records are shown. Through a reference to an entry of such a type, `{ref->linked}` reads that entry's link.
- **What it reads.** "Yes" or "No" in a merge template, and true or false in a formula. `{linked|label}` gives "Yes" or "No" in both.
- **Linked to anyone, not to you.** `{linked}` says whether the entry is linked to any user account, so a stored value is the same for everyone who looks. Whether it is linked to *you* ("Linked to me") is still something only a [badge](/modules/structure/fields/#the-badge-v1540) can test.
- **When it reads something else.** A type that defines its own field called `linked` keeps that field: `{linked}` reads it. A type whose entries can't be linked (a Record, or linking turned off) reads `{linked}` as blank.
- **Build it.** The **Built-in** group lists it after Status, named with the type's switch label ("This is me" unless the type has its own) and the note "Linked to any account". Through a reference, the entry's built-ins include it when a type the reference can point at can be linked. It always shows as Yes or No, so there's no **How it shows** choice, and Build it writes it as `{linked|label}`, which reads Yes or No in a formula too. "No" isn't blank, so **Text before** and **Text after** show with it and **If empty, show** never does. The preview uses a sample entry that is linked, and its empty case one that isn't ("No").
- **Advanced.** The type editor's **Insert field...** pickers list `{linked}` under **Node basics**, including the ones for the computed name, description and slug. The public merge-field list (`getMergeFieldSchema` and its endpoint) and the page layout editor's **Insert field...** list leave it out; Build it there offers it.
- **Kept current.** Linking or unlinking an entry works out again its stored values that read `{linked}`: the computed name, description and slug, formula fields, and data fields' merge templates, field-group sub-fields included. Then the formulas and computed built-ins that read the entry through a reference follow (`{ref->linked}`), as after any write. This covers every way a link changes: the switch and the link API, a create with "This is me" on, a link that moves to another entry, an assign or a move, a merge, a delete, an account erasure, and a copy (which is never linked). Not covered: a data field's merge template on **another** entry that reads `{ref->linked}`. It follows when that entry is next saved, or when an admin runs **Update entries**, as for any `{ref->x}`.
- **Turning linking on or off.** A type save that turns linking on or off changes what `{linked}` reads. When a stored value of the type reads it, the "Save changes to …?" dialog lists "This is me (linking turned on)" or "This is me (linking turned off)", with the type's own switch label, and **Also update the existing …** works those values out again.
- **Computed fields on the forms.** The create form, the edit form and the detail page now give a computed data field, and a field group's computed and formula sub-fields, the entry they belong to, as the server does. A side effect: `{stage}`, `{status}`, `{reference}` and `{parent->x}` now resolve in those fields on the form too. Before, they read blank there.
- **Lists in formulas.** A node-view of an entry whose type can be linked carries `linked` (true or false), so `map(children(), {linked|label})` and `filter(list, 'linked', true)` read each entry's link. A stray `linked` key in an entry's data never counts.

See [The user-account link](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#the-user-account-link-v1570) on Merge Fields, and [Profile types](/modules/structure/usage/#profile-types-v1500) on Usage.

**For developers**
- **The shared resolver** (`utils/formula.ts`, both halves). New `LINKED_TOKEN` (`'linked'`, the same text as `CONDITION_LINKED_KEY`). `FormulaTokenOptions` gains `linkable?: FormulaLinkableLookup` (`(nodeType) => boolean | null`: can this type's entries be linked?) and `asText?: boolean` (a merge template sets it, so the link reads Yes or No). Without `linkable`, no type counts as linkable, and `{linked}` reads its data key as before. `FormulaTokenHost`, `FormulaContext` and `FormulaNodeSource` gain `linked?`. `formulaNodeView` carries `linked` when the source gives a boolean, over a data key of that name. `fieldLabelOptions` gives Yes and No for the built-in `linked`.
- **The browser.** New `registryLinkableLookup` (the default `linkable` of `resolveTemplateAsync`, `resolveTokenValue` and `resolveMergeFields`) and `registryLinkLabel(slug)` (the switch label, or `null`), from `utils/merge-fields` and the utils barrel. `resolveMergeFields` reads `node.linked`, and `toNodeView` carries the link on a type whose entries can be linked and that defines no field called `linked`.
- **The merge builder** (`utils/merge-builder.ts`). New `LINKED_BUILT_IN`, added to `BUILDER_BUILT_INS` (labelled "Linked to a user account" until a type's switch label replaces it), `BUILT_IN_TARGET_BUILT_INS`, `FIELD_TARGET_BUILT_INS` and `REFERENCE_TARGET_BUILT_INS`. `builderFieldsOf` offers it only with the new options `link?: { label } | null` (the host type's `profileLinkSettings`) and `linkLabelOf?(slug)` (for references). `BuilderField` gains `hint?`. `changedComputedDefinitions(before, after, { link? })` takes a `ComputedLinkChange` (`{ before, after, label }`) and adds `{ key: 'linked', label: '… (linking turned on)', slug }` when linking changes and a stored value reads `{linked}`.
- **The server.** The formula engine reads the stored `linkedUserId` (a create's `linkToMe` before it is stored), through the host's new optional `linkableTypes(userId)`. `formulaEngine.linkChanged(userId, ids, self?)` runs after every link change and after a duplicate: it recomputes the entries' values that read `linked`, as an update of the existing entries does (a system write, `structure.node.updated` with `source: 'formula'`), then the dependants' formulas, with the usual bounds. It never throws. `recomputeTypeValues` and `POST /types/:slug/recompute` take `linked` in `fields`. The server's node-views carry the link too.
- **Tests.** `tests/linked-builtin.test.ts`. Text guards in four existing test files follow the new code.

Frontend and backend. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

## v1.56.1

**A Task type kind, and the Visible When picker on every type** *(generated-output)*

- **Task.** On a type's Basic tab, **Type kind** now offers **Task (managed by a tasks module)** beside Record and Profile. A Task type keeps no profile settings (only a Profile type can link its entries to user accounts), and a note says "A tasks module can find this type and manage its entries as tasks. Structure keeps it as an ordinary type until one does." Structure itself treats a Task type as an ordinary type; a tasks module decides what it means.
- **Visible When and Required When always pick from a list.** A type whose entries can't be linked to user accounts showed a typed "Field ID" box instead of the field picker (since v1.50.0). Every type now gets the picker, as the Rules tab always did, with the user-account link first on a type that has one.
- For developers: `features.profile.kind` takes `'record' | 'profile' | 'task'`; a save with another kind is refused with `Profile settings: "Type kind" must be Record, Profile or Task.`, and `linkUsers` on a Task type is refused as before (only a Profile type). New `typeKindOf(nodeType)` in `utils/profile-link` (exported from the utils barrel) returns the kind, own or inherited, defaulting to `'record'`.

Frontend and a backend validation change. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

## v1.56.0

**A "Connections" field: an entry's connections, listed and edited in place** *(generated-output)*

Kim asked for a "How they're connected" field done "as a connection rather than a node reference": a parent and a child, with the connection's own role field saying "mother". A new field type, **Connections**, lists an entry's connections of the connection types the admin chooses, and adds, edits and removes them right there. It stores nothing: it is a live view of the entry's connections, so it always agrees with the **Connections** tab, and every existing connection of those types shows in it. Types without the field work exactly as before.

- **Where it's set up.** In the field editor of a node type or a field template, choose **Connections** (under **References**). Its settings: **Relationships** (one or more connection types; with one, "Always this relationship: …"), **This record is** (**Either end**, **The "from" end** or **The "to" end**), **Connected to** (types), **Only records that…**, **Where** (once a type is isolated), **Show each as** (Build it, with **The connected record** and **The relationship** among its fields), **Show these connection fields**, **Allow adding**, **Allow editing**, **Allow removing** and **At most**. Its help text: "Shows this record's connections of these types. Adding one makes the connection; removing one deletes it."
- **The rows.** Each row is the other record, linked to its page, with its [badge](#v1540), then the relationship in brackets, then " — Label: value" for each shown field: "Sam (Related) — Role: mother". **Show each as** replaces the row's text; besides the connection's fields it can use `{other}` (the other record's name) and `{type}` (the relationship's name). Secret fields and fields that link to records are never shown.
- **Add, Edit and Remove.** **Add** opens a small form: **Connected to** (the field's types narrowed to the relationship's end types), **Relationship** when the field offers more than one, then the connection's own fields. If the two records already have that connection (either way round for a two-way relationship), Add edits it instead of making a second, or says "Already connected. This connection can't be changed here." when a reference field made it or editing isn't allowed. **Edit** changes a connection's fields. **Remove** asks first ("Remove this connection?").
- **When it's saved.** The changes wait for the save, then go through the existing connection API, so the connection type's rules and its fields' checks apply. On the detail page they are part of **Save changes**, **Cancel** drops them, and leaving asks first. The create form makes them once the record exists. The edit form makes them before it saves the rest. A change that fails stays, with "Not saved: …" under its row, and a message lists each one.
- **Safe edits.** An edit sends only the values that were changed, written over the connection as it is at the save. If the record's connections can't be read then, nothing is written.
- **What it can't do.** It takes no default and can't be required, set by a value rule or made one per section, and a write that gives it a value is refused. **Visible When**, **Required When**, value rules and badge rules can't test it. It can't be a field-group sub-field, or a connection type's own field (including through a field template the connection type attaches). A field template can carry it, and a subtype inherits it.
- **In text and tables.** `{field}` in a merge template works out blank, and the pickers leave it out; a formula can use `connections(type)`. It isn't offered as a table column or a card field, and card and list text skip it.
- **At most** is checked in the browser only. The connection type's own **Most per entry** is the limit the server holds.

Every rule is on [Field Types](/modules/structure/fields/#connections), the admin screens on [Usage](/modules/structure/usage/#connections-fields-v1560), and the JSON in [the JSON reference](/modules/structure/json-reference/#connections-fields-v1560).

**For developers**
- **The shape.** `connectionsConfig: { connectionTypes, direction?, otherTypes?, filter?, scope?, label?, showFields?, allowAdd?, allowEdit?, allowRemove?, maxItems? }` on a field of type `connections`, with `direction` one of `'either' | 'from' | 'to'` and `scope` one of `'section' | 'any'`.
- **One set of rules for both halves.** The new `src/templates/shared/connections-field.ts` is rendered into the frontend's `utils/connections-field.ts` (with the browser's staging helpers) and the backend's `structure.connections-field.ts`: `connectionsSettingsOf`, `connectionsConfigProblem`, `connectionsFieldProblem`, `connectionsFieldsProblem`, `connectionsLabelReads`, `connectionsLabelSecretReads`, `connectionSecretPaths`, `connectionMatchesDirection`, `connectionOtherEnd`, `connectionEndsFor`, `isSameConnection` and `connectionsOfField`, with `CONNECTIONS_FIELD_TYPE`, `MAX_CONNECTIONS_FIELD_TYPES` (20), `MAX_CONNECTIONS_FIELD_OTHER_TYPES` (50), `MAX_CONNECTIONS_SHOW_FIELDS` (50), `MAX_CONNECTIONS_FIELD_ITEMS` (1000), `MAX_CONNECTIONS_LABEL_LENGTH` (500) and `CONNECTIONS_ROW_LIMIT` (200).
- **The staging.** A form keeps a field's changes in its slot of the data as `{ $connectionChanges: [...] }`. `splitConnectionChanges(data, fields)` takes them out before the entry's save; `planConnectionWrites(entryId, changes, existing, bidirectional)` turns them into creates, updates and deletes (an add of an existing connection becomes an update, an edit is merged over the stored data, a change to a connection that isn't the entry's is ignored); `useApplyConnectionChanges()` reads the entry's connections, then applies the writes in order through `structureService.createConnection`, `updateConnection` and `deleteConnection`, one apply at a time per entry, and resolves `{ applied, failed, ignored }`.
- **`NodeForm` creates.** A create's `onSubmit` should resolve the created entry, so the form can make its staged connections. Structure's own create handlers now do; one that resolves nothing gets "The record was created, but its connections weren't made: open it and add them again."
- **The server.** `GET /nodes/:id/connections?types=a,b` (at most 20 names, each up to 100 characters, else `400`) reads only those connection types: `getNodeConnections(nodeId, userId, { connectionTypes })`. `assertFieldValueShapes` refuses a value for the field, for every caller. `connectionsFieldsProblem` runs in the shape check every field-set save makes, imports included; `assertConnectionsFieldTargets` also checks the names on the admin saves (node types, proposals, field templates). `getConnectionFieldDefs` leaves out a Connections field, and a connection type can't attach a field template that holds one (`assertConnectionTemplatesTakeNoConnectionsField`).
- **The browser.** `structureService.getNodeConnections(nodeId, { types })`, and the new `updateConnection(connectionId, { data?, metadata? })` and `getConnection(connectionId)` (`null` on a `404`); the hook `useNodeConnectionsOfTypes(id, types)`; the components `ConnectionsField` (`components/fields/ConnectionsField.tsx`) and `ConnectionsFieldSettings`, with `connectionsSaveProblem` (`components/admin/ConnectionsFieldEditor.tsx`). Types: `ConnectionsFieldConfig`, `ConnectionsDirection`, `ConnectionsScope`, `NodeTypeField.connectionsConfig` and `'connections'` in `NodeTypeFieldType`. See [Connections Fields](/modules/structure/integration/#connections-fields-v1560) on Integration.
- **Tests.** `tests/connections-field.test.ts`; the text guards in five existing test files follow the new save code.

Frontend and backend. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

## v1.55.1

**A field template's fields form a section of their own** *(generated-output)*

Kim asked for field templates to become a section automatically, unless the template has sections of its own. Sections nested inside a template are left for later.

- **Named after the template.** A field from a shared field template that the template gives no section is in a section named after the template. A field the template does give a section keeps it, so a template's own section names become sections of the type (not nested).
- **On a type with sections,** a template's fields that the sections don't place, and whose section matches none of the type's, appear as their own section after the type's sections, instead of in **Other fields**. **Sections and field order** saves it, under the same id, on your next change there; then it's an ordinary section.
- **Without sections,** the Info tab groups the template's fields under the template's name, as it groups any section label.
- **The type's own fields are unchanged:** a section label that matches no section still puts an own field in **Other fields**.
- For developers: `withTemplateSection(fields, templateName)` in the backend (applied in `loadFieldTemplateFields`, so every resolved `resolved.fields` carries it); `groupFieldsByLayout` returns automatic groups (`automatic: true`) between the stored sections and the fields in no section; new `automaticSectionId(label, taken)`; `placedFieldLayout` stores automatic groups as sections. Tests: `tests/template-sections.test.ts`, and four `field-sections` expectations updated for template fields with labels.

Frontend and backend. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

## v1.55.0

**Options drawn from other records, and suggestions from values already entered** *(generated-output)*

Kim found that node references weren't joined up enough, and asked for a select's options to be "the values from another field, or node type", with conditions, such as records from the primary scope. On what a pick should store, she said "admin choice on that, but default link to record and create connection". A select or multiselect field's option list can now hold, besides its typed options and in the order the admin sets, **Records of a type** and **Values already entered**. Text fields can **Suggest values already entered** as people type. Everything is set up by the admin in the field editor. A field without these settings works exactly as before.

- **Where it's set up.** In the field editor, a select or multiselect's **Options** now has **Add an option** (it said "Add Option") and **Add options from…**, with two choices: **Records of a type** and **Values already entered**. Each row, typed or not, can be moved up and down, so the typed options and the sources share one order. A source shows as one summarised row, such as "Records: Supplier, anywhere, shown as `{name}`", with **Edit**, the move buttons and **Delete this source**. A field can have up to 10 sources.
- **Records of a type.** Its settings:
  - **Type(s)**: "Its records are offered, and the records of its subtypes."
  - **Only records that…**: the condition builder, over the records' fields: "Offer, and accept, only the records that match this condition."
  - **Where**: **In this** (the name of the app's isolated primary scope type, offered only when there is one), **Beside this entry (the same parent)**, **Under this entry** or **Anywhere they can see**. "Only records the person can see are offered, and never across an isolation section."
  - **Show them as**: Build it, the record's name unless you change it. "What the picker shows for each record. It can't read a secret field."
  - **When picked, store**: **A link to the record** (the default: "A link shows the record's current name, follows a rename and opens the record's page.") or **Its text** ("The text is kept as it is when picked: no link, and no connection.").
  - **Also connect the chosen entries**, for a link, on by default: each picked record gets a connection named after the field, shown on both entries' **Connections** tab, exactly as a reference field's [connections](/modules/structure/fields/#connecting-the-chosen-entries-v1460) work. One switch covers all of a field's link sources.
- **Values already entered.** **Type**, **Field** (one of that type's text fields) and **Where**: "The values people have typed in that field, the most used first. Only records the person can see count." A pick stores the text.
- **On the forms.** A select or multiselect with sources opens a searchable list: the typed options (with their descriptions) and each source's records or values, in the admin's order, with **Search...**. A records source lists the first 50 records that match, then "Showing the first 50. Type to find more." A select has **Clear the choice**; a multiselect's picks show under the button, each with a remove button.
- **Suggest values already entered.** A text, long text, email, URL or phone field has the switch: "As people type, offer the values already entered, the most used first. They can still type anything." **From** is **This field, on this type** or a text field of another type, and **Where** the same choices. The input lists up to 8 values that start with what's typed; arrow keys move through them, Enter takes one, Escape closes the list.
- **What a pick shows.** A link shows the record's current name, linked to its page, with its [badge](#v1540), or "An entry you can't see" when the record is gone or the viewer can't read it. Never its id. This holds on the Info tab, the detail page, fields cards and the table view's column. A card's or list row's field line counts the links ("2 linked entries"). A text pick shows as stored.
- **In merge fields.** `{field}` and `{field|label}` give a linked record's name, and `{field->x}` reads field `x` of the picked record, as `{ref->x}` does for a reference field. A stored value that reads through a pick is worked out again when the picked record changes. In a formula, a bare `{field}` stays the link, so `nodes({field})` reads the records.
- **What the save checks.** A pick of a record must exist, be one the writer can read, be of one of the source's types, match its condition and its **Where**, and keep to the isolation section. A text pick must be a record's name in scope (with the plain label) or a copy of the text the picker showed (with a label of its own). A value already entered must still be held by that field on a record the writer can read. Only values a save changes are checked.

**Behaviour that changes**
1. **Typed options can be reordered.** Every select and multiselect option row now has **Move up** and **Move down**, and the button that adds one says **Add an option**.
2. **A typed option's value can't start with `node:`.** That marks a link to a record. A save that has one is refused: `the option value "node:x" cannot start with "node:" (that marks a link to a record)`. This only applies to a field with sources.
3. **A field's sources go with the type change.** Changing a field to another type takes off sources it can't keep (only a select or multiselect has them) and suggestions (only a text field has them).

**Known gaps**
- Conditions and list filters can't pick a record yet. **Is one of** offers the typed options; **Is blank** and **Is not blank** work for any pick.
- On a new top-level entry with no section yet, a records source lists the records of every section, but the save refuses the ones across the isolation section.
- The page shows a linked record's current name, not the **Show them as** label.

**For developers**
- **The shape.** `options` stays the typed options only, so every reader of it is unchanged. The new `optionSources` holds the source entries, each `{ id, kind, position?, … }`: `{ kind: 'records', nodeTypes, filter?, scope, label?, store?: 'link' | 'text', connect? }` or `{ kind: 'used-values', nodeType, field, scope }`, with `scope` one of `'section'`, `'siblings'`, `'children'` or `'any'`. `position` is the entry's place in the combined list; two at one position keep their stored order. A text field's `suggestFrom` is `{ nodeType?, field?, scope? }`. See [the JSON reference](/modules/structure/json-reference/#option-sources-and-suggestions-v1550).
- **Link values.** A pick stored as a link is the text `node:` and the record's id (`RECORD_LINK_PREFIX`), so a select stays text and a multiselect a list of texts. The public API, `getNode`, `queryNodes` and AI Core's record reads return it as stored. Server data filters and conditions compare the stored text.
- **One set of rules for both halves.** The new `src/templates/shared/option-sources.ts` is rendered into the frontend's `utils/option-sources.ts` and the backend's `structure.option-sources.ts`: `optionSourcesOf`, `optionListEntries` and `splitOptionList` (the combined list), `recordLinkValue`, `recordLinkId`, `recordLinkIdsIn`, `withoutRecordLinks`, `remapRecordLinks`, `holdsRecordLinks`, `recordLinkTypes`, `connectsRecordLinks`, `optionSourcesProblem`, `suggestFromProblem`, `optionSourceFieldsProblem`, `optionScopeLabel` and `optionSourceSummary`, with `MAX_OPTION_SOURCES` (10), `USED_VALUES_LIMIT` (200), `RECORD_SOURCE_PAGE` (50), `MAX_RECORD_LABEL_LENGTH` (500), `MAX_RECORD_SOURCE_TYPES` (50) and `SUGGESTION_FIELD_TYPES` (`text`, `textarea`, `email`, `url`, `phone`).
- **Where they work.** Top-level fields of node types and field templates, and the fields a type inherits. A field-group sub-field (`a field inside a group cannot take options from other records`) and a connection type's field (`the fields of a connection type cannot take options from other records`) are refused, and likewise for suggestions.
- **Write checks.** `validateNodeData` holds every caller, system writes included, to what a field's sources can give (`sourcedValueKind`): a typed option, a link when a source stores links, other text when a source stores text or reads values already entered, at most 100 picks a field (`MAX_PICKS_PER_FIELD`). For every write that isn't the system's, `assertSourcedOptionValues` checks the values the write changes, with one statement per source (`optionSourceSql`) and all the write's links in one read: create, update, bulk create, a duplicate's overrides, and the entries a reference field creates (`$new`, `assertPendingSourcedValues`). A text pick with a label of its own is taken as plain text of at most 500 characters. Refusals: `Field "x" picks an entry that was not found`, `Field "x" cannot pick "Name": it is not one of the records this field offers`, `Field "x" can only pick entries in the same isolation section, or of a shared type: "Name" is outside it` and `Field "x" has an invalid option ("…")`.
- **Links as references.** A `node:` value in a top-level select or multiselect is read as a reference by the delete-and-keep and assign strip, the move wall, the wall on create, update, duplicate and restore, the duplicate remap and the formula recompute, whatever the field's sources say now. A merge's backfill carries a link, and connects. Connections reuse the reference-field machinery (`referenceConnectFieldsOf` takes the field with `links: true`); a field's link sources all connect or none do.
- **Save checks.** Every path that saves fields checks the shape (`assertOptionFieldConfigs` runs `optionSourceFieldsProblem`); an import checks only that. The admin saves (node types, proposals, field templates) also check that each type and field named is there (`assertOptionSourceTargets`): `Field "x": option source 1 names the type "y", which is not one of the node types` or `… reads "z", which is not a text field (values already entered are read from text fields only)`. The secret guard reads a records source's label: `Field "x": option source 1 may not show its records with the secret field "y": a label shows in pickers and lists`.
- **The values read.** `structureService.getFieldValues(slug, userId, { field, q?, scope?, entryId?, parentId?, newSection? }, { isAdmin? })`, over HTTP `GET /types/:slug/field-values`, returns `{ values: [{ value, count }] }`: at most 200, the most used first, counted over the 5,000 most recently changed records in scope after the prefix search. A field that isn't a top-level text field of the type, or is a secret, is a `400`; a type or host the caller can't read is a `404`. See [Option Sources and Values Already Entered](/modules/structure/integration/#option-sources-and-values-already-entered-v1550).
- **Frontend.** `components/fields/FieldOptions.tsx` adds `SourcedOptionsInput`, `SuggestingTextInput`, `SelectedOptionsValue` and `UNSEEN_ENTRY_TEXT`; `components/admin/OptionEditors.tsx` adds `SuggestFromEditor` and `optionSourceSaveProblem`, and `SelectOptionsEditor` takes `optionSources`, `sourcesAllowed` and `conditionEditor`, its `onChange` now `(options, optionSources)`. `structureService.getFieldValues(slug, query, { signal })` in the browser. The merge-field schema offers `{field->x}` for a field that holds links (`recordLinkTargets`). Types `OptionSource`, `RecordsOptionSource`, `UsedValuesOptionSource`, `OptionSourceScope` and `SuggestFromConfig`; `NodeTypeField` gains `optionSources` and `suggestFrom`.

**Tests.** 47 new tests in `tests/option-sources.test.ts`: the shared rules in both halves (the combined order, the link tag, the save's refusals), the resolver's names and `{field->x}`, the save and the config round trip, the write checks (exists, readable, type, filter, scope, only changed values, 100 picks, text picks, bulk create), scopes and the wall, the review fixes (reference-field entries, shared types, duplicate and restore, a link after its sources change), values already entered (rights, the wall, secrets refused, the bound) and the endpoint's parameters, connections, delete stripping, merge, duplicate and the move wall, shared fields, the recompute, fields without sources, and text guards.

Frontend and backend. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

---

## v1.54.0

**A Badge built-in: a short label next to an entry's name** *(generated-output)*

Kim asked for "something that can go in a badge next to the name", built like the name, but able to say "if value equals/not equals this, display "this"". Her example was "You" on the profile linked to the person looking. A type can now have a **Badge**: rules of "when this, show that", read in order, and an optional Otherwise. A badge is worked out for whoever is looking, so the same entry can say "You" to the person it is linked to and show nothing to anyone else. Everything is set up by the admin. A type without a badge works exactly as before.

- **Where it's set up.** The type form has a **Badge** block in the **Fields** tab's built-in fields, after Name, Description and Slug, and a **Badge** card in the **Rules** tab's **Worked-out values**. Both edit the same setting. The block opens with: "A short label shown next to the name in lists, pickers and the page header. It's worked out for whoever is looking, so "Linked to me" can say "You" to just that person."
- **Rules.** **Add a rule** adds a row of **When** (the condition builder), **Show** (the text) and **In** (a colour). Each row can be moved up, moved down or removed, and the first rule that matches wins. A type can have up to 20 rules.
- **Otherwise.** **Otherwise show** gives the badge when no rule matches. With no rules it reads **Always show**, for a badge on every entry.
- **The text.** Fixed words, like "You" or "Urgent", or a value from the entry, put together with **Build it** or typed under **Advanced** (placeholder "e.g. You, or `{status|label}`"), the same way as a computed name. Its hint: "Fixed words, like "You", or a value from the entry. Leave it empty to show no badge." Unlike a computed name, a badge's text can read the entry's own name, description and slug.
- **What shows.** Spaces and line breaks become single spaces. A badge longer than 40 characters is cut, ending in "…", and the whole text shows when you hover over it. A text that comes out blank shows no badge. A rule that matches with a blank text shows no badge either: it doesn't move on to the next rule or to Otherwise, so an empty text is a way to say "no badge for these".
- **Colours.** **In** offers the same 12 swatches as Structure's other colour pickers, or none. A colour shows as the badge's border and a small dot beside the text, and the text keeps its normal colour, so it reads in light and dark mode. With no colour the badge has the plain style.
- **Linked to me and Created by me.** The badge's condition builder lists two choices only a badge has, first: **Linked to me** (the entry is linked to your own user account) and **Created by me** (you created the entry). Then come **Linked to a user account** on a type whose entries can be linked, the built-ins (**Name**, **Description**, **Web address (slug)**, **Stage** and **Status (active / archived)**) and the type's fields. It never offers a secret, a field group, a section break, or a formula that is worked out only when shown.
- **Kim's example.** On a profile type, add a rule: When **Linked to me** **Is on**, show "You". The person linked to an entry sees "You" beside its name. Everyone else sees no badge on it.
- **The preview.** Under the rules, **Preview:** shows the badge as it comes out for the type's first active entry ("For "*Jane Smith*", as you see it."), or for sample values ("With sample values.") when the type has no entries yet. It says "(no badge)" when none would show. **As if it's linked to me** and **As if I created it** show what someone else would see.
- **Where it shows.** Beside the name in the detail page's header, list rows and cards (the Children tab's too), the table view's name column (and a page layout's table card, in a column that is just `{name}`), a page layout's featured cards and header card, the entry picker (its options and the chosen entry, so the scope selector in the header too), a reference field's linked entries, and the tree. The list's search and the tree's search show it on their results. An entry has at most one badge. On a narrow row the badge shrinks first, so the name stays readable.
- **Where it doesn't.** The breadcrumbs, the Connections tab and the sidebar's entry links. The public REST API, AI Core's record reads and other modules never get one: a badge belongs to a person looking, and they have none.
- **Stored values only.** A badge reads what is stored on the entry, so every page shows the same badge. A formula that is worked out only when shown isn't offered, and the save refuses a badge that reads one.
- **What the save refuses.** The form and the server check the badge on every save, naming the place, such as "Badge, rule 2: its condition tests "x", which is not a field of this type". A badge can't read a secret field, directly, through a reference or through the parent: "The badge (rule 1) reads secret field "x". A badge shows in lists and pickers, so it cannot read a secret field." A rule that has no condition yet is left out of the save until it has one, so a half-made rule never blocks a save. On a problem, the form opens the **Badge** block (or stays on the **Rules** tab).

**Behaviour that changes**
1. **A badge replaces "This is me".** Kim's decision: when a type has a badge of its own (any rule, or an Otherwise), the built-in "This is me" mark no longer shows on that type's entries anywhere: cards, list rows, the table, the detail page's header and header cards. The type's badge shows instead. Types without a badge keep "This is me". To keep those words on a type with a badge, add a rule: When **Linked to me** **Is on**, show "This is me". A header card's **Show the "This is me" badge** switch only turns the "This is me" mark on or off: the type's own badge shows whatever it says.
2. **Linked to me and Created by me are a badge's alone.** A value rule, a **Visible When**, a **Required When** or a reference field's **Only entries where…** that tests one is refused when it is saved, because those rules are stored or checked on the server and can't depend on who is looking. Their builders don't list the two, so this only stops a condition typed in by hand or brought in by an import. A field the type defines with one of those ids is read as that field, as before.

**For developers**
- **The setting.** `builtInFieldConfig.badge?: { rules?: Array<{ when, text, colour? }>, otherwise?: { text, colour? } }`. `when` is a condition in the `visibleWhen` grammar, `text` a merge template, and `colour` one of `BADGE_COLOURS` (the 12 swatch hex values, any case; anything else reads as no colour). Limits: `MAX_BADGE_RULES` (20), `MAX_BADGE_TEMPLATE_LENGTH` (500 characters as written) and `MAX_BADGE_TEXT_LENGTH` (40 characters shown, a code point each, the last an ellipsis). The types are `NodeBadgeConfig`, `NodeBadgeRule`, `NodeBadgeOtherwise` and `NodeBadge` (`{ text, colour? }`), exported in both halves. The config round-trips through export and import with the rest of `builtInFieldConfig`. Each type has its own badge: like the rest of `builtInFieldConfig`, it isn't inherited. See [the JSON reference](/modules/structure/json-reference/#builtinfieldconfigbadge-v1540).
- **One set of rules for both halves.** The new `src/templates/shared/node-badge.ts` is rendered into the frontend's `utils/node-badge.ts` and the backend's `structure.badge.ts`, so the type form's preview, the server's reads and the save agree. It holds `badgeConfigProblem`, `workOutBadge`, `badgeBranch`, `finishBadgeText`, `badgeColourOf`, `hasBadge`, `badgeConfigOf`, `badgeConditionRecord`, `withBadgeViewerValues`, `conditionViewerTargets`, `badgeTemplates`, `badgeReadIds` and `badgeReadsData`, with `BADGE_VIEWER_KEYS` (`linkedToMe`, `ownedByMe`) and `BADGE_VIEWER_LABELS`.
- **Worked out per reader, on the server.** `listNodes`, `getNode`, `getNodeNames`, `getTree` and `searchNodes` take `badges?: boolean` (`KeywordSearchOptions.badges` for search). With it, each row of a type with a badge carries `badge?: { text, colour? }`, absent when none shows (`StructureNode.badge`, `NodeNameRecord.badge`, and `'badge'` on `NodeTreeNode`). Only the four session routes pass it: `GET /nodes`, `GET /nodes/:id`, `POST /nodes/names` and `GET /tree`. `searchNodes` has no route, so an in-process caller that wants badges must pass it. A badge is never stored and never on a write's answer. See [Badges on Reads](/modules/structure/integration/#badges-on-reads-v1540).
- **How a read works it out.** The formula engine's `badgesFor(viewerId, rows, configs)`, over the rows the read returns (at most 5,000). The conditions read the row's stored condition record, less secrets and on-read formula values, with the reader's `linkedToMe` and `ownedByMe`. A `{token}` reads the row's own data first. A record is read only for a token that needs one (`{ref->x}`, `{parent->x}`), through the reader's confined reads (the section wall, the reader's readable types, secrets removed), shared by the read's rows: one wall per type and section root, one record cache per section, and at most 200 distinct records (`MAX_BADGE_RECORD_READS`). Past the budget, a row that needs a new record shows no badge. The types' badges are cached with the type config. A badge never fails a read. The names read reads the badge columns again by id (with data only when a badge reads a data field, `badgeReadsData`); the tree selects the extra columns when asked and takes them out again.
- **Save checks.** `assertBadgeConfigSave` checks the shape on every save; the configuration import and proposals check the shape only. An admin save that brings the badge in or changes it also checks that each condition tests a field of the type (own, from a field template, inherited, or recorded under `integrations.fields`), a built-in, `linked`, `linkedToMe` or `ownedByMe`, and refuses an on-read formula: `Badge, rule 1: it reads "Age", which is worked out only when an entry is shown, so a badge can't use it (a badge reads stored values)`. The secret guard reads the badge's texts, the fields its conditions test and their `{token}` values: `The badge (rule 1) may not read secret field "x" — a badge shows in lists and pickers, so it would expose the secret`.
- **A blank `{token}`.** A single-condition `contains`, `greaterThan` or `lessThan` whose value holds a `{token}` is refused in a badge (`Badge, rule 1: the contains test on "notes" holds a {token}: use rules for a {token} value`), because the evaluator skips such a test when its value is blank, which would make the rule hold for every entry. The badge editor saves that condition in the `rules` form (`badgeConditionForSave`).
- **The viewer targets elsewhere.** `assertNoViewerConditionTargets` runs in `assertSafeFieldPatterns`, so every path that saves fields checks it: `Field "x": its Visible When tests "Linked to me", which only a badge can test (it's worked out for whoever is looking)`, and likewise `its Required When`, `value rule 2` and `its "Only entries where" filter`. A field with that id wins: on a node type's admin save, any field the type has (own, field templates', inherited); in a reference filter, a field of a type it may point at; on other saves, the fields saved.
- **Frontend.** `NodeBadge` (`components/node/NodeBadge.tsx`, from `components/node`) shows a read's badge. `BadgeEditor` and `BADGE_HELP_TEXT` (`components/admin/BadgeEditor.tsx`, from `components/admin`). `RulesTab` takes `onBadgeChange` and `badgeBuilderFields`. `useNodeNames` entries carry `badge`. New utilities from `@/features/structure/utils`: `badgeColourOf`, `badgeConditionRecord`, `badgeConditionTargets`, `badgeConfigOf`, `badgeConfigProblem`, `badgeStyle`, `badgeTemplates`, `conditionViewerTargets`, `finishBadgeText`, `hasBadge`, `workOutBadge`, `withBadgeViewerValues`, `BADGE_COLOURS`, `BADGE_VIEWER_KEYS`, `BADGE_VIEWER_LABELS`, `MAX_BADGE_RULES`, `MAX_BADGE_TEMPLATE_LENGTH` and `MAX_BADGE_TEXT_LENGTH`. `showsLinkBadge` is now false for a type with a badge, and `ThisIsMeBadge`'s `nodeType` takes `builtInFieldConfig`.

**Tests.** 33 new tests in `tests/badge.test.ts`: the shared rules in both halves (first match, Otherwise, blank means none, cutting, colours), the save checks and the import's shape-only check, the secret guard, Linked to me and Created by me for different readers, the refusal in value rules, visibility, required and filters, badges on each read path, the shared record reads and their budget, on-read formulas, the blank `{token}` guard, the config round trip, "This is me" replaced, and text guards for the editor and every display site.

Frontend and backend. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

## v1.53.2

**A type's place, chosen like the sidebar's** *(generated-output)*

- On a type's Basic tab, the **Order** number box is now **Position**: **First**, **After** another type, or **Last**, the same way **Sidebar position** works. It decides where the type comes in lists of types, such as the **+ New** menu and the types list's **Order** sort. Its place in the sidebar is still set under Sidebar Navigation.
- When there's no room between the two types either side (they share a number, or **First** would go below 0), the save renumbers the other types 10 apart, keeping their order, so the type lands exactly where you chose.
- For developers: a new `placeAtPosition(siblings, position, min = 0)` in `components/admin/OrderControls.tsx` returns `{ order, renumber }`. `PositionSelect` takes an optional `onPlace` (exact placement) and `id`, and `onChange` is now optional. The renumbering uses the existing `POST /types/reorder` endpoint, at most 200 types per call.

Frontend only. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

- For developers: code comments for the rules v2 work now name v1.52.0, the release it shipped in.
