## v1.53.0

**Chained select as a tree of options, and a description, source and link on every option** *(generated-output)*

Kim found the chained select's levels confusing and asked: "Instead of levels, can we have the top level option, and then add their sub level options". She also asked for "description and source/link" on each option, so options can be rich in content without being node types of their own. A chained select is now a tree: people pick one top-level option, and each option can ask a follow-up question with its own sub-options, picked one or several at a time, to any depth. Every option of a select, multiselect or chained select can carry a description, a source and a link. Everything is set up by the admin in the field editor.

- **Descriptions, sources and links on options.** Each option row in the field editor has **More**, which shows **Description**, **Source** and **Link**; while it is closed, an option that has any of them shows a small marker.
  - In the pickers, the description shows as a muted second line under the label.
  - On the Info tab, the detail page and fields cards, a picked option with content has an info button (named "About" and its label), which opens the description and "Source:" with its link, or the link alone.
  - Tables and other text show the label only.
  - A link must start with `http:`, `https:` or `mailto:`. It is checked when the type is saved, in the form and on the server, and again before it shows as a link, so an older or imported `javascript:` link never becomes one. A description can be 2,000 characters, a source 200 and a link 2,000.
- **A chained select is a tree.** The top level is one dropdown. Under each picked option that has sub-options come its heading and a dropdown (**Pick one**) or toggle buttons (**Pick several**), indented, and so on down, at most six levels and 1,000 options. Changing a pick clears what was under it, and unticking an option clears its sub-picks. "Chained" just means nested: there is no separate setting.
- **A new editor.** The field's **Chained select** section has **Placeholder** ("People pick one of these. Give an option sub-options to ask a follow-up question."), then **Options**, each row with its label and value, **More**, move up and down, delete, and **Add sub-options**.
  - An option with sub-options shows their count ("3 sub-options") and expands to **How are they picked?** (**Pick one** or **Pick several**), an optional **Heading**, a **Placeholder** for Pick one, its sub-options and **Add a sub-option**.
  - Deleting an option with sub-options asks first: "Delete Family and its 3 sub-options?"
  - **Add an option** ends the list. The limits show in place, not as an error.
  - The old **Levels** editor is gone.
- **Labels, not values.** A chained select's read-only view showed the stored values; it now shows labels. The detail page shows a nested view ("Family › Children", with the last picks as badges and several sub-picks indented). Tables and other text read "Family › Children › Schooling; Family › Children › Health › Dental". A pick that is no longer an option shows its stored text.
- **Conditions on a chained select.** Visible When, Required When, a value rule's condition (on the Fields and Rules tabs) and a reference field's **Only entries where…** offer **Is one of**, **Is not one of**, **Is blank** and **Is not blank**, with a list of every option, indented by level, to choose from. The condition holds while a chosen option is picked, at any depth, and its hint names the option by its labels ("Area is one of Family › Children").
- **Merge fields.** `{area|label}` gives the labels as above, and a bare `{area}` the picked option values ("family, children"). In a formula, `last({area})` gives the last pick.
- **Older chained selects keep working.** A config in the old `{ levels, options }` shape is read everywhere, as deep as its levels go, with blank rows left out. It is checked only for its shape, limits and content, so a type carrying one keeps saving and importing, and the editor rewrites it as a tree on its first edit. An old one-item-per-level value (`["family", "children"]`) is read as that chain, and stored as paths the next time a write changes it.
- **Field-group sub-fields.** Editing a sub-field's comma-separated options keeps each option it still names, with its content.

**Behaviour that changes**
1. **"Is one of" over a list.** **Is one of** (`in`) and **Is not one of** (`notIn`) now test each item of a value that holds two or more: a multiselect or a multiple reference with several picks matches **Is one of** when any pick is listed. Before, such a value was compared as one joined text and never matched. Existing **Visible When**, **Required When**, value-rule and reference-filter conditions on those fields can start to apply on the next save or render, so a field may now show, be required or be set where it wasn't before. Check them after updating. A value with one pick, or none, compares as before.
2. The server's `in` list filter also matches a stored list when one of its items is listed.
3. The server now checks a chained select's value on a write, as it checks a select's: only a value the write changes, and each pick must be an option, under a picked option, with one pick at the top and one under each **Pick one** option. A field with no options takes any list of text.
4. Saving a type (or importing one) refuses an option whose description, source or link breaks the rules, and a chained tree that does.

**For developers**
- **One set of rules for both halves.** The new `src/templates/shared/chained-options.ts` is rendered into the frontend's `utils/chained-options.ts` and the backend's `structure.chained-options.ts`, as the condition evaluator is. It holds `normaliseChainedConfig`, `chainedConfigProblem`, `optionFieldsProblem`, `optionContentProblem`, `optionLinkProblem`, `safeOptionHref`, the value helpers (`readChainedValue`, `normaliseChainedValue`, `tidyChainedValue`, `chainedValueProblem`, `chainedPickOne`, `chainedToggle`, `withChainedConditionPaths`) and the display helpers (`chainedDisplayText`, `chainedPickTree`, `chainedPathLabels`, `chainedLabelFormatter`), with `CHAINED_PATH_SEPARATOR` (`/`), `CHAINED_MAX_DEPTH` (6), `CHAINED_MAX_OPTIONS` (1000) and `OPTION_CONTENT_LIMITS`.
- **The config shape.** `chainedSelectConfig` is `{ placeholder?, options: ChainedOption[] }`, each option `{ value, label, description?, source?, link?, pick?: 'one' | 'several', heading?, placeholder?, children? }`. Types: `FieldOption`, `ChainedSelectOption`, `ChainedSelectConfig` and `LegacyChainedSelectConfig` on the frontend's `NodeTypeField`; the backend's `options` gains the content keys and `chainedSelectConfig` is `unknown`. See [the JSON reference](/modules/structure/json-reference/#field-options-and-chained-selects-v1530).
- **The value shape.** A list of the full paths of every picked option, ancestors included, each option value one segment with `%` as `%25` and `/` as `%2F`. It stays a list of text, so list filters, conditions, merge fields and other modules' reads take a pick at any depth as one item. The server converts an older by-level value to paths on create, update and a duplicate's overrides (`withChainedPaths`), only when nothing would be dropped.
- **Checks.** `assertOptionFieldConfigs` (`optionFieldsProblem`) runs on every path that saves field configs, the imports and field templates included, beside the validation-pattern check. `validateNodeData` gains a `chained-select` case. Refusals start `Field "x": `, as listed on [Field Types](/modules/structure/fields/#chained-select).
- **Conditions.** `fieldConditionRecord` and `valueRuleRecord` read a chained value as paths when given the fields (`withChainedConditionPaths`), an older value included. `in` and `notIn` test each item of a list of two or more (`conditionValueIn`), on both halves. `buildDataFilterQuery`'s `in` adds a `?|` match for stored JSON arrays. `FieldConditionEditor` takes `chainedConfigOf`, and its `fieldOptions` and `referenceFilterFields` carry `chainedSelectConfig`, so a chained row offers its four tests and `ChainedConditionPicker`.
- **Merge fields.** `fieldLabelOptions` takes a fifth argument, a chained formatter (`chainedLabelFormatter`), and a label lookup may answer with a `FormulaLabelFormatter` (`formatLabel`, and `formatRaw` for a bare token's list). Both halves pass it, so `{x|label}` and `{x}` read the same on the server and in the browser.
- **Components.** `components/fields/FieldOptions.tsx`: `OptionPickerText`, `OptionInfoButton`, `OptionValueBadge`, `OptionToggleList`, `ChainedSelectInput` and `ChainedSelectValue`. `components/admin/OptionEditors.tsx`: `SelectOptionsEditor`, `ChainedSelectEditor` and `ChainedConditionPicker`.
- **Defaults.** A chained select still takes no default value.

**Tests.** 55 new tests in `tests/chained-options.test.ts`: the shared core in both halves, normalising both shapes, the tree's limits and refusals, option links and content, the value helpers and the older value shape, the display text and nested view, conditions at any depth on both halves, "Is one of" over a list and the `in` filter, bare and `|label` tokens on both halves, the config round trip and the in-process import, and text guards for the editor and the forms.

Frontend and backend. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

---

## v1.52.0

**Rules v2: copy from other records, "Otherwise", a Rules tab and more condition tests** *(generated-output)*

Kim asked "is it possible to pull data from other nodes", for example a child's address copied from the parent they live with, and for a builder for if / otherwise rules: if a field matches, do this, otherwise do that. A field's **Set automatically** rules can now copy a value from another field, on this entry, on an entry it links to, or on its parent, or put a value together with Build it. A rule list can end in **Otherwise**, and the admin chooses what happens when the copied value changes at its source. A new **Rules** tab shows every rule on the type in one place, and the condition builder has rows and offers every test, six of them new. Everything is set up by the admin. A type that doesn't use the new settings works exactly as before.

- **Copy from another field.** A rule's **Set to** is now **A value** (as before), **Copy from another field**, or **Put it together (Build it)**. A copy takes the other field's value as it is stored, so a whole address or name copies too. The field picker groups the places it can copy from under **This entry**, **Through** each link to one entry (for example **Through Parent A**), and **Parent**, and offers only fields whose kind fits (text into a long text, say). Through a link or the parent it can also copy the other entry's name, description, slug or reference number, as text.
- **Address and name fields** can now be set automatically, by a copy of another address or name field only.
- **Put it together.** A text, number or date field can be set to a value made with Build it, or a formula under **Advanced**. "The result is made the field's kind of value: text, a number or a date." A result that can't be (text that isn't a number, for a number field) leaves the field as it is.
- **Otherwise.** Under the rules, the **Otherwise** switch gives the value used when no rule matches, so the rules read "When …", "Or else, when …", "Otherwise". With **Otherwise** on, the field is always set by its rules. With it off: "When no rule matches, the field is left for people to fill in." The rules can be moved up and down, and the first one that matches wins.
- **When the source changes.** For a copy or a put-together value, the admin chooses:
  - **Keep it up to date** (the default): "A copied or put-together value follows its source: when the other entry changes, this field is updated too." While a rule applies the field is read-only, and its hint names the source, such as "From Parent A's Home address while Lives with Parent A is on."
  - **Ask first**: "When someone saves the other entry, they are asked whether to update the entries that copy it." The question is "3 records copy this. Update them too?", listing each entry with its type and the fields that would change, each ticked. **Yes, update them** updates the ticked ones; **No, leave them** leaves them all.
  - **Set it once**: "The value is set when a rule first applies, then left alone: it can be changed by hand after that." The field is never read-only.
- **More condition tests.** The condition builder offers 15 tests, with plainer names: **Is exactly**, **Is not**, **Contains**, **Does not contain**, **Begins with**, **Does not begin with**, **Ends with**, **Contains the word**, **Does not contain the word**, **Is one of**, **Is not one of**, **Greater than**, **Less than**, **Is blank** and **Is not blank**. The six new text tests ignore capitals, and **Contains the word** matches whole words only ("cat" is in "the Cat sat", not in "category"). The older tests compare as they always have, so **Contains** still minds capitals.
- **A condition builder with rows.** **Visible When**, **Required When**, a rule's condition and a reference field's **Only entries where…** can now hold several tests: **Add another test** adds one, a bin removes one, and with two or more you choose **Match all of these** or **Match any of these**. Before, the builder held one test with four choices, and anything more had to come from JSON.
- **The Rules tab.** The type form has a new **Rules** tab, after **Fields**, with every rule on the type in one place, in the type's field order. It edits the same settings as the field editor, so a change in one shows in the other. Its three sections:
  - **Set automatically**: each field with rules, as its chain, and **Add a rule** ("Choose the field it sets").
  - **Show and require**: each field with a **Show when** or **Required when** condition, and **Add a condition to** a field.
  - **Worked-out values**: the computed **Name**, **Description** and **Web address (slug)**, and every field with a merge template or a formula, each with **Build it** and **Advanced**.
  - In the field editor, a field with rules says "Also on the Rules tab."
- **The same safety rules.** A copy or a put-together value never reads a secret, through a link or the parent included. A rule can't read the field it sets, or a field that is set automatically itself, so the rules on an entry never go round in a loop. The save checks each copy: its source must exist, fit the field, not be a secret, and not be read through a link to several entries.

**Behaviour that changes**
1. A field with **Otherwise** always has a value its rules set. Before, a field whose rules stopped matching kept its value; a field without **Otherwise** still does.
2. The tests have new names: **Equals** is **Is exactly**, **Not equals** is **Is not**, **Has value** is **Is not blank**. Stored conditions keep their meaning. A condition the old builder could show (one test, of an older kind) is saved in the same shape as before; several tests, **Match any of these**, or a new test are saved as `rules`.
3. A copy of an empty field clears the field it sets, except that it never empties a required field: on an update the field keeps its value, and a create that the copy leaves empty is refused with the field's own "required" message. A required field that a copy or a put-together value will fill isn't asked for before the create.

**Known gaps**
- The "Ask first" question is asked after the edit form's save, the detail page's **Save changes**, save and leave, the save when the page goes away, and the Info tab's auto-save when an edit ends (once per edit). It isn't asked after a save on the **Settings** tab, a bulk edit or an import.
- After a system write (the reference write-back, one per section's turn-off, the user-account link), a duplicate or a merge, an **Ask first** or **Set it once** value that reads another record waits for the entry's own next save. **Keep it up to date** values are worked out again after those writes.
- **Set it once** on rules that set fixed values only can be set in JSON alone: the **When the source changes** choice shows once a rule copies or puts a value together.
- The loop guard works within one entry. Entries whose rules copy from each other are kept in step only as far as the recompute's chain depth of 3.

**For developers**
- **Value kinds.** A rule's `value`, and the new `setOtherwise`, is a fixed value (any plain value, as before), `{ copy: 'field' | 'link->field' | 'parent->field' }`, or `{ build: 'template' }` (a merge template) or `{ build: 'expression', formula: true }` (a formula). A build is for `text`, `textarea`, `hidden`, `email`, `url`, `phone`, `number` and `date` fields only (`BUILD_RULE_TARGET_TYPES`); `address` and `name-field` take copies only (`COPY_ONLY_RULE_TARGET_TYPES`). `parent->field` reads the entry's parent, unless the type has a field called `parent`. Which source types fit which targets is `copySourceFits`; `coerceBuildValue` makes a build's result the field's type.
- **New field keys.** `setOtherwise?: unknown` and `setSync?: 'automatic' | 'prompted' | 'initial'` (absent means `'automatic'`). A field has value rules when `setWhen` holds a rule or `setOtherwise` is set (`hasValueRules`). See [Set automatically](/modules/structure/fields/#set-automatically-setwhen) and [the JSON reference](/modules/structure/json-reference/#value-rules-v2-v1520).
- **Who works what out.** The service applies fixed values and copies of the entry's own fields, as before (`valueRulePatch`). A copy through a link or the parent, and a build, read other records, so the formula engine works them out on the entry's own writes (create, bulk create, a reference field's new entries, update; `computeOwn`'s new `rules` option), with the entry's confined reads, as a `{ref->x}` token reads: its section, the owner's readable types, secrets removed. The forms work them out the same way in the browser (`useRuleValueResolver`, `hooks/useRuleValues.ts`); a value they can't tell yet is left to the server.
- **Keep it up to date.** A rule's reads join its type's formula dependency summary, so a change to a source recomputes its dependants with the formula recompute's bounds (a chain depth of 3, at most 200 dependants) and fires `structure.node.updated` with `source: 'formula'`.
- **Ask first.** Two new routes: `GET /nodes/:id/rule-updates` returns `{ items: RuleUpdateItem[] }`, and `POST /nodes/:id/rule-updates` with `{ ids }` (1 to 500) returns `{ updated: string[] }`. The service methods are `previewRuleUpdates(nodeId, userId, { isAdmin? })` and `applyRuleUpdates(nodeId, userId, ids, { isAdmin? })`. Both need edit permission on the saved record's type, and offer only the caller's own live, editable, in-scope rows (at most 200); the list holds only records that read through the saved one. See [Copies, Builds and Ask First](/modules/structure/integration/#copies-builds-and-ask-first-v1520).
- **Set it once.** A rule applies when its branch first holds: the branch that holds now isn't the one that held over the stored entry, or the entry is new. A value the write itself changes is a hand edit and stays. On a create, a carried value equal to the field's default isn't a hand edit.
- **Operators.** `FIELD_CONDITION_OPERATORS` adds `notContains`, `beginsWith`, `notBeginsWith`, `endsWith`, `containsWord` and `notContainsWord` (`TEXT_CONDITION_OPERATORS`), case-insensitive, in the rules form only (`{ field, beginsWith }` is `unknown key "beginsWith"`). They hold everywhere a condition does: `visibleWhen`, `requiredWhen`, `setWhen`, a reference's `filter`, `matchesCondition`, `queryNodes` and `formulas.matches`. They are never pushed to SQL: `conditionDataFilters` leaves them out and they are tested on the fetched entries. An unknown operator is still refused.
- **Guards.** The secret guard reads a rule's copies and builds as computed values (`Computed field "Home address (set automatically)" may not reference secret field …`), and checks `{parent->x}` in a rule against every type's secrets. Node type and field template saves check copy sources (`assertRuleValueSources`), skipping a copy the stored row already had. The configuration import checks the shape only, because a type a copy reads may come later in the file.
- **Browser.** `structureService.previewRuleUpdates(id)` and `applyRuleUpdates(id, ids)`; `promptRuleUpdates(nodeId)` after a save, shown by `RuleUpdatesHost` (`components/common/RuleUpdatesPrompt.tsx`), registered once in `shellInitRegistry` as `structure-rule-updates`. The Rules tab is `components/admin/RulesTab.tsx`; it and the field editor share `ValueRulesEditor` and `FieldConditionEditor`. New utilities from `@/features/structure/utils`: `CONDITION_OPERATOR_OPTIONS`, `conditionRowsOf`, `conditionFromRows`, `containsWholeWord`, `hasValueRules`, `valueRuleSync`, `valueRuleBranch`, `valueRuleReads`, `ruleValueKind`, `parseCopyPath`, `copyPathReadable`, `copySourceFits`, `coerceBuildValue`, `describeCopySource`, `isOtherwiseRule`, `valueRuleHandEdits`, and the lists `COPY_ONLY_RULE_TARGET_TYPES`, `BUILD_RULE_TARGET_TYPES`, `VALUE_RULE_SYNC_MODES`, `TEXT_CONDITION_OPERATORS` and `FIELD_CONDITION_OPERATORS`. `RuleUpdateItem` and `RuleUpdatePreview` are exported with Structure's other types, in both halves.

**Tests.** 38 new tests and a 16-case table in `tests/value-rules-v2.test.ts`: each value kind on both halves (addresses and names, through a link and the parent), Otherwise, the recompute when a source changes, Ask first's preview and apply with rights checked again, Set it once, each new test on both halves with the older ones unchanged, the data-filter exclusion, the secret and loop guards, the config round trip, and text guards for the Rules tab, the field editor and the one dialog.

Frontend and backend. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

---

## v1.51.0

**Sections and field order, nested, and fields cards that show a section** *(generated-output)*

Kim asked for the field order to be nested: sections that can be put in order, with the fields inside each one in order too, which "makes it easy when doing the bento display page". The **Fields** tab's **Field order** is now **Sections and field order**. A type can have its own sections, in order, each with its fields in order, and a page layout's fields card can show one section. Everything is set up by the admin. A type with no sections works exactly as before.

- **Sections and field order.** On the **Fields** tab, the list that was **Field order** now groups the fields into sections. Every field the type's entries have is in it: its own, its shared templates', its parent types' and other modules'.
  - **Each section** has its name, which you change in place (placeholder "Section name, e.g. About them"), buttons to move it to the top, up, down or to the bottom (its fields go with it), and **Delete section**, which moves its fields to the end of **Other fields**. **Add a section** adds "New section" at the end, up to 50 sections.
  - **Each field** has buttons to move it within its section, and a **Section** menu that moves it to the end of another section, or to **Other fields**. A field from a shared template, a parent type or another module keeps its "From …" or "Inherited from …" badge.
  - **Other fields** come last: "Fields in no section. They come after the sections, with no heading."
  - **Starting from the fields' own sections.** A type with no sections yet, whose fields have a **Section** setting, starts with one section for each of those names, in the current order: "These sections come from each field's own Section setting. Nothing is saved until you change something here." Your first change sets them up, and from then on they replace the old field order.
  - **Use the standard order** now asks first ("Use the standard order?"). It takes away the sections and the order, and the fields go back to their standard order, grouped by each field's own **Section** setting.
  - **Fields that went away.** If the sections list a field the type no longer has, a note says "Some fields listed here aren't on this type right now", names them, and offers **Remove them**. The pages leave them out meanwhile, and they never stop a save.
- **Where the sections show.** The create and edit forms and the **Info** tab show each section as a heading, in your order, with its fields in your order, then the fields in no section, with no heading. A heading shows only when its section has a field that will actually show. The table's default columns follow the same order, as one list.
- **A field's own Section setting.** While the type has sections, the field editor's **Section** setting is a menu of the type's sections plus **No section**, and choosing one moves the field ("Moves the field in Sections and field order, below."). Without sections it is the free-text name it always was. The **Custom Fields** list groups the fields under the type's sections too.
- **A fields card can show a section.** A fields card on the **Pages** tab has a new **Show** choice: **A section** (pick one) or **Fields I choose** (the list it always had). A card that shows a section shows that section's fields in the section's order, so reordering them on the Fields tab changes the card too, and its title is the section's name unless you give it one. If the section is taken away later, the card shows nothing, and the **Page layout** lists it with **Remove them**. It never stops a save.
- **Make a card for each section.** Under each tab's cards, this button adds a two-thirds fields card for each section the tab doesn't show yet, as far as the tab has room. When every section has a card it says "Every section already has a card on this tab."
- **Use these sections.** When the fields have **Section** settings but the type has no sections set up yet, the **Pages** tab says that a card can't show one yet, and offers **Use these sections**, which sets them up as the Fields tab would. "Nothing changes until you save the type."

**Behaviour that changes**
1. A type with sections shows them as headings on the create and edit forms, which had no headings before, and on the Info tab in the order you set.
2. With sections, a field's own **Section** name no longer decides where it shows. A field the sections don't list joins the section with the same name (ignoring capitals and spaces at either end), at its end; the rest go last, with no heading.
3. The first change in **Sections and field order** replaces the type's old field order: the save writes the sections and drops `navigation.fieldOrder`.
4. A type with no sections is unchanged: its field order and each field's **Section** name work as in v1.50.1.

**For developers**
- **New navigation key:** `navigation.fieldLayout` `{ sections: [{ id, label, fields }], otherFields? }`, the sections in order, each with its effective field ids in order, and `otherFields`, the order of the fields in no section. **Precedence:** a `fieldLayout` decides the grouping and the order on the forms, the Info tab and the table's default columns, over `fieldOrder` and each field's `section` label. Without one, `fieldOrder` decides the order and the labels group the Info tab, as in v1.49; without either, the labels group it in the fields' own order. A field the sections don't list joins the section its label names; a field `otherFields` lists never joins a section by its label. It round-trips through config export and import. See [Sections and field order](/modules/structure/json-reference/#sections-and-field-order-v1510).
- **What a save checks.** `fieldLayoutProblem`, in the shared rules (`utils/page-layout.ts` and the backend's `structure.page-layout.ts`), checks the shape only, on every save and import: unique section ids, a name of up to 80 characters for each section, each field in at most one place (a section or `otherFields`), at most 50 sections and 1,000 placed fields. A field id the type no longer has never blocks. A refusal starts `Sections and field order:`, and the admin form opens the **Fields** tab.
- **Section cards.** `PageLayoutFieldsCard` is now `{ kind: 'fields', id, title?, width?, fields?, section? }`: a `section` (a section id) or `fields`, not both. A card with a section and a list of fields is refused (`… shows a section and fields it chooses. Choose one.`). A missing section never blocks a save, even one the save brings in; it is listed in `pageLayoutStaleReferences` (`neverBlocks`), and `withoutStaleReferences` drops the card.
- **Section ids are never reused.** A section added in the editor gets `section-` plus a time and a random part, never an id that a card still names. Sections made from the fields' labels are `section-1`, `section-2` and so on, skipping the ids cards name. So a card left showing a deleted section never starts showing another one.
- **Duplicate field ids** (another module can register a field with an id the type already has) are placed once, where they first come.
- **The first sections keep a place for fields that are away.** When the sections are made from the labels, the old `fieldOrder`'s ids that aren't among the fields now (a module field switched off, say) go at the end of `otherFields`, in their order.
- **Utilities**, from `@/features/structure/utils`: `fieldLayoutOf`, `fieldLayoutProblem`, `groupFieldsByLayout`, `applyFieldLayout`, `applyTypeFieldOrder`, `sectionLabelKey` and `pageLayoutSectionIds`, with the type `FieldGroup`. `FieldLayout` and `FieldLayoutSection` are exported with Structure's other types, in both halves. The forms (`NodeForm`), the Info tab and `useNodeTypeColumns` now use `applyTypeFieldOrder(fields, navigation)`.
- **`NodeFieldEditor`** gains `fieldLayout` (the type's sections, or `null`) and `rendersField(field)` (whether the field shows anything now); `ordered` is true with sections too.
- **The admin editor** `FieldOrderEditor` is now `FieldSectionsEditor` (`components/admin/PageLayoutEditor`), with `value` (the sections), `fieldOrder`, `onChange` and `takenSectionIds`.

**Tests.** 40 new tests in `tests/field-sections.test.ts`: the precedence (sections over the field order over the labels), unlisted fields joining by label or going last, one section per field, stale ids, the first save dropping `fieldOrder`, the config round trip and the in-process import, section cards on the page and in the save, section ids never reused, duplicate ids, and text guards for the editor (moving between sections, reordering, deleting a section) and the form headings. Without sections, nothing changes.

Frontend and backend. No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

## v1.50.1

**A tidier Structure Types list** *(generated-output)*

- **Sort** the admin's type list by **Hierarchy** (the default: top-level types first, each followed by the types it can contain, indented, with "Also inside: …" for a type that sits in several places), **Name A–Z**, **Name Z–A**, **Recently changed** or **Order**.
- **Show** **All types**, **Top-level types**, or **Inside** a chosen type, plus a **Show disabled** switch. The search box works with both.
- **Pages** of 20, 50 or 100 types. Your choices are remembered in this browser, and the open type stays open across pages.
- **No more dragging** in the list. A type's **Order** is a number on its Basic tab (lower first), which orders lists of types such as the **+ New** menu; its sidebar place is still set under Sidebar Navigation.
- **The Can contain hint was wrong.** It said an empty list prevents nesting; an empty list allows any type inside. To allow none, set **Maximum children per node** to 0. The hint now says so.
- For developers: `listNodeTypes` and `getNodeType` now include `updatedAt` (an ISO string). `POST /types/reorder` and `reorderNodeTypes` still exist but Structure's own UI no longer uses them. New pure helpers in `utils/type-list.ts`.

Frontend and a read-only backend addition, no schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

## v1.50.0

**Profile types: entries linked to user accounts, and a side column of cards** *(generated-output)*

Kim asked: "how do i convert a node type to a profile type so that it can be connected to the user in admin UI?" A type can now be a **Profile** (people and organisations), and its entries can be linked to user accounts, so a person can say "this is me" about one of them. Users link themselves, and admins can link or unlink anyone. Every choice is made by the admin on the type, not in code. A page layout also gets a side column of cards, with a new **Header** card that shows the entry itself. A type that doesn't use the new settings works exactly as before.

<Aside type="caution" title="SCHEMA CHANGE: run npx structure update without --skip-schema">
This release adds a column (`linkedUserId`) and an index to `structure_node`. Run `npx structure update` **without** `--skip-schema`. The backend fails until the schema step has run.
</Aside>

- **Profile settings.** The **Basic** tab has a new **Profile** section. **Type kind** is **Record** (the default) or **Profile (people and organisations)**. A Profile type offers **Entries can be linked to user accounts**, and with it on:
  - **Each user has one linked entry**: **In the whole app**, or **In each** *Matter* (offered when the app has an isolated primary-scope type). Linking a user to another entry in the same place moves the link: their other entry is unlinked.
  - **Label for the switch**: the words on the switch and on the badge. "This is me" unless you write others.
  - **Also tick this field when linked**: an optional yes/no field kept in step with the link, ticked when an entry is linked and unticked when it is unlinked. Changing the field by itself doesn't change the link.

  A subtype has its parent's profile settings unless it sets its own. See [Profile types](/modules/structure/usage/#profile-types-v1500).
- **The "This is me" switch.** For a type whose entries can be linked, the create and edit forms, a reference field's **Create new** popup and a page layout's "Add a …" show the switch. On a new entry, it links you as the entry is made. On the edit form, it links or unlinks you when you save. Under it, a hint says what it means now: "This is you.", "Turning this off unlinks you.", that you are already linked to another entry and turning it on moves the link here, or that the entry is linked to another user and only an admin can change it.
- **The badge.** An entry linked to you shows the switch's label as a badge on its card, its list row, its table row, the detail page's header and a header card.
- **Admins link anyone.** On an entry's page, while editing, an admin also has **Link a user…**, which searches the app's users by name or email, and **Unlink the user**.
- **The link as a condition.** A field can be shown, required or set automatically depending on whether the entry is linked. The condition builders offer the link by the switch's label, with **Is on** and **Is off**; in JSON it is the field `linked`. On a form it follows the switch as it stands, before the save. So a rule that read a yes/no field such as `is_me` can read the link instead, and the field can go. See [The link as a condition](/modules/structure/fields/#the-link-as-a-condition-v1500).
- **When a save would give a user two links.** A type save that turns linking on, or changes **Each user has one linked entry** from each Matter to the whole app, first counts the users who would have more than one linked entry in one place. If there are any, it asks ("Some users have more than one linked …"), with **Keep each user's most recently updated one and unlink the others** ticked.
- **A side column on the page.** The **Pages** tab's **Page layout** has a new **Side column**: up to 12 cards shown beside every tab of the page, built-in tabs included, to the left on a computer and above the tabs on a phone. Every kind of card works there, at the column's full width.
- **The header card.** A new kind of card, **Header**, shows the entry itself: its photo (from a field holding a picture's web address), else the type's icon, else its initials; its title (the name unless you build another); up to three lines; and the "This is me" badge. It is made for the side column and works on a tab too. See [Side column and header card](/modules/structure/usage/#side-column-and-header-card-v1500).

**Behaviour that changes**
1. Deleting an entry, merging it into another, or erasing the linked user's account unlinks the entry. Restoring a deleted entry doesn't bring its link back.
2. A duplicate is never linked.
3. Turning linking off, or making the type a Record, keeps the links but reads every entry as not linked until linking is back on.
4. For a type whose entries can be linked, a `linked` key in a write's data is dropped unless the type defines a field called `linked`.

**For developers**
- **Schema.** `structure_node.linkedUserId String?`, a plain id with no `@relation`, and `@@index([linkedUserId, nodeType])`. There is no module SQL migration: the column comes in with the schema step. See [structure_node](/modules/structure/configuration/#structure_node).
- **Settings.** `features.profile` `{ kind?: 'record' | 'profile', linkUsers?, linkScope?: 'app' | 'section', syncField?, switchLabel? }`, inherited by subtypes like every feature key. It round-trips through config export and import. A save refuses an unknown key, `linkUsers: true` without `kind: 'profile'`, a label over 80 characters, and, on an admin save that names a new one, a `syncField` that isn't a yes/no field of the type. An import checks the shape only. See [`features.profile`](/modules/structure/json-reference/#featuresprofile-v1500).
- **The link API.** On `structureService`: `linkUser(nodeId, actorId, { targetUserId?, caller? })`, `unlinkUser(nodeId, actorId, { caller? })`, `getLinkedEntry(userId, nodeType, { scopeRootId?, nodeId?, parentId?, caller? })`, and the admin-only `getLinkConflicts` and `resolveLinkConflicts`. Routes: `POST` and `DELETE /nodes/:id/link-user` (body `{ targetUserId? }`), `GET /types/:slug/linked-entry?scopeRootId=|nodeId=|parentId=`, and, for admins, `GET /types/:slug/link-conflicts?scope=` and `POST /types/:slug/link-conflicts/resolve`. One link per user per scope (the app, or each primary-scope section), moved under transaction-scoped advisory locks. Create and update data never set `linkedUserId`.
- **`linkToMe`.** `createNode`'s input and a reference field's `{ "$new": { …, "linkToMe": true } }` link the new entry to its creator in the same transaction, so its own value rules read `linked` as true. A caller passing `publicApi: true` (the public REST API), a bulk create and an import never link.
- **Reads.** Every node row carries `linked` and `linkedToMe`. `linkedUserId` reaches a reader only when it is their own id, or on an admin's `getNode` and `listNodes`. Another user's id never reaches a non-admin.
- **Hooks.** `structure.node.userLinked` `{ userId, actorId, node, linkedUserId }` and `structure.node.userUnlinked` `{ userId, actorId, node, linkedUserId, reason }`, with `reason` one of `'unlinked'`, `'moved'`, `'deleted'`, `'merged'` and `'erased'`. A link change that writes data (the sync field, or what the value rules set) also fires `structure.node.updated`.
- **The condition.** `CONDITION_LINKED_KEY` (`'linked'`) and `withLinkedConditionValue` in the shared condition code. `fieldConditionRecord` takes the type's fields, so a defined field called `linked` wins. Read by `visibleWhen` and `requiredWhen` on the forms, by `setWhen` on the forms and the server, and by `nodeReferenceConfig.filter`.
- **Page layouts.** `navigation.pageLayout.side` `{ cards }` (at most 12) and the `header` card `{ photoField?, titleTemplate?, lines? (up to 3), showLinkBadge? }`, in the shared layout rules for both halves.
- **New files:** `utils/profile-link.ts`, `hooks/useProfileLink.ts` and `components/node/ProfileLink.tsx` (`LinkSwitch`, `ThisIsMeBadge`, `NodeLinkControls`, `AdminUserLink`). `NodeLayoutSide` is in `components/tabs/NodeLayoutTab`. Everything is listed under [Profile Types](/modules/structure/integration/#profile-types-v1500) on the Integration page.

**Not in this release.** The residuals are on the roadmap under [Known residuals of v1.50.0](/modules/structure/roadmap/#known-residuals-of-v1500). The main ones: some less-used reads can still show a link while linking is off; a merge doesn't move the retired entry's link to the kept one; and nothing has been checked by eye in a host app yet. A `{me->field}` token and chart cards may come later.

**Tests.** 47 new tests in `tests/profile-link.test.ts`: the link rights (a self-link, an admin linking others, a non-admin refused), one link per user in the app and per section with the link moving, the sync field with one per section, delete, merge and account erasure clearing links, `getLinkedEntry` with and without a scope, `linkedUserId` never writable through create or update data, no other user's id in a non-admin's answer, the `linked` condition and its defined-field rule, the config's round trip and refusals, and the header card's rules.

Frontend and backend, with a schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in, schema step included; it adds three new files. Restart the backend if it doesn't reload by itself.

## v1.49.1

**Select options get a value you can see** *(generated-output)*

- **Options added by hand had no proper value.** A new option took its label as its value (spaces and capitals included), and its value box never showed: the code button meant to reveal it cleared the value instead, which kept the box hidden. Options from imported JSON showed theirs.
- **Now every option shows its value.** A new option’s value is worked out from its label as you type, lower case with dashes (“Separated under one roof” → “separated-under-one-roof”), the same style as imported JSON, until you change the value yourself. A typed value is tidied the same way when you leave the box.
- **Existing options keep their values**, so entries that already chose them still match. Changing a value doesn’t change entries that already chose it.
- **Two options with the same value** are flagged in the editor.

Frontend only, no schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

---

## v1.49.0

**Page layouts with cards, a field order for every field, and the entry's parent in templates** *(generated-output)*

Kim asked for three things: to put shared fields in any order, since a field from a field template couldn't be moved among the type's own; to group fields into cards, on tabs, with "a bit of a bento vibe"; and, for a Matter, a card for each child and a paged table of court proceedings whose columns can come from the proceeding or from the Matter it sits under. All of it is set up by the admin; nothing about a Matter is in the code. Without the new settings, every page and form is exactly as before.

- **Field order.** The **Fields** tab has a new **Field order** list of every field the type's entries have: its own, its shared templates', its parent types' and other modules'. Each row has buttons to move it to the top, up, down or to the bottom, and a badge says where a field that isn't the type's own comes from. **Use the standard order** goes back to the usual order. The create and edit forms, the **Info** tab and the table's default columns follow it. Lists you order by hand, such as **Table columns** and the card's fields, keep their own order. See [Sections and field order](/modules/structure/usage/#sections-and-field-order-v1510), its name since v1.51.0.
- **Page layouts.** The **Pages** tab has a new **Page layout**. Turn on **Use a custom page layout**, and the entry's page shows your tabs of cards in place of the **Info** tab (and of **Overview**, with **Replace the Overview tab**); the other tabs stay. The cards sit in a grid three wide on a computer and one wide on a phone, each **One third**, **Two thirds** or **Full**. There are four kinds of card:
  - **Fields**: the fields you pick, in your order, shown and edited as on the Info tab, with **Edit**, **Save changes** and **Cancel** as before.
  - **Featured**: one card for each of the entry's children of a type, or each entry in a link field, with its icon or initials, a title and up to two lines. Clicking one opens it. With **Allow adding**, they end with an "Add a …" button: for children it opens the usual form and makes the entry under this one; for a link field it opens the field's own **Create new**.
  - **Table**: the entry's children of a type, the entries in a link field, the entries of another type that link to it, or the entries connected to it by a type of connection, a page at a time (5 rows unless you choose another number). Each column is built with **Build it** and can show the row's own fields or its parent's.
  - **Widget**: a widget another module provides.

  A small preview shows each tab's grid, and a line lists the fields that are on no card, so not on the page. If a field, type or widget a card uses is taken away later, the page leaves it out, and the **Page layout** lists it with **Remove them**. See [Page layout](/modules/structure/usage/#page-layout-v1490) and, for what people see, [Page layouts](/modules/structure/usage/#page-layouts-v1490).
- **The entry's parent in templates.** A merge template or formula can read the entry's parent: `{parent->court}` gives the court of the Matter a court proceeding sits under, and `{parent->full_name|surnameInitial}` works too. **Build it** offers it as a **Parent** group. It reads the parent the way a reference is read: within the entry's section, never a secret. A formula field, or a computed name, description or slug, that reads the parent is worked out again on the server when the parent changes. A data field's merge template isn't: it follows when the entry itself is next edited, or when you run **Update entries**, as a reference's `{ref->x}` always has. See [The entry's parent](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#the-entrys-parent-v1490).
- **Computed fields stay current while you edit.** Every computed field is kept up to date in an edit, even when it is on no card, on another tab or not shown on the page.
- **The Table columns picker's note is right now.** With no columns chosen, it says the table shows the name, then the card's fields (or the first five fields), with Status, Created and Updated hidden until turned on. Before, it said the table showed only the name and the built-in fields.

**Behaviour that changes**
1. A type with a **Field order** shows its fields in that order on the forms, the Info tab and the table's default columns. A type without one is unchanged.
2. A type with a page layout turned on has no **Info** tab: its fields show only on the layout's fields cards. A page that opened on Info opens on the layout's first tab.
3. A template token `{parent}` or `{parent->x}` used to read blank on a type with no field called `parent`; it now reads the entry's parent. A type with a field called `parent` still reads that field.
4. A formula field or computed built-in that reads `{parent->x}` is recomputed when an ancestor changes.

**For developers**
- **New navigation keys:** `navigation.fieldOrder` (field ids, at most 1,000) and `navigation.pageLayout` (`{ enabled?, replaceOverview?, tabs: [{ id, label, cards }] }`, with `fields`, `featured`, `table` and `widget` cards). Both are stored in the type's `navigation` JSON, round-trip through config export and import, and are checked on save by one shared set of rules generated into both halves (`utils/page-layout.ts` and the backend's `structure.page-layout.ts`). The shape always blocks; what a layout names (fields, link fields, child types, other types' link fields, connection types, and in the browser widgets and tab ids) blocks only when the save brings it in, never while the layout is off; imports check the shape only. See [Field order and page layout](/modules/structure/json-reference/#field-order-and-page-layout-v1490).
- **`applyFieldOrder(fields, fieldOrder)`** orders a field list by the type's order; the forms (`NodeForm`), the Info tab and `useNodeTypeColumns` use it.
- **`NodeTabProps`** gains `tabId?` and `canEdit?`, passed to every tab. **`useNodeTabs(node, nodeType, layoutTab?)`** takes an optional third parameter, the component a layout's tabs render with (the detail page passes the new `NodeLayoutTab`); without it the tabs are the registry's. `layoutTabOrder(tabOrder, layout)` puts a layout's unlisted tabs after `info`.
- **`useNodeFieldEditor(node, nodeType, isEditing)`** and **`NodeFieldsGrid`** (from `components/tabs`) are the Info tab's field showing and editing, shared with the layout's fields cards. **`useComputedTemplateWrite`** and **`ComputedFieldSync`** (from `components/fields/NodeFieldRenderer`) keep a merge-template field current in an edit whether or not it is rendered; `referenceDraftFields` there is a link field's **Create new**, which "Add a …" uses too.
- **`NodeDataTable`** gains `compact` (no **Columns** or **Density** menu, compact rows, nothing remembered), and a column `source` can be `{ formula }` (`nodeFormulaColumn(id, label, formula)`), worked out per row with the row as its host.
- **The parent token** is in the shared resolver (`utils/formula.ts`): `FormulaTokenHost` and `FormulaContext` gain `parentId`, `FormulaTokenOptions` gains `fields` (`FormulaFieldLookup`, which decides whether a field called `parent` wins), and the server engine counts `{parent->x}` as a `parents()` read. `structureService.formulas.evaluate` refuses it without a record, as it refuses `{ref->x}`.
- **`listNodes` takes `ids`**: comma-separated over HTTP, 1 to 100 node ids, each checked (`400` otherwise); in process an array, an empty one listing nothing. Every other rule of the list still applies. A layout's link-field cards read with it. See the [query parameters](/modules/structure/configuration/#query-parameters).
- **Module fields in a layout.** The admin form records another module's field or link field that a card uses under `integrations.fields` as `{ enabled: true }`, so the server's check knows it.
- Everything is listed under [Page Layouts](/modules/structure/integration/#page-layouts-v1490) on the Integration page.

**Not in this release.** Four residuals are on the roadmap under [Known residuals of v1.49.0](/modules/structure/roadmap/#known-residuals-of-v1490): a data field's template that reads `{parent->x}` doesn't follow a change to the parent; a computed value can be missed if you leave the tab before it has been worked out; the reads behind a table card's connection source and the `ids` route check have no direct tests; and nothing has been checked by eye in a host app yet. Profile pages and chart cards come later.

**Tests.** 49 new tests in `tests/page-layout.test.ts`: the shared rules rendered into both halves, the field order across every field source with stale ids ignored, the layout's shape refusals, references refused only when a save brings them in, the detail page's decisions, the save's checks and the config round trip, the reads behind a card's entries (owner, section wall, unreadable types, secrets), and the parent token on both halves with its wall and secrets.

Frontend and backend, no schema change: both settings live in the type's existing `navigation` JSON. `npx structure update` brings it in; restart the backend if it doesn't reload by itself.

---

## v1.48.0

**The table view now uses the app's shared table** *(generated-output)*

The list page's **Table** view is now built on the starter's shared table, the one the admin pages use. It pages, sorts, hides columns and handles ticked rows the same way they do. What you'll see:

- **Columns.** **Name** comes first and can't be hidden. **Reference** comes next when the type gives out reference numbers. Then come the fields chosen under **Table columns** on the type's **Pages** tab. When none are chosen, the table shows the fields the cards show, and when the cards show none either, the type's first five fields that appear on the detail page. After those come any columns other modules add, then **Stage** when the type uses stages. **Status**, **Created** and **Updated** are there too, but hidden until you turn them on. A secret field is never a column, even when it has been chosen.
- **Columns and Density.** Two menus above the table show or hide columns and set how closely the rows sit. Your choice is remembered in this browser, separately for each type.
- **Pages and sorting.** You can show 20, 50 or 100 rows a page (it starts at 50). The server does the paging. It also does the sorting when you click a column heading, across all pages: by name, by date, and by any field it can sort. A sort stays with the type you chose it on, so another type's table starts unsorted. Changing the search, a filter, the status or the space goes back to the first page.
- **Search, filters and presets** stay where they were, above the table.
- **Values look as they do elsewhere.** A choice shows its label, a yes/no field shows **Yes** or **No**, an email address opens your mail program, and a web address opens in a new tab. A reference field shows the linked entries' names, each a link. A date-and-time field shows the date and the time, and a plain date shows the same day wherever you are.
- **Ticking rows.** You can tick only your own entries. With some ticked, the table offers **Archive**, **Restore**, **Delete** (the same delete question as the rest of the app, from [v1.43.0](#v1430)) and other modules' bulk actions. In **Deleted** it offers **Restore** and **Delete permanently**. Each row's menu offers what its card offers.
- **The buttons follow the type's permissions.** **Archive**, and **Restore** for an archived entry, now show only to people the type's **Archive** permission allows. **Delete** shows only to people its **Delete** permission allows. This holds in the table and in the grid and list views' selection bar. Before, the bar showed them to everyone, and the server then refused them.
- **Grouped types** show one table, without the groups. The grid and list views still group.
- **Linked names load in one go.** Wherever a reference field shows the names of the entries it links to, the names on the screen now come in one request instead of one request per entry.

**Behaviour that changes**
1. A type with no **Table columns** chosen now shows its card fields (or its first five detail fields), its reference number and its stage. Before, it showed only the name and the modules' columns.
2. A secret field chosen as a table column is left out. Before, it showed masked.
3. A grouped type's table view is one table. Before, it was one table per group.
4. The table pages itself: 20, 50 or 100 rows, with the table's own page controls in place of the page's **Previous** and **Next**.
5. **Name**, **Created** and **Updated** sort on the server. A chosen field column sorts when its `sortable` is on, which the **Table columns** picker sets for every field except secrets and field groups. A default column (a card field or one of the first five) sorts when the server can sort its field type. **Reference**, **Status** and **Stage** don't sort.
6. A module column with `sortable` and `getSortValue` sorts only the rows on the current page, because the server can't sort by it. `sortable` without `getSortValue` no longer makes the heading clickable. Before, it did, and the click sorted nothing.
7. In the grid and list views' selection bar, **Archive** and **Restore** need the type's archive permission and **Delete** its delete permission, as on the server.

**For developers**
- **New component:** `NodeDataTable` (`components/views/NodeDataTable.tsx`, exported from `components/views`, not from the feature barrel) renders entries on the starter's [`DataTable`](/starter-template/data-table/), always with its **Columns** and **Density** menus. Props: `nodes`, `columns` (`NodeColumnSpec[]`, memoised), `nodeType?`, `noun?`, `aria-label?`, `server?: { total, onQueryChange(query: NodeTableQuery) }` (server paging and sorting; without it the table pages and sorts the rows it is given), `initialSort?`, `initialPageSize?` (default 20), `pageSizes?`, `rowActions?`, `bulkActions?` (rows can be ticked only when there are some), `canSelectRow?`, `onRowClick?` (default: open the entry), `rememberColumns?` (a key, or `false`), `isLoading?`, `error?`, `onRetry?`, `emptyState?` and `toolbarEnd?`.
- **New util:** `utils/node-columns.ts` (React-free). A `NodeColumnSpec`'s `source` is `{ field }`, `{ builtIn }` (`name`, `reference`, `status`, `stage`, `createdAt` or `updatedAt`), `{ template }` (a merge template worked out per row with the cards' resolver, the row's own secrets left out) or `{ module }`. Its `kind` is `text`, `number`, `currency`, `percent`, `date`, `badge`, `yes-no`, `email`, `link`, `references` or `name`; a field's kind follows its type (`nodeFieldColumnKind`). Builders: `nodeTypeColumns(nodeType, fields, registered?)`, `nodeFieldColumn`, `nodeBuiltInColumn`, `nodeTemplateColumn` and `nodeModuleColumn`. Helpers: `nodeColumnKind`, `nodeFieldCellText`, `nodeColumnValue`, `nodeColumnHref`, `nodeFieldServerSort`, `nodeColumnSortable`, `nodeListSort` (a table sort as the list query's `sortBy`, or `dataSortField` and `dataSortType`), `sortRowsInPlace`, `dateOnlyParts` and `DEFAULT_TABLE_FIELD_COUNT` (5). These are exported from `@/features/structure/utils`. The table view state helpers, `initialNodeTableView`, `nodeTableView` and `nextNodeTableView`, and `dateOnlyMoment` are only in `utils/node-columns`.
- **New hook:** `useNodeTypeColumns(nodeType)` in `hooks/useNodeColumns.ts` returns `nodeTypeColumns` over the type's merged fields (own, inherited, template and module) and its registered module columns.
- **Batch names, backend:** `structureService.getNodeNames(ids, userId, { isAdmin? })` and `POST /nodes/names` with `{ ids }` (1 to 500) return `NodeNameRecord[]`, `[{ id, name, nodeType }]`, in the order asked, a repeated id once. Each id is read under `getNode`'s rules: own rows and the shared types' rows, the per-type read permission for non-admins, the active space (or the owner's unassigned rows), never a connection row. A missing or unreadable id is left out, with no error. Nothing of an entry's data comes back, so never a secret.
- **Batch names, frontend:** `structureService.getNodeNames(ids)`. `useNodeNames(ids)` keeps its shape, but reads through `createBatchReader` (new `utils/batch-reads.ts`): the ids asked for in the same tick, by every component on the page, go out as one request, in chunks of 500. Each name is cached under `structureKeys.key('node', id, 'name')` for 30 seconds, and taken from an entry already loaded with `useNode` without a request.
- **Removed:** `NodeTable` (`components/views/NodeTable.tsx`) and its `serverSort` and `onServerSort` props. The list page was its only user; code that imported it should move to `NodeDataTable`.

**Not in this release.** Four residuals are on the roadmap under [Known residuals of v1.48.0](/modules/structure/roadmap/#known-residuals-of-v1480): a grouped type shows one flat table; cancelling the delete question after a bulk **Delete** still clears the ticks; modules' `context-menu` actions show neither in a table row's menu nor on the cards; and the **Table columns** picker's empty text still says the table shows only the name and built-in fields.

**Tests.** 45 new tests: 37 in `tests/node-data-table.test.ts` (the kind of each field type, a type's columns, cell values, template columns, the list query sort, the sort and page staying with what they were chosen on, the permissions on the write actions, date cells and the batched names) and 8 in `tests/node-names.test.ts` (`getNodeNames` under `getNode`'s rules, and the route).

Frontend and backend (one new endpoint), no schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in. It lists the old `components/views/NodeTable.tsx` under **Orphaned files** and asks "Remove NodeTable.tsx? (will be backed up)". Say yes: nothing uses it now, and the file is moved to `.module-backups/structure/`.

## v1.47.2

**Admins are recognised everywhere, and an admin can do whatever the system can** *(generated-output)*

- **Admin-only buttons were hidden from admins.** Structure read the admin role from the sign-in session, but the starter keeps it on the user's profile (`useUserProfile().isAdmin`), so on hosts whose session carries no role an admin looked like everyone else: an admin-only type's **New** button, admin-only fields, and other admin controls were missing. A new `useIsAdmin()` hook reads the profile (with the session's role as the fallback outside the app shell), and all ten places use it.
- **No more "System / AI only".** A type permission, a field's **Editable By** or a built-in's **Editable By** set to `system` (older configs, or imported JSON) now lets admins in too: AI and modules never have more access than an admin. The admin UI offers **Anyone** and **Admin Only**; a stored `system` shows as Admin only.

Frontend and backend, no schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in; restart the backend if it doesn't reload by itself.

---

## v1.47.1

**The header "+" menu works again, "Create a …" in the picker, and generic wording** *(generated-output)*

- **The header "+" menu was empty.** The host now draws the dropdown itself and puts Structure's menu inside it, but the menu brought its own dropdown too, and a dropdown inside a dropdown never shows. It now returns just its items.
- **"Create a …" in the picker.** When there are none of the primary type yet, the header picker is a **Create a …** button (named after the type, with "a" or "an") that opens the type's create form.
- **The create form isn't pulled away.** With **Always have one … open**, opening the create form (the "+" menu, or a sidebar "New …" link, both use `?action=create`) no longer sends you straight to the open one, and the type's page waits until the create form is closed before opening the chosen one.
- **Generic wording.** The type page's Scope section said "(e.g. Matter)"; it now says "(a client, a project or a case, say)". The switch **Always have one … open** already used the type's own name.

Frontend only, no schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

---

## v1.47.0

**Build it: text before and after a value, a fallback when it's empty, and initials** *(generated-output)*

Kim asked for two things in **Build it**: words around a value that disappear when the value is empty (so "(" and ")" don't show around nothing), with something to show instead, and names without the full stop, which an admin can set up without a code change. Both are now building blocks in Build it, so a name like "Collier, K (helping me)", or "Collier, K" when there's no helping, needs no code.

**1. Before / After / If empty.** Each field part in Build it has a **Before / After / If empty** button. It opens three boxes, all optional:
- **Text before (only when it has a value)**, such as " (".
- **Text after (only when it has a value)**, such as ")".
- **If empty, show**, such as "Not given". It shows instead of the value when the value is blank, without the text before and after.

A part that joins several entries puts its text before and after around the whole list. The preview adds a second line showing what comes out when those fields are empty ("If Helping is empty"), worked out with sample values. A value with any of these is saved as a formula, so a data field's merge template and a card can't hold them, and say so ("Only a formula can add text before, after or if empty. To use them, make this a Formula field." and "A card can't add text before, after or if empty."). Without them, Build it saves exactly what it saved before.

**2. Initials, with or without full stops.** **How it shows** has an **Initials** group: **First initial (K.)**, **First initial (K)**, **Initials (K.A.)** and **Initials (KA)**. On a name field they come from the first name (first initial) or the first and middle names (initials). A text field, and the built-in name, description, slug and reference number, now have **How it shows** too: **As it is**, or the same four initials, one per word. A name's title, first name, middle name, surname and suffix are under their own **Parts** heading.

**3. Short names without full stops.** Next to each short name format there is one without full stops: **Initial and surname (J Smith)**, **Initials and surname (JQ Smith)**, **Surname and initial (Smith, J)** and **Surname and initials (Smith, JQ)**. Kim's "Collier, K" is **Surname and initial (Smith, J)**. They are in Build it and in merge templates and formulas, not in a name field's **Display order**.

**4. A computed name or description of only spaces isn't stored.** If a computed name or description works out as nothing but spaces, the form and the server leave the name or description that was typed or already stored. A value with text in it is stored as it is.

**5. A new formula function, `isBlank`.** `isBlank({nickname}) ? 'none' : {nickname}` shows "none" when the nickname is empty. It's what Build it uses for the fallback. A function an admin saved earlier under the name `isBlank` is now hidden by the new one, and the **Functions** page flags it: "A built-in function has this name, so formulas use the built-in instead. Rename this one to use it."

How it all works is on [Merge Fields](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#build-it-v1430) (Build it and [Formats](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#formats-v1430)), with `isBlank` on [Field Types](/modules/structure/fields/#formula) and the steps on [Usage](/modules/structure/usage/#build-it-v1430).

**Behaviour that changes**
1. Nothing saved changes. With no text before, after or if empty on any part, Build it writes the same template or formula as before.
2. A stored admin function named `isBlank` is no longer called: the built-in wins, as for any built-in name. The editor now also refuses a keyword's name (`map`, `children`, …) for a new function ("A built-in function already uses this name.").
3. A computed `name` or `description` that resolves to only whitespace is not written, by `formulaPatch` on the server or by `NodeForm`.
4. Initials are upper-cased with `toUpperCase`, never `toLocaleUpperCase`, in the formula formats and in the name field's display orders, so the browser and the server give the same letter in every locale (an "i" is never the Turkish dotted capital).
5. A formula's fetch budget (200 per evaluation) counts each entry a token reads once: a read the cache already holds costs nothing. Before, every read counted, so a part with text, which reads its reference twice, halved the entries it could read.
6. The secret guards, on the server and in the admin forms, read a formula's parsed tokens as well as its text, so `concat('{', {code})` no longer hides a secret token from them.

**For developers**
- **Formula built-in:** `isBlank(value)`, `category: 'logic'`, arity 1, in `registerBuiltinFormulaFunctions` (shared with the server), so `formulas.functions` now lists 51 built-in functions. Its test is `isBlankFormulaValue(value)` in `utils/formula`: true for `null`/`undefined`, text blank once trimmed, a non-finite number, a name, address or node-view whose text is blank, and an empty or all-blank list; false for `0` and `false`.
- **Token formats:** `FORMULA_TOKEN_FORMATS` adds `initial`, `initialPlain`, `initials`, `initialsPlain`, `initialSurnamePlain`, `initialsSurnamePlain`, `surnameInitialPlain` and `surnameInitialsPlain`. The initials work on a whole name (first name; first and middle names), a name part, any text (first word; each word) and through references. Any other value gives blank.
- **Merge builder:** `BuilderFieldPart` gains optional `before`, `after` and `ifEmpty`. A part with any of them is written as `isBlank({token}) ? 'ifEmpty' : concat('before', value, 'after')` (unset texts left out, `''` for no fallback) and `readBuilderValue` reads that shape back. New in `utils/merge-builder` (not re-exported from the barrel): `hasPartTexts`, `builderBlankFields` and `builderBlankSampleValues`. `BuilderShowOption` gains an optional `group` (`'Initials'`, `'Parts'`), and `builderShowOptions` now returns options for a `text` field. `ComputedValueEditor` takes an optional `textsNote`, the note a merge template shows for the texts.
- **Fetch budget:** `budgetedNodeRead(read, isRead, onFetch?)` in `utils/formula` charges `onFetch` only for a node not read yet. The browser's `resolveTokenValue` (its node cache) and the server's formula reads (their per-evaluation records) both use it.
- **Secret guards:** `secretTokensInTemplate`, in `utils/merge-fields` and in the backend's save-time guard, scan `templateTokensOf(text)` and `formulaTokensOf(text)` together.
- **Functions page:** `FormulaFunctionsAdminPage` flags a stored function whose name `formulaFunctionRegistry.has` or `isFormulaKeyword` matches, and refuses such a name for a new one.

**Not in this release.** Four residuals are on the roadmap under [Known residuals of v1.47.0](/modules/structure/roadmap/#known-residuals-of-v1470): the server doesn't refuse an admin function named like a built-in; the secret guard can refuse a fallback text that only looks like a secret's token; Build it doesn't offer a name part with a format, such as `{full_name.first|initial}`, which works typed by hand but reads as hand-written; and the no-full-stop formats aren't display orders.

**Tests.** 38 new tests in `tests/merge-builder-parts.test.ts`, the format ones run on both halves: every new format on whole names, parts, text, blanks and references; `isBlank`; write and read-back round trips for every combination; unchanged output without texts; the preview's empty case; the server storing what Build it writes (with the browser giving the same); a recompute storing a fallback; the fetch budget over 120 entries; names of only spaces; the brace secret guard; and the UI text.

Frontend and the shared formula code, plus three small backend changes (the stored-name check, the fetch budget and the secret guard). No schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in; restart the backend if it doesn't reload by itself.
