## v1.46.2

**A computed name, description or web address always follows its template** *(generated-output)*

Changing a computed name (for example to **Surname and initial**) and ticking **Also update the existing …** could report the names as already up to date, and leave a computed web address as it was. The cause: the built-in's **Editable By** setting, which sits just under the template on the type page. Set to **Admin Only** or **System / AI Only**, it locked the computed value as well as hand-typing, so **Update entries** skipped it, and so did every edit by someone it didn't allow.

- A computed name, description or slug now always follows its template: on every edit, on **Update entries** and the save dialog's update, and when an entry it reads changes.
- **Editable By** now only decides who may type a value by hand. A typed value for a locked built-in is still ignored, and the computed one is stored.
- To bring existing entries up to date, press **Update entries** on the type once.

Backend only, no schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in; restart the backend if it doesn't reload by itself.

---

## v1.46.1

**The header picker: a deleted record no longer lingers, a wider picker, and always one open** *(generated-output)*

**1. A deleted record no longer lingers in the picker.** Deleting the record you had open cleared the picker, but the detail page, still holding the record as it was a moment before, chose it again straight away, so it sat in the picker until the page was reloaded. The page now adopts the record it opens only once per visit. Archiving the chosen record now clears it too, as deleting does (single and bulk).

**2. A wider picker.** The header picker is wider (280px), and so is its dropdown (420px, never wider than the screen), so longer names fit. `NodeSelector` takes an optional `popoverClassName` for the dropdown's width.

**3. Always have one open.** A new switch on the primary type, under *Use as the primary scope*: **Always have one … open** (`primaryScope.requireSelection`).
- The header picker has no "All …" choice, and can't be cleared.
- With none chosen (the first visit, or the chosen one was deleted or archived), it chooses the most recently updated one.
- Choosing another while on one's page opens that one.
- Opening the type, for example from the sidebar, goes straight to the chosen one's page instead of the list. The Deleted and Archived views (`?status=deleted`, `?status=archived`) still show the list, so deleted ones can be restored. With none at all, the list shows its empty state.
- For developers: `useCurrentScope()` also returns `ready` (the saved choice has loaded).

Frontend only, no schema change: the setting is stored in the type's `primaryScope` JSON. `npx structure update` brings it in.

---

## v1.46.0

**Reference fields can connect the chosen entries** *(generated-output)*

Kim asked that using a node reference field should also create a connection automatically. A reference field now has a switch for it, **Also connect the chosen entries**. It is off until an admin turns it on, so other apps and modules don't change. **No schema change:** the setting is stored in each type's fields JSON, and the connections are ordinary connections. `npx structure update` brings it in.

**1. The switch.** In a Node Reference field's settings, after **Maximum number of entries**: **Also connect the chosen entries**, with the hint "Each chosen entry gets a connection named after this field, shown on both entries' Connections tab." It is on a node type's fields and their sub-fields, and on field templates. A field a type inherits connects too.

**2. A connection type named after the field.** Saving the type makes it, with no setup: a Matter's **People** field gets a "People" connection type, so the Connections tab and the graph say what the link means. It follows the field's label and allowed types. The **Connection Types** tab shows it with a **From a field** badge, and it isn't offered when someone adds a connection by hand.

**3. One connection for two entries.**
- Two entries get one connection from their fields, however many fields point between them, in either direction.
- It is made with the first reference, unless the two are already connected: any connection, of any type, either way, one added by hand included.
- It goes only when the last reference between them goes.
- A connection someone added by hand is never removed, and never taken over by a field.
- A multiple field connects to each entry chosen in it.

**4. Locked by hand.** On the Connections tab a field-made connection has a lock where the remove button was, with the note: This connection comes from the "People" field of "Smith & Jones". Change that field to remove it. The server refuses a delete, archive or edit by hand with the same note.

**5. Existing entries.** Turning the switch on connects the entries already saved, straight after the save, with one progress message ("Updating connections… 400 of 1,234", ending "Added 12 connections."). Turning it off removes the connections the field made ("Removed 12 connections."); connections added by hand stay. **Update entries** does it again, and its question says "It also updates the connections its reference fields make." A configuration import does it in the background, with no message.

**6. Group headings.** The Connections tab now heads every group with its connection type's name. Before, it showed the slug.

The rules are on [Field Types](/modules/structure/fields/#connecting-the-chosen-entries-v1460), what you see on [Usage](/modules/structure/usage/#connecting-the-chosen-entries-v1460), and the connection type on [Configuration](/modules/structure/configuration/#connection-types-made-for-reference-fields-v1460).

**Behaviour that changes**
1. Nothing changes for a field with the switch off, or for connections added by hand.
2. A connection a reference field made can't be deleted or archived by any caller but the system, and a request can't edit it: a `400` with the note. Bulk delete and bulk archive report it as a per-row error.
3. Only the system can set or remove the field-made marker (`structure:referenceField`) on a connection: `createConnection` drops a caller's, and `updateConnection` keeps the stored one.
4. `importNodeTypes` makes the connection types of the imported types' connecting fields, returns `connectionSync` when it then syncs any types, and syncs their existing entries in the background.
5. A configuration import brings the existing entries of the types it wrote in step in the background, after it commits.
6. A connection type made for a field can't be deleted while the field connects (`Turn off "Also connect the chosen entries" on the "People" field first.`). Once the field is off, deleting the type removes its field-made connections for good.
7. The Connections tab heads each group with the connection type's name, and the slug only when the type has none.
8. **Update entries** on a type with any reference field also updates its connections, after the computed values. With no field connecting, that only clears connections a field turned off left behind, and says nothing when there are none.

**Changes to the brief as first written**
- **How a field finds its type.** The brief suggested `moduleId: 'structure:reference-field'` plus a fixed slug. The `moduleId` names the field instead (`structure:reference-field:<type>:<path>`), and it, never the slug, is how a field finds its type. The slug is plain (`ref-<type>-<field>`) only when every part is lowercase letters and digits and it fits 100 characters. Otherwise, or when another type holds the plain slug, it ends in `--` and an 8-digit hash, so no two fields ever share a type.
- **A field turned off or deleted** leaves its type. Deleting the type is refused while the field connects, and once it is off the type's field-made links go with it.
- **Still held by another field.** When the field that made a link lets go but another field still holds the pair, the link is changed in place to that field's type and direction (`structure.connection.updated`), so its type always names a field that holds it.
- **Merge.** `mergeNode` doesn't re-point references, so it doesn't re-point field-made links either: they stay with the duplicate. The references the canonical gains from the backfill connect.
- **Restore.** A restore reads its links again just after it commits, not inside its own transaction, so a failure there is logged and never fails the restore.
- **Bulk delete** of a field-made link gives a per-row error.
- **Update entries** does the connections too, rather than a separate **Update connections** action.
- **Imports** bring existing entries in step in the background after the commit, so a large import holds no long transaction.
- **A refused link.** A request's references have already passed the section wall, so a link is refused only when an admin has changed the field's connection type (its `allowGlobalTypes`, From or To types, or a maximum), or for a system write's reference across two sections. It is skipped with a warning and never fails the save.

**For developers**
- **New, backend:** `structureService.syncReferenceConnections(slug, actorId, { caller, after?, next?, maxEntries? })` and `POST /types/:slug/reference-connections` (`syncReferenceConnectionsSchema`, body `{ after? }`, admin only), returning `SyncReferenceConnectionsResult` `{ created, removed, unchanged, failed, remaining, next }`; `referenceConnectionSyncsSettled()`, exported from `structure.service.ts`; `connectionSync?: string[]` on the result of `importNodeTypes`, only when there are some; `connect?: boolean` on `nodeReferenceConfig`.
- **The marker:** `metadata['structure:referenceField'] = { nodeType, field }` on each field-made connection: the declaring type and the field's path (`group.sub` for a sub-field).
- **The lock:** `deleteConnection`, `deleteNode` (with or without `keepContents`) and `archiveNode` refuse a field-made connection for non-system callers; `updateConnection` and `updateNodeMetadata` refuse it for requests (`viaHttp`) only. System callers may change it.
- **Hooks:** for field-made links, after the commit, `structure.connection.created`, `structure.connection.updated` (a link moved to another field) and `structure.connection.deleted`, then the formulas of both ends; none for a `bulkCreateNodes` with `skipHooks: true`. For the connection types Structure makes, `structure.connectionType.created` and `structure.connectionType.updated`. A repeat save writes nothing and fires nothing.
- **Limits:** the sync reads 200 entries at a time and works runs of up to 50 entries of one owner, in transactions of at most 200 pairs, up to 5,000 entries and 20 seconds per call. A write that connects gets a 30-second transaction budget. The pair locks are one statement per thousand pairs, new links are written with `createMany`, and `IN` lists hold at most 500 ids.
- **New, frontend:** the hook `useReferenceConnectionsSync()` (in `hooks/useNodeTypes`); `structureService.syncReferenceConnections(slug, { after? })`; the type `SyncReferenceConnectionsResult`; `connectingReferencePaths`, `isFieldMadeConnection`, `isReferenceFieldConnectionType` and `referenceConnectionNote` in `utils/node-values`; `connect?: boolean` on `nodeReferenceConfig`.
- The whole in-process contract is on [Integration](/modules/structure/integration/#reference-field-connections-v1460).

**Not in this release.** Three residuals are on the roadmap under [Known residuals of v1.46.0](/modules/structure/roadmap/#known-residuals-of-v1460): the import's background sync has no progress message; a field whose two slugs are both held by other connection types connects nothing (logged); and the import-time scan for field-made links isn't indexed.

**Tests.** 42 new tests in `tests/reference-connections.test.ts` cover the setting and its refusals, the connection type (made, renamed, idempotent, sub-fields, template and inherited fields, the slugs), every write path, one per pair and the last reference, the lock for requests and the system, the sync (on, off, the cursor, across owners), the section wall, imports, deleting the type, and the frontend helpers.

Frontend and backend, no schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

---

## v1.45.2

**More ways to show a name, and leaving asks first** *(generated-output)*

**1. Name display.** A name field's **Display order** has three more choices:
- **First and last name (Jane Smith)** (`'first-last'`): no title, middle names or suffix.
- **Initials and surname (J.Q. Smith)** (`'initials-surname'`) and **Surname and initials (Smith, J.Q.)** (`'surname-initials'`): one initial for each word of the first and middle names.
- Merge templates and formulas have them too, as `{full_name|firstLast}`, `{full_name|initialsSurname}` and `{full_name|surnameInitials}`, and **Build it** offers them.

**2. Leaving asks first.** Leaving an entry you're editing with changes not saved, for another page, another entry or with Back, now asks "Save your changes?" instead of saving them:
- **Keep editing** stays.
- **Discard changes** leaves without saving.
- **Save changes** saves and then leaves. If the save is refused, you stay with your changes, and the reason shows.

Switching tab on the same entry still keeps your changes without asking. If the page goes away some other way, the changes are still saved, so nothing typed is lost. Uses React Router's `useBlocker`, so the host must use a data router (the starter's `RouterProvider` does).

Frontend only, no schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

---

## v1.45.1

**Two more ways to show a name, and Save changes on the detail page** *(generated-output)*

**1. Initial and surname.** A name field's **Display order** has two more choices: **Initial and surname (J. Smith)** and **Surname and initial (Smith, J.)** (`nameConfig.displayOrder` `'initial-surname'` and `'surname-initial'`).
- They show the first name's initial and the surname, without the title, middle names or suffix. With no first name they show the surname alone, and with no surname, given names first.
- Merge templates and formulas have them too: `{full_name|initialSurname}` gives J. Citizen and `{full_name|surnameInitial}` gives Citizen, J., through a reference as well (`{client->full_name|initialSurname}`). **Build it** offers both.
- Picking a name field in **Build it** starts in the field's own display order.

**2. Save changes.** Editing an entry on its detail page no longer saves after each pause in typing. While you edit, the header shows **Cancel** and **Save changes** instead of **Done**.
- **Save changes** sends only the fields you changed, in one save, and leaves edit mode. If the save is refused, you stay in edit mode with your changes, and the reason shows.
- **Cancel** drops the changes, and asks "Discard your changes?" first when there are any.
- Switching tab keeps the changes. Leaving the entry, for another page or another entry, saves them, so nothing typed is lost. Closing or reloading the browser tab with changes not saved asks first.
- For developers: `NodeDetailHeader` takes optional `onSaveEdit`, `onCancelEdit` and `isSavingEdit` (without `onSaveEdit` it shows **Edit** and **Done** as before), and the Info tab writes into `NodeEditDraftContext` when the page provides one. Used anywhere else, the Info tab still saves 500 ms after the last change.

Frontend only, no schema change. `npx structure update` brings it in.

---

## v1.45.0

**Kim's field batch: show on create or on the detail page, value rules, one per section, reference edit, delete and maximum, and updating existing entries, with the v1.44.0 review's hardening** *(generated-output)*

Kim's notes from writing veradai's types in JSON asked for four field settings, and for a way to bring existing entries up to date when a computed value changes. They are all here, as generic settings any app can use, together with the four hardening items (H1–H4) the v1.44.0 review found. **No schema change:** the new settings are stored in each type's fields JSON. Hosts need only the new templates (`npx structure update`; `--skip-schema` is fine), which add one new file, `components/fields/ReferenceEntryActions.tsx`. Every key, with its default, where it is allowed and what is refused, is on [Field Types](/modules/structure/fields/#showing-setting-and-one-per-section-v1450) and [JSON Import & Export](/modules/structure/json-reference/#show-set-and-one-per-section-v1450).

**1. Show when creating, and on the detail page (F1).** Two switches on every field, both on by default: **Show when creating** (`showOnCreate`) and **Show on the detail page** (`showOnDetail`). Only `false` is stored.
- Turned off, the field is left off the create form (and a reference field's **Create new** popup), or off the detail page and the edit form. Its value, its default and its value rules still apply.
- A field the create can't do without stays on the create form anyway: one that is typed in, gets no default and is required, or that the entry's name is made from. The field editor says why, for example "Still shown when creating: it's required and has no default."
- On the detail page, a hidden field still shows while it is required and empty, and stays for the rest of that edit once shown.

**2. Set automatically: value rules (F2).** `setWhen: [{ when, value }]`. While a rule's condition holds, the field takes the rule's value, for example the category becomes Family while "This is me" is ticked.
- The first rule that holds wins, at most 20 rules. The condition uses the **Visible When** rules and reads only the entry's own data fields.
- On the forms, the field is locked while its rule holds, with a hint: "Set to Family while This is me is on."
- On the server, the rules run again on every write, system callers included, since a rule is part of the type's data definition. The rule's value wins over what the write sends, with no error.
- In the field editor: **Set automatically**, with **Add rule**, the condition builder and the field's own input for the value.

**3. One per section (F3).** `uniqueInScope: 'section'` on a yes/no field: at most one entry per Matter (or whatever the isolated type is called) has it on.
- Ticking it on one entry unticks it on the others in the same Matter, in the same save.
- Entries in no Matter yet (unassigned) are left out until they are assigned. Assigning one that is ticked unticks the others in its new Matter.
- The switch reads **Only one per Matter**.

**4. Reference fields: Edit, Delete and a maximum (F4).** Three new `nodeReferenceConfig` settings, next to **Allow creating new entries here**:
- **Allow editing the chosen entries here** (`allowEdit`): an **Edit** button beside each chosen entry opens its form in place.
- **Allow deleting the chosen entries here** (`allowDelete`): a **Delete** button deletes the entry itself, through the usual delete question, and takes it out of the field. Removing an entry from the field without deleting it works as before.
- **Maximum number of entries** (`maxItems`, 1 to 1000, for a multiple field): the picker stops offering **Add** at the limit ("You can choose up to 10 entries."), and the server refuses more.
- As Kim asked, the Field Types page now lists every reference setting in one table, old and new: `allowedTypes`, `excludeTypes`, `multiple`, `maxItems`, `filter`, `allowGlobalTypes`, `allowCreate`, `createTypes`, `allowEdit`, `allowDelete` and `syncTargetField`. See [node-reference](/modules/structure/fields/#node-reference).

**5. Updating existing entries (D6).** A computed value is stored when an entry is saved, so changing how a type works one out used to leave the existing entries as they were.
- **The save asks.** When a type save changes a computed definition and the type has entries, a dialog lists the changed fields and has one checkbox, **Also update the existing people (12 entries)**, **unticked**: left alone, only new and edited entries get the new values. It warns when the slug's definition changed, because the entries' web addresses would change.
- **Update entries,** a new button on the type page, works out every computed value of every existing entry again: for example after a field template or parent type changed, or to refresh values stored before the H1 fix (6).
- **Progress** shows in one message ("Updating entries… 400 of 1,234"), and ends with what was updated.
- **For modules:** the admin-only `recomputeTypeValues` and `countRecomputeEntries`, and `GET` and `POST /types/:slug/recompute`. A call works through at most 5,000 entries, 200 at a time, each as its owner would see it, and returns `{ updated, unchanged, failed, remaining, next }`. Each changed entry fires `structure.node.updated` with `source: 'formula'`.

See [Updating existing entries](/modules/structure/configuration/#updating-existing-entries-v1450).

**6. Hardening from the v1.44.0 review (H1–H4).**
- **H1. Hidden columns stay hidden.** Through a reference, a stored value (a computed name, slug or description, a merge template, a formula) now reads only the referenced record's data fields and its built-ins and dates, the same list the reads for other modules use, on the form and the server alike. Before, it could read the text prepared for search (`embeddingText`, which can hold a secret's plain text), the owner's id, or the favourite and pin flags, and store it unencrypted. And no response hands back `embeddingText` or `embeddingModel` any more, for anyone's row: reads, write answers and the public API alike. See [Node reference dereference](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#node-reference-dereference).
- **H2. `scopeRootId` is checked for modules too.** Every in-process read that takes a `scopeRootId` refuses anything other than a non-empty string, `null` or nothing, with `getNode`'s `400` `Invalid scope for this request`. Leaving it out means no scope, and `null` means what it meant before (no scope on most reads, "outside every section" on the reads that offer that).
- **H3. `listNodes` with subtypes** decides space confinement on the types with their subtypes added, as `queryNodes` does.
- **H4. `withinSectionOf`** reads its host under an explicit `scopeRootId`, as `getNode` does.

**Behaviour that changes**
1. A stored value that read a hidden column through a reference now reads blank there. Values stored earlier keep their text until the entry is saved again or **Update entries** runs. The exception is a data field's merge template that now works out blank altogether: nothing is written then, so it keeps its old text until it is cleared by hand. Templates that read data fields, built-ins or dates are unchanged.
2. No read or write answer carries `embeddingText` or `embeddingModel`: both are `null` on every row, the owner's own included, in the app's API, the public API, the searches and the embedding backfill list. Action-hook payloads still carry the row as stored.
3. An in-process `scopeRootId` other than a non-empty string or `null` is a `400`, system callers included. Before, an object such as `{ not: '' }` widened a list to every section, and `getNode` gave a `404`, a `400` or a normal read depending on the data.
4. `listNodes` with `includeSubtypes` keeps to the active space when a subtype is scoped, and the unscoped type's own rows outside the space drop out too. With both `type` and `types`, `types` decides.
5. `withinSectionOf` under an explicit `scopeRootId`: a host outside that section, or an unassigned host, is a `404`; with no saved space, a host in the named section is now read (before, a `404`).
6. The reference field's **Create new** popup now starts a new entry with its type's create defaults, as the full create form does, and applies the type's value rules, locking a field a rule sets. Before, the popup filled in no defaults.
7. Value rules apply to every write, system callers included, so a module that writes a field a rule sets gets the rule's value instead, with no error.
8. Turning a one-per-section field on changes other entries: they are turned off in the same save, and each fires `structure.node.updated` with no `source`.
9. The reference write-back (`syncTargetField`) applies the target entry's own value rules in the same write, and its hook reports them.
10. A configuration that makes a field one per section while a reference's `syncTargetField` keeps it on is refused, from either side, on every save, the configuration import included.
11. `setWhen` or `uniqueInScope` on a connection type's own field is refused on save.
12. The condition builder offers **Is on** and **Is off** for a yes/no field. This also makes **Visible When** and **Required When** on a toggle easy to get right: before, the builder offered only Equals, Not equals, Is one of and Has value, and Has value holds for an unticked box too.
13. In the field editor, changing a field's type, or giving it a merge template, takes off a `uniqueInScope` or `setWhen` it can no longer keep.
14. When a type has fields but none of them is shown, the detail page says "None of its fields are shown on this page."

**Changes to the brief as first written**
- **F1.** A field that a refused save names stays shown until the form closes. A default on a field with a `visibleWhen` condition, or with `isHidden`, is never filled in, so such a field stays on the create form when it is required. The popup's create defaults leave out a reference field's default, which a new entry can't hold.
- **F2.** `setWhen` and `uniqueInScope` can't be on the same field: a rule could turn back on what one per section turns off. A condition can't read a built-in, a secret, a formula, a merge-template field or another field with rules (no chains). A rule whose value no longer fits the field is skipped with a warning, never a `400`. A restore runs no rules, because it writes no data.
- **F3.** Unassigned entries are left out (the brief left that open). A section's own top-level record never competes, archived entries do, and a subtype that declares its own field of the same id competes on its own. The pair with `syncTargetField` is refused on save, with a backstop for a pair stored by hand. A field template, which has no type, offers the switch once any type is isolated, named after all of them.
- **F4.** The buttons are hidden, not greyed out, for someone who can't use them, as elsewhere in the app; only an unassigned entry's **Edit** is greyed out, with a hint, because assigning the entry fixes it. `maxItems` is checked on a single field too, but limits only a multiple one; new-entry placeholders count towards it.
- **D6.** **Update entries** was added, for values the save dialog can't see. The dialog's checkbox updates only the changed definitions, plus the values on the same entry that read them. A data field's merge template is worked out on the server by the update only; an ordinary save still leaves it to the form. Each entry gets up to three rounds of templates and formulas, so one that reads another comes out current. A call starts no new batch after 20 seconds. An app-level type's update includes other owners' entries of their own subtypes of it. A computed name that clashes with a sibling's counts as failed.
- **H1.** The two columns are `null` on the owner's own rows and on every write's answer too, not only on other owners' rows.
- **H3.** With both `type` and `types`, `types` decides, as the type filter does.

**For developers**
- **New, backend:** `structureService.recomputeTypeValues` and `structureService.countRecomputeEntries`; `recomputeEntryCap`, `writtenNodeRow` and `assertScopeRootIdOption`, exported from `structure.service.ts`; the routes `GET` and `POST /types/:slug/recompute` (`recomputeTypeValuesSchema`); the types `RecomputeCaller` and `RecomputeTypeValuesResult`.
- **New, shared** (`utils/formula`): `REFERENCE_READ_COLUMNS`, the one list of columns a `{ref->column}` reads. The formula engine's own `RECORD_READ_COLUMNS` and the `FormulaReadsOptions.columns` option are gone.
- **New, shared** (`utils/field-conditions` and `structure.conditions`): `matchingValueRule`, `valueRulePatch`, `valueRuleRecord`, `valueRuleProblem`, `valueRuleValueProblem`, `MAX_VALUE_RULES` and `VALUE_RULE_TARGET_TYPES`, one code for both halves.
- **New, frontend:** in `utils/field-conditions`, `staysOnCreate`, `isShownOnCreate`, `isShownOnDetail`, `fieldsShownInEdit`, `stayOnCreateHint`, `quickCreateDefaults`, `quickCreateNameBlank`, `quickCreateRules`, `withEntryRulesThatFit`, `activeValueRule`, `describeFieldCondition` and `describeValueRule`; `seededCreateDefault` in `utils/node-values`; the hook `useTypeRecompute` (in `hooks/useNodeTypes`) and `invalidateNodeQueries(queryClient)` (in `hooks/useNodeMutations`); `structureService.countRecomputeEntries` and `recomputeTypeValues`; the types `NodeTypeSaveOptions`, `RecomputeTypeValuesInput` and `RecomputeTypeValuesResult`; `ConfirmOptions.details`; and two optional `NodeSelector` `draftFields` members, `rules` and `nameBlank`, with an optional `ruleHint` for `render`.
- **New file:** `components/fields/ReferenceEntryActions.tsx`, mapped in `module.ts`.

**Not in this release.** The small residuals the build left open are on the roadmap under [Later](/modules/structure/roadmap/#known-residuals-of-v1450). Among them: the **AI** tab's fields don't offer the new settings, and one **Update entries** call can fire up to 5,000 `structure.node.updated` hooks.

**Tests.** 145 new tests cover the four field settings and their refusals, the server applying the rules on every write path, the one-per-section turn-offs on every write path (including concurrent saves), the update of existing entries and its paging, the reference limit, the hidden columns on every read and write, and H2–H4. Each new setting and each fix has tests that fail on the old templates; the rest pin what must stay the same. There are 877 in all.

Frontend and backend, no schema change. Structure's files type-check against a scratch copy of veradai with 0 errors in both halves.

---

## v1.44.0

**The formula engine and record reads, for other modules** *(generated-output)*

Other modules in the same app can now use Structure's merge templates, formulas and conditions on the server, instead of writing their own. AI Core asked for five reads (S1–S5). Kim added the engine itself (E1–E6), so AI Core doesn't have to reinvent how formulas work. **It is the same engine Structure's forms and stored formula fields use:** the same tokens, functions, admin-defined functions and limits, except a function that a module adds only in the browser. All of it is in-process, on `serviceRegistry.get('structure')`, with no web routes, and none of it saves anything or fires an action. The full contract is [AI Core: record reads and the formula engine](/modules/structure/contracts/#ai-core-record-reads-and-the-formula-engine).

**1. Five reads (S1–S5).** Each one reads as the person the call is for. A record is read through the same check `getNode` makes, so one they can't read is "not found".
- **`resolveTemplate`** fills in a merge template for a record, such as `Your lawyer, {lawyer->first_name}`, and lists the tokens that came out blank, so the caller can tell when a value is incomplete.
- **`evaluateFormula`** works out a formula for a record, such as `count(children('task'))`.
- **`matchesCondition`** says whether a condition holds for a record, in the `visibleWhen` rules a form uses to show a field. A rule can compare one field with another, as in `registry` equals `{home_registry}`.
- **`queryNodes`** finds records by type and condition: for example, the directory entries for a Matter's registry, kept to what the Matter may point at (its own section and the shared types). It returns up to 100 at a time, ordered by name or by a field.
- **`getMergeFieldSchema`** lists a type's tokens, for an **Insert field** picker in another module's editor.

**2. The engine itself (E1–E6).** `structureService.formulas` works on values the caller supplies, with no record:
- `evaluate` works out a formula, and `check` says what a formula reads and calls without running it, for an editor;
- `functions` lists the functions a formula can call, and `fillTemplate` fills in a merge template;
- `matches` tests a condition, and `format` writes a value out the way a formula field shows it.

Anything that needs a record, such as `children()`, `nodes()` or `{ref->field}`, is refused in a formula with "needs a record; use evaluateFormula", and left blank in a template or a condition.

**3. Kept safe.**
- **Secrets are never read**, neither the record's own nor a referenced record's. A search, sort or condition that names a secret field is refused.
- **What a record can read follows its own section.** A record in a Matter reads that Matter and the shared types. A record in no section reads only records in no section and shared records.
- **Through a reference, a token reads only the record's fields, its built-in details and its dates.** It never reads the text prepared for search, which can contain a secret, or anyone's favourite and pin marks.
- **Admin rights open only the record named,** never what is read through it.
- **Limits.** A template, or one condition's tokens, reads at most 200 records. A query tests at most 5,000 records, or 500 when it filters on a field worked out when shown, and says `truncated` when it stopped early.

**4. One list of merge-field tokens.** The code that lists a type's tokens is now one shared file, used by the browser and the server, so the pickers and `getMergeFieldSchema` can't drift apart. The browser's list is unchanged: a test compares it with a copy saved before the move.

**Nothing changes for existing callers.** `getNode`, `listNodes`, the forms, field visibility and stored formulas give the same results as before. `listNodes` now shares its section and sorting code with `queryNodes`, and all its tests pass unchanged.

**Changes to the request as first written**
- **An index is written `{group[0].sub}`.** `{group.0.sub}` isn't a token form, on the server or in the form, so it reads as blank.
- **A plain `{select}` gives the stored value,** as the form shows it. `{select|label}` gives the label.
- **A condition on a field the record lacks reads the field as blank,** as a form does, rather than always being false. `isEmpty`, `notEquals` and `notIn` hold, and so does `lessThan` a positive number, because blank compares as 0.
- **A `{token}` in a condition's value reads the stored value,** such as a select's option value, not the display text, so one select can be compared with another.
- **An empty condition holds,** as in a form: it isn't refused.
- **A list of blank values writes out as `, `** in a formula's `text`, so test `value` for "blank".
- **More refusals,** each with the agreed prefix, except `getNode`'s `Invalid scope for this request`: conditions a form would misread (rules beside a single condition, a one-line `notIn`, a one-line `contains`, `greaterThan` or `lessThan` that is blank or holds a `{token}`), queries with a bad `limit`, `orderBy` or `hostNodeId`, a `{token}` with no host, a sort on a secret field, and the read limits.
- **`getMergeFieldSchema` leaves out** secret tokens, and tokens that would always read blank: those into types the person can't read without admin rights, for admins too. Fields and types that only the browser adds aren't on the server, so they aren't listed.
- **A job with no request** passes the record's section as `scopeRootId`: the record's `rootNodeId`, or the `sectionRootId` that `getNodeSection` gives with `isSystem: true`.
- **The engine,** which the request didn't ask for. In it, `formulas.matches` returns a promise, and `formulas.functions` also gives each function's category and number of arguments.

**Not in this release.** Two questions for Kim are on the roadmap's [Decisions needed](/modules/structure/roadmap/#decisions-needed): should admin rights reach through a pinned record, and should other modules get a public way to add server-side formula functions. Four hardening items the review found outside these reads (H1–H4) are planned for v1.45.0, with the field batch: see [Now / Next](/modules/structure/roadmap/#now--next).

**Tests.** 160 new tests cover S1–S5 and E1–E6 with their refusals, a 34-row table that checks each token form gives the same text on the server and in the form, the browser's token list against a copy saved before the move, the section rules, the scan cap and the read limits. There are 732 in all.

Frontend and backend, no schema change. Structure's files type-check against a scratch copy of veradai with 0 errors in both halves.

---

## v1.43.0

**Delete and keep, unassigned records, a guided builder for templates and formulas, and automatic slugs** *(generated-output)*

The fixes for "the saving and deleting is messy", and the four things asked for with it. **Hosts need `npx structure update` (schema change and migration).**

**1. What was going wrong.** Deleting a Matter did delete its people and children: they went to Deleted with it. Three things made it look otherwise.
- **The deleted Matter stayed the active space.** Its people were confined to it, so once the space changed they couldn't be found, not even under Deleted. A deleted record is now never the active space, and deleting the open one clears it.
- **A deleted entry still held its name and slug.** The `(userId, parentId, name)` and `(userId, slug)` unique keys counted entries in Deleted, so re-adding a deleted person to the Matter was "already exists here", while nothing the user could see had the name. They now count live entries only.
- **A blank slug was a value.** The form's empty URL Slug box was saved as `''`, so a second entry with no slug was "An entry with this slug already exists". A blank slug is now stored as no slug.

A person created at the top level, or restored after their Matter was deleted, was in no section, so the enforced space hid them everywhere. They now show as unassigned records (3).

**2. Subtypes are scoped.** A type based on a scoped type, directly or through others, is scoped too: a Child based on Person is confined to the active Matter as a Person is. It holds wherever scoping is read: the list page, the server's read confinement, the section wall and the unassigned rule. See [Primary scope](/modules/structure/configuration/#primary-scope).

**3. Unassigned records.** A row of a scoped type that is in no live section is **unassigned**. See [Unassigned records](/modules/structure/configuration/#unassigned-records-v1430).
- **Visible to its owner** beside the active space: in lists, `getNode`, the neighbourhood reads, the tree and search, and in lists instead of nothing when no space is open. Rows carry `unassigned: true`, and `?unassigned=true` lists only them. Pickers inside a section and reads with an explicit `scopeRootId` stay confined.
- **Read-only** for request callers and in-process callers without `isSystem`, with a `400` that names the isolated type: `"Sam Smith" isn't in a Matter yet. Assign it to one before editing it.` Archive, delete, restore, favourite and pin stay open.
- **Assigning.** `assignToSection` and `POST /nodes/:id/assign` file the row into a section: under the root when the hierarchy allows it, otherwise top level and labelled into it. The unassigned rows linked to it go with it, the move wall applies, and links are re-stamped. A move into a live section assigns too.
- **In the app,** an **Unassigned** badge on cards, list rows, the table and the detail header; on the detail page, "Not in a Matter yet. Assign it to a Matter to edit it." with an **Assign to…** picker; and an **Unassigned** button on the list pages of types that can be unassigned. See [Unassigned entries](/modules/structure/usage/#unassigned-entries).

**4. Deleting a record that contains records.**
- **The impact.** `getDeleteImpact` (`GET /nodes/:id/delete-impact`) and `getBulkDeleteImpact` (`POST /nodes/delete-impact`) return `{ descendants, labelled, byType, sectionRoot }`.
- **The dialog** says, for example: "Smith & Jones - me" contains 4 records: 2 People, 2 Children. A checkbox, **Also delete the records inside it**, is ticked by default. Unticked, it says: They'll be kept as unassigned records, and each needs to be assigned to a Matter before it can be edited. (They'll be kept at the top level, for an entry that isn't a section root.) Bulk delete shows the summed impact with the same checkbox, and all four delete places use it.
- **`keepContents`** on `deleteNode`, `bulkDeleteNodes`, `DELETE /nodes/:id?keepContents=true` and the bulk-delete body deletes the record alone. Its direct children go to the top level (in the record's live section, if it was in one), or under its parent, and their subtrees follow. The references between the kept rows and a deleted section's rows are removed both ways. The default is still the cascade. Restoring the record later doesn't bring the kept rows back.

See [Deleting a record that contains records](/modules/structure/configuration/#deleting-a-record-that-contains-records-v1430).

**5. Build it: a guided builder for merge templates and formulas.** Next to **Advanced** (the text box as before) wherever a computed value is written: the built-in name, description and slug, data-field merge templates, formula fields, the card's title and body, field templates and connection-type fields.
- **Parts:** text, or a field's value: own, inherited, from a field template or module, or a built-in (name, description, slug, reference number, stage, status). Through a reference, any field of the entry it points at. A name's full name, surname-first name or one part; an address's line or one part; a select's label or value; a date in one of seven formats; and for several entries, a separator and "and" before the last.
- **Writing:** a merge template when one can say it, otherwise a formula (`concat(…)` and `join(list, sep, last)`, never `+`). Admins don't need to write `join(map(…))`.
- **Reading back:** a value Build it couldn't have written says "This one was written by hand — edit it in Advanced."
- **Safety:** secrets are never offered (through a reference, an id that is a secret on any type it can point at), nor the field being edited or anything that reads it.
- **Preview** for an active entry of the type, or sample values, warning when the result is blank or too long.
- **"This field's formula":** the merge-field pickers label every formula field, and every field with a merge template, "(formula)", as in **Total (formula)**.
- **New tokens:** a part through a reference (`{focus_parent->full_name.last}`); formats (`{dob|long}`, `{kind|label}`, `{full_name|surnameFirst}`, `{home|locality}`); and `{stage}`, `{status}` and `{reference}` for the entry itself. The new formula function `formatDate(date, preset)`, and `join` takes a last separator.

See [Build it](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#build-it-v1430) and [Formats](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#formats-v1430).

**6. Automatic slugs.** One `slugify`, the same on both halves: accents transliterated (é → e, ß → ss), lower case, every run of other characters one `-`, trimmed, at most 255 characters.
- **A computed slug** (a template or formula) is slugified by the form and the server, on create, update, bulk create, new reference entries and recompute. A slug another live entry holds is stored as `base-2`, `base-3` and so on, instead of failing with a `409`. The suffix stays only while another entry, live, archived or Deleted, holds the base.
- **A typed slug** is slugified by the form and the Settings tab when the box loses focus, and on save. The server keeps a typed slug as sent, and a taken one is still a `409`.

See [How a slug is made](/modules/structure/fields/#how-a-slug-is-made-v1430).

**Behaviour that changes**
1. A Deleted entry no longer holds its slug or its name, so people can be re-added after a delete. A restore that now clashes is a `409` that says so.
2. A blank slug (`''` or spaces) is stored as no slug.
3. A computed slug that clashes gets a `-2` suffix instead of a `409`, and computed slugs are lower case. The suffix drops when no other entry holds the base.
4. A deleted record is never the active space.
5. Subtypes of scoped types are scoped.
6. Unassigned rows are visible to their owner and read-only. Copies and moves under one are refused, and so are two-way connections to one.
7. With `scopedTypes` empty or `'*'`, every top-level row of a type that isn't isolated or shared is unassigned. A module that keeps top-level rows of a scoped type and writes to them in-process without `isSystem` now gets the `400`.
8. With `keepContents`, a kept row of a scoped type that would be in no section is parked at the top level whatever `isRoot` says. A kept row that would take the name of a live record under the parent is a `400`.
9. An assign files the linked unassigned rows with it, and a linked child stays under its parent. An assign out of a deleted section removes the references to and from the rows left in Deleted, so a Matter restored afterwards comes back without that person, and a required reference field can come back blank (a restore doesn't check required fields).
10. A token through a multiple reference reads every entry: a list in a formula, and in a template joined with ", ", blank items dropped. A Matter-style name built from `{other_parents->name}` now lists all the other parents, not one.
11. A formula reads another formula's value from the same write, whatever the field order.
12. `{stage}`, `{status}` and `{reference}` read the entry itself.
13. A trailing `|word` inside a token is a format.
14. The secret guard also refuses a part or a format of a secret, such as `{code|year}`.
15. `NodeListPage` sends no `pathPrefix` under an enforced space.
16. In Build it, a formula field's hand-written `join(…, ', ')` or `concat()` around one item opens in Advanced.
17. `resolveFormulaToken` and `resolveFormulaTemplate`, deep imports from `utils/formula`, take a node read and `{ host?, labels? }`. The field-level `FormulaTokenDeref` is removed.

**Changes to the request as first written**
- **Archive, delete, restore, favourite and pin** stay open on an unassigned row, not just delete and archive.
- **A one-way link to an unassigned row is allowed.** The request named connections from it; a two-way type counts both ends.
- **The namespaced metadata write** (`updateNodeMetadata`) is refused on an unassigned row for a request only, so a module's in-process metadata still goes through.
- **A kept row that has nowhere else to go is parked, unassigned,** rather than refusing the delete, when its type is scoped and it would be in no section.
- **The impact also says `sectionRoot`,** so the dialog knows which wording to use.

**Not in this release.** Updating the existing entries when a computed definition changes. Until then, a changed template or formula applies to new entries, and to an existing entry when it is next saved or recomputed. See the [Roadmap](/modules/structure/roadmap/#now--next).

**Tests.** The new tests cover the report reproduced on a Matter config, subtype scoping, unassigned reads, writes and assigns, the delete impact and keep, slugs and the migration, and Build it's parts, writing and reading back. There are 572 in all, 110 more than v1.42.4.

Frontend and backend. **Schema change:** `structure_node`'s `@@unique([userId, slug])` and `@@unique([userId, parentId, name])` become plain `@@index`es, and the new module migration `1.43.0__live-unique-keys.sql` builds the live-only partial unique indexes. It turns blank slugs into `NULL` and gives any live duplicates written while no key held them a free suffix first. Hosts need `npx structure update`. See [Module SQL migrations](/modules/structure/configuration/#module-sql-migrations).
