## v1.42.4

**Entries created from a form can be read straight away** *(generated-output)*

- **The bug.** People created from a new Matter's form couldn't be read and didn't show in their list. Person is scoped to the Matter's space, and a brand-new Matter wasn't the active space until it was picked in the header.
- **The fix.** Opening a top-level record of an isolated type (a Matter) now makes it the active space, and creating one opens its page.
- **Computed names in Create new.** When the new entry's type computes its name (a merge template or formula), Create new asks for the fields the name is built from instead of a Name box. The entry shows its preview, or "New Person", followed by "(new)". The server works out the name.
- **The host's computed fields see the new entries in the same save.** A Matter named from `{focus_parent->name}` gets the new people's names, not their ids.

462 tests. No schema change. Type-checked against veradai: 0 errors.

---

## v1.42.3

**Create form fixes** *(generated-output)*

- **The form says why it didn't save.** A refused create or save, from the server or from the form's own checks, shows a red alert by the buttons and a toast. The field it names is highlighted and scrolled into view. Before, a server refusal was never awaited, so nothing showed in the sheet.
- **New entries become children.** An entry created from another entry's form is created as that entry's child, so a new Matter's new Person sits under the Matter and inside its space. If the type can't be a child there, it falls back to a top-level entry in the same space. If neither is allowed, the error names the hierarchy rule to change.
- **Required fields when creating a new entry.** Create new asks for the new entry's required fields as well as its name, and sends them with it (`{ $new: { nodeType, name, data } }`).
- **Several new entries in one field** now add to the list instead of replacing each other.
- **Templates.** A `{ref->name}` template that reads a not-yet-saved new entry shows its name.
- **The create and edit sheet** is a third of the screen wide on desktop, at least 28rem, and full width on small screens.

454 tests. No schema change. Type-checked against veradai: 0 errors.

---

## v1.42.2

**Create a referenced entry from the form** *(generated-output)*

- **Allow creating new entries here** (`nodeReferenceConfig.allowCreate`, with optional `createTypes`) gives a reference field's picker a create option. The new entry shows as "Jane Smith (new)" and is created when the form is saved, in the same save as the entry it belongs to. If the save fails, nothing is created.
- **Placement.** The new entry goes inside the host's isolation section: a new Matter's new Person lands in that Matter. That keeps the wall intact.
- **Checks.** It counts as filled for a required field, and `syncTargetField`, the filter and the type rules all apply.
- **Where it works.** Top-level fields, multiple lists and field-group sub-fields. Not `bulkCreateNodes`.
- **Over the API,** the value is `{ "$new": { "nodeType", "name" } }`. An object with a `$new` key anywhere else is refused (400).

9 new tests; 449 in all. No schema change.

---

## v1.42.1

**Reference write-back, excluded types and date formulas** *(generated-output)*

Follow-ups from veradai's Matter design, now that Child is its own node type (a subtype of Person).

- **`nodeReferenceConfig.syncTargetField`.** Adding an entry to the field sets a boolean field on that entry to `true`, and removing it sets it back to `false`, unless another entry still references it through a field with the same setting. It runs on the server in the same save, and covers entries created from the picker. The target field should be `editableBy: 'system'`, so a direct edit is refused (400). Use: the Matter's `focus_children` sets `is_focus_child`. Deleting the host clears its flags, archiving keeps them, restoring sets them again, and duplicating sets them for the copies.
- **`nodeReferenceConfig.excludeTypes`.** It leaves a type and its subtypes out of a reference field, in the picker and on the server. Use: `other_parents` with `allowedTypes: ['person']` and `excludeTypes: ['child']`. The field editor has an **Excluded node types** control.
- **Date formulas.** These are new: `today()`, `age(date)`, `yearsBetween`, `monthsBetween` and `daysBetween`. A new **Work it out when shown** setting (`calcConfig.evaluate: 'onRead'`, automatic for formulas that use `today()` or `age()`) keeps the value out of storage. The server works it out on reads and the app when it shows it, so an age never goes stale. A list can't sort or filter by such a field.
- **Confirmed, no change needed.**
  - A subtype hides an inherited field by declaring its own field with the same id and **Hidden** on. The parent is unaffected. The subtype's copy doesn't follow later edits to the parent's field.
  - Connection fields support every field type, including `address`, `email`, `phone`, `select`, `boolean`, `date` and `node-reference`. A connection's node-reference value now shows entry names instead of ids in the connection details.
- **Pickers.** A reference field's picker now also lists subtypes of its allowed types, matching what the server already accepted.

**Tests.** 41 new tests cover the write-back, excluded types, connection field types and the date functions. There are 440 in all.

Frontend and backend, no schema change. Structure's files type-check against a scratch copy of veradai with 0 errors in both halves.

---

## v1.42.0

**Matter P1: a hard wall around isolation sections, reference filters, and formulas that stay current** *(generated-output)*

The first three items of veradai's Matter request, built as generic config that any app can use. **Hosts need `npx structure update` (new column).**

**1. The hard wall around isolation sections.** Each top-level record of an isolated type (`primaryScope.isPrimary` with `isolation: 'enforced'`) is a section. Nothing in one section may point at, or link to, anything in another. Shared/global types are the exception: they belong to every section, and a shared row is in no section wherever it is filed.
- **References.** Every id in a `node-reference` field, field-group sub-fields included, must name an entry in the host's section or of a shared type. Ids already stored are checked as well as new ones. A breach is a `400` naming the field.
- **Connections.** If either end is in a section, the other must be in the same section or of a shared type.
- **Two-way.** Once any type is isolated, an entry in no section may reference, or link to, only entries in no section or of a shared type.
- **Narrowing the shared types.**
  - `nodeReferenceConfig.allowGlobalTypes` on a field;
  - `allowGlobalTypes` on a connection type, stored in the new `isolationConfig` column;
  - absent or `'*'` means every shared type, and `[]` means none;
  - in the admin forms, **Global types it may point at**, shown once a type is isolated.
- **Write paths checked:** create, bulk create, update, the public API, duplicate (references to copied rows are re-pointed at the copies), move (with link re-stamping), restore, connection create, update and promote, and `bulkCreateConnections`. Merge already refused two sections.
- **Who it binds.** Request writes and in-process module writes. System callers (`isSystem: true`) are exempt.
- **Reads.**
  - `listNodes` takes `withinSectionOf`, plus `sectionGlobalTypes`, for pickers;
  - `getNodeSection` and `GET /nodes/:id/section` report a node's section;
  - node-reference pickers (field-group sub-fields included) and the Add Connection dialog's picker are confined;
  - formula traversal and `{ref->field}` read only the host's section plus the shared types.

See [The hard wall around sections](/modules/structure/configuration/#the-hard-wall-around-sections-v1420) and [The Section Wall](/modules/structure/integration/#the-section-wall-v1420).

**2. `nodeReferenceConfig.filter`.** A condition on the **target** entry, in the `visibleWhen` grammar, for example `{ "field": "person_type", "equals": "child" }`. The picker shows only matching entries, and the server refuses a write that names one that doesn't match. Both halves run one condition evaluator, generated from one template. In the admin field editor it is **Only entries where…**. See [node-reference](/modules/structure/fields/#node-reference).

**3. Formulas stay current.**
- **One evaluator.** `utils/formula.ts` is now one template, rendered into both the frontend and the backend.
- **On every write** the server computes the node's own formula fields, and its computed built-in `name`, `description` and `slug`. A computed name or slug that clashes with another entry keeps the value the write carried.
- **After a write** the server recomputes the formulas that can see the changed node, through `children`, `parents`, `scope`, `connections` and references.
  - It goes at most 3 levels deep and recomputes at most 200 dependants per write.
  - Bulk archive, delete and restore are batched, so each dependant is recomputed once.
  - The recompute's `structure.node.updated` carries `source: 'formula'`.
- **The formula language** gains list literals (`[a, b]`) and `highest(values, order[, fallback])`. Node-views already carried `stage` and `status`, and `filter(list, 'status', 'active')` already worked, so the Matter's `court_involvement` is one formula:

  ```
  highest(pluck(filter(children('court_proceeding'), 'status', 'active'), 'stage'), ['none', 'concluded', 'considering', 'preparing', 'active'], 'none')
  ```
- **The form leaves out admin-only types** in its formula and computed-value reads, so it agrees with the server.

See [formula](/modules/structure/fields/#formula) and [Formula Recompute](/modules/structure/integration/#formula-recompute-v1420).

**Behaviour that changes**
- **In an app with an isolated type:**
  - a reference or connection that crosses a section is a `400` for request writes and non-system in-process writes, including ids already stored in the field;
  - an entry in no section can no longer reference, or be linked to, an entry inside a section;
  - a non-system move that changes a subtree's section is refused if it would leave a reference or link crossing a section; when it goes ahead, the links touching the moved rows are re-stamped, for every caller;
  - a restore is refused when the entry's references would cross a section, and a crossing link stays in Deleted;
  - reference pickers and the Add Connection dialog list only what the host may point at.
- **In every app:**
  - the server replaces a submitted formula value, and a computed built-in `name`, `description` or `slug`, with its own result on every write;
  - formula values are recomputed after writes, and each recompute fires `structure.node.updated` with `source: 'formula'`;
  - a field with a `filter` refuses a target that doesn't match, for request writes and non-system in-process writes;
  - a formula that calls a function the server doesn't have, such as `semanticSimilarity`, keeps the value the form wrote.
- `bulkCreateConnections` passes `isSystem` and `scopeRootId` through to each row.

With no isolated type and none of the new config, formula recompute is the only change.

**Changes to the request as first written**
- **No `scopeTo` key.** The wall comes from making the Matter an isolated type, the request's "space root" option.
- **`allowGlobalTypes` left out means every shared type.** `[]` means none.
- **Stage and status needed nothing new.** Node-views already carried them, and the three-argument `filter` already existed.
- **`nodes()` hydrates reference ids;** it doesn't list every node of a type. Its dependants are the nodes whose reference fields point at the changed node.

**Tests.** 114 new tests cover the wall on every write path, the pickers and reads, the filter, the server formula engine and its limits, and the `court_involvement` case. There are 399 in all.

Frontend and backend. **Schema change:** a new `structure_connection_type.isolationConfig` column (`Json`, default `{}`). Hosts need `npx structure update` (new column). Host type-check against a scratch copy of veradai with the new column: 0 errors frontend and backend.

---

## v1.41.1

**The name field: type a full name, sort by surname** *(generated-output)*

**Entry.** A `name-field` now has a **Full name** box with the parts underneath: Title, First name, Middle name(s), Surname and Suffix.
- Typing "Dr Jane Mary Anne Smith" fills the boxes. They stay editable, and editing one updates the full name.
- **How a name splits:**
  - "Smith, Jane" reads as surname first;
  - a known title at the start becomes the Title;
  - suffixes such as Jr, III or PhD become the Suffix, when the suffix box is shown;
  - surname particles stay with the surname ("van Beethoven");
  - every word between the first name and the surname is a middle name.
- The Title is a list. It defaults to Mr, Mrs, Ms, Miss, Mx, Dr and Prof, with a **None** choice.
- The stored value keeps its shape (`prefix`, `first`, `middle`, `last`, `suffix`), so existing names carry over. See [`name-field`](/modules/structure/fields/#name-field).

**Admin switches.** The field editor now has:
- **Show title**, **Show middle names** and **Show suffix**;
- **Type any title**, for free text instead of a list;
- the **Titles** list;
- a new **Display order** setting (`nameConfig.displayOrder`, `'given-first'` or `'surname-first'`).

**Shown properly everywhere.**
- Cards, list rows, the table, connection badges, merge-field templates and formulas now show "Dr Jane Smith" instead of raw data.
- With surname first, lists show "Smith, Jane".
- Addresses show on one line in the same places.
- Cards and list rows now mask secret fields, as the table already did.

**Sorting and search**
- A list sorted by a name field sorts by surname, then first name, then middle names, ignoring case. Blank names sort last.
- Keyword search already found every part of a name.
- **List filters** on a name field now match its parts: "contains" matches the whole name, and "equals", "starts with" and "in" match any part. Before, they compared the stored object as text.

**Saving.** A request that saves a name field as anything other than an object of text parts gets a 400. A module writing in-process gets a warning, and stored values aren't re-checked.

**Tests.** 47 new tests cover the name splitting, the display formats, sorting, search, filters and the save check. There are 285 in all.

Frontend and backend, no schema change and no data migration. Host type-check against veradai: 0 errors frontend and backend.

---

## v1.41.0

**Config import and export move to the starter's ConfigTransfer** *(generated-output)*

Structure's admin import and export now run on the starter's shared [ConfigTransfer](/starter-template/config-transfer/), like every other module's. **The minimum starter is now v2.16.0.** See [JSON Import & Export](/modules/structure/json-reference/) for the details.

**What an admin sees**
- At the top right of **Admin → App Structure**, the starter's **Export** and **Import** buttons replace Import Config / Export Config.
- Import opens the step-by-step dialog:
  - drop, browse or paste a file;
  - the file is checked in the browser and on the server;
  - a review lists every item with what will happen to it, and you can leave items out;
  - **Add new only** is a new choice, next to Merge and Replace;
  - before a Replace removes anything, you can download a backup and must confirm;
  - one all-or-nothing import, then a report.
- The field editor's **Import Fields** is unchanged.

**Endpoints.**
- The config routes are now `GET /api/v1/structure/admin/config/`, `GET …/export` and `POST …/import`. They are admin-only, and a real import asks for the step-up code.
- The old `GET /types/export`, `POST /types/import`, `GET /config/export` and `POST /config/import` are **removed**, with no aliases.
- Also removed:
  - the service methods `exportConfigBundle`, `importConfigBundle` and `exportNodeTypes`;
  - the frontend service methods and hooks for them (`useImportConfigBundle`, `useImportNodeTypes`);
  - the `stagesKeptLine` helper.
- The in-process **`importNodeTypes`**, which modules use to seed their node types, is unchanged.

**The file**
- An export is a ConfigTransfer file (`format`, `moduleId: 'structure'`, `version: '2'`, `counts`, `sections`) with five sections: `fieldTemplates`, `groups`, `nodeTypes`, `connectionTypes` and `functions`.
- Types list parents before their subtypes.
- An emptied display value is written as `null`.
- Attached field templates are named by slug.
- **Older files still import.** A version 1.0 file is upgraded on the way in, so it behaves exactly as before:
  - an absent display key clears;
  - template ids map to slugs;
  - `isUnique` becomes `unique`;
  - types are ordered parents first.
- **In a version 2 file,** a key you leave out is kept and an explicit `null` clears. `null` is now accepted on the display keys of field templates, groups, connection types and functions.

**Behaviour that changes**
- **Replace no longer refuses the whole import.** It keeps, with a reason:
  - a type that still has entries;
  - a type others inherit from;
  - built-in types;
  - app-level groups, connection types and functions.

  It applies the rest.
- **A bad item** is listed as a problem and skipped, and the rest still imports. Before, one bad item refused the whole file.
- **Unchanged items aren't rewritten.**
- **A version 2 file must list a parent type before its subtypes.** An export always does.
- **A connection-type update** now writes `moduleId` when the file carries it, as node types already did.
- **Review notes** flag:
  - a type that keeps a running module's stage set;
  - a sharing switch;
  - a field template the import can't attach;
  - a linked component template that isn't one of yours here, so the saved look is kept.
- **Field-template links.** A type whose field template wasn't in an earlier import gets it attached by the next import that brings the template in.
- **Time limit.** An import runs for at most 45 seconds, so it finishes, or fails with nothing changed, inside the dialog's 60-second limit. A very large file (around 1,000 new node types with computed fields) has to be split.
- **Events.**
  - `structure.config.imported` fires only after a real import. Its `mode` can be `'add'`, and it still carries `stagesKept`.
  - The starter records `config.exported` and `config.imported` in the audit trail.

**Tests.** The import/export tests now run through the ConfigTransfer engine, and 27 new tests cover:
- the round trip;
- the three strategies and each Kept reason;
- stage sources;
- the secret guard;
- field-template links;
- version 1.0 files;
- the hook payloads;
- `importNodeTypes` staying unchanged.

There are 238 in all.

Frontend and backend, no schema change and no data migration. Host type-check against veradai: 0 errors frontend and backend.

---

## v1.40.1

**Fixes from the 2026-10-29 docs check** *(generated-output)*

Checking the JSON Import & Export and Field Types pages against the code turned up seven places where the code itself was wrong or half-finished. All seven are fixed in this release.

**Config imports.** Field templates, groups and functions now update the way node types and connection types already did.
- A key an import row leaves out keeps its stored value: a field template's `fields` and `sortOrder`, a group's `sortOrder`, and a function's `params`.
- The nullable display keys that an export leaves out when they are empty are still cleared when absent, so a value cleared in the source environment travels. These are a field template's `description`, a group's `icon` and `color`, and a function's `description` and `category`.
- Before, every omitted key went back to its empty value or default.

**Component templates no longer get baked into exports.**
- An export now writes each type's own stored `templateOverrides`, and adds `templateSnapshot`: the linked component templates' settings, per slot (`card`, `listItem`, `detailHeader`, `overview`).
- On import, a slot whose component template exists here keeps the live link and stores only the type's own overrides. A slot whose template is missing gets the snapshot merged in, so the type keeps its look. `templateSnapshot` itself is never stored.
- Files exported before v1.40.1 have no snapshot and import as before.
- The effect is that re-importing into the same environment no longer turns the template's settings into the type's own, so later edits to the template show through again.

**Field editor and forms**
- **Width picker.** It shows **Half** when a field has no width set, matching how the detail page lays it out.
- **Defaults on new entries.** The node create form now starts each field with its `defaultValue`, which is what the toggle's "Pre-checked on new entries" label always promised.
  - The form is never pre-filled on an edit or a duplicate, and never over a value the form opened with.
  - A field the form may not show (`isHidden` or `visibleWhen`) isn't pre-filled either.
  - Locked fields are still filled by the server.
  - Creates through the API or in-process are unchanged.
  - The field editor no longer offers a default for types where one can't apply: formula, address, name, chained select, and computed fields.
- **`helpText`** now shows under the input on forms, including the Add Connection dialog. It isn't repeated when it's the same as the description.

**Read-only view**
- A node reference shows the linked entries' names, with a link where the type is known. It falls back to the id while loading or when an entry can't be read.
- Toggles show **Yes** or **No**.
- Addresses and names show formatted, rather than as raw data.

**Types.** `listPage.defaultView` includes `'tree'` on both halves. It already worked at runtime.

**Tests.** 52 new tests: config imports and the export round-trip on the generated backend, and the defaults and display helpers. There are 211 in all.

Frontend and backend, no schema change and no data migration. Host type-check against veradai: 0 errors frontend and backend.

---

## v1.40.0

**Stage sources: another module can supply a node type's stages** *(generated-output)*

A module can now register as a **stage source** and write a node type's stage set through Structure, with the source recorded. The slot is generic, so any module can use it. Structure stores the set in the type's own `stageConfig`, keeps its own type editor and imports from changing it while the module runs, shows admins where the stages come from, and goes on enforcing it everywhere it already enforces stages. Structure never remaps stored stages: moving entries between stages is the source module's job. See [Stage Sources](/modules/structure/integration/#stage-sources-v1400) for the contract and [Sourced stage sets](/modules/structure/configuration/#sourced-stage-sets-v1400) for what admins see.

- **Types.** `Stage.ref`, `StageConfig.source` (a `StageSource`, `{ moduleId, name, ref?, version? }`) and, on responses only, `NodeTypeConfig.stageSource` (a `NodeTypeStageSource`, which adds `live` and `manageHref`), on both halves. The backend also exports `StageSourceRegistration`, `StageSourceInput`, `StageCaller` and `StageUsage`.
- **A backend registry,** `stageSourceRegistry` (`config/stage-source.registry.ts`, built on the starter's `BaseRegistry`). Modules don't import it; they call `structureService.stages.registerSource` from their init.
- **`structureService.stages`**, in-process only with no HTTP routes: `registerSource`, `unregisterSource`, `registerTypeStages`, `releaseTypeStages` and `getStageUsage`. The three type methods choose the row by `ownerId`. `null` is the app-level row and needs an admin or the system; a person's id is that person's own row and needs that person or the system. They run under a per-slug advisory lock. `registerTypeStages` shape-checks the set and takes an optional compare-and-set, `expected`.
- **Protection.** While a source is live, a type update that carries a different set is refused with a `409`, an equal set is dropped, and `features.stages` stays on. Both imports keep the stored set and list the type in a new `stagesKept` field, on the result and on the `structure.nodeType.imported` and `structure.config.imported` payloads. Every other write drops an incoming `source`. Once a source stops, a changed set becomes the type's own.
- **Export** leaves `source` out and keeps each stage's `ref`.
- **The type editor** shows a live sourced set read-only, with the Stages switch on and disabled, a line naming the source and a link to where it is managed, and leaves `stageConfig` out of the save. A set whose source isn't running stays editable, with a line saying so. The import dialog says which types' stages were kept.

**Changes to the contract as first written** (all additive or stricter):

- The caller type is exported as `StageCaller` rather than `Caller`, with the same shape.
- `createNodeType(userId, data, { appLevel: true })` also needs `isAdmin: true` or `isSystem: true` in the same options, or it is a `403`. Otherwise the call carries no caller tier, and the service has no role lookup.
- While a source is live, `stageSource.name` and the `409` messages use the name the module is registered with now. The name stored in `stageConfig.source` stays as written, and is the one used once the source stops.
- `expected` compares only the stored source's `ref` and `version`, whichever module wrote it. `null` expects no stored source. A `ref` or `version` left out of `expected` matches only a stored source that has none.
- The `stages.*` audit lines also carry `ownerId` and `appLevel`. The `structure.nodeType.updated` payload stays `{ userId, slug, changes }`.

**Two list options** *(generated-output)*

- **`withoutStage: 'include' | 'only'`** on `listNodes` and `GET /nodes`. `'only'` lists the entries with no stage, and is a `400` beside `stage`, `stages` or `stageGroup`. `'include'` adds them to whichever stage filter is set.
- **`ownerOnly: true`** reads a shared type only for the caller's own entries.
- **Fix: a stage filter over `dataFilters` no longer misses entries.** The stage filter, `withoutStage` and `ownerOnly` now apply inside the data-filter scan, before its 5,000-row cap rather than after it. Results are identical wherever the cap wasn't reached.

**App-level types from the New type form** *(generated-output)*

Admins get a new choice in the form's Scope section, "Everyone, each with their own entries". It creates an app-level type that isn't shared: every user can use it, and each user's entries stay their own. Before this, a type built in the form belonged to the admin who built it unless it was shared, so nobody else could use it. `POST /types` takes `appLevel: true` in its body, for admins only, and `createNodeType` takes it in its options (see above). Another user must not already own a type with that slug.

**For apps with nothing registered** the behaviour is the same as v1.39.0. A type update carrying `stageConfig` or `features` (every editor save does) now takes a short advisory lock and one extra read, and an import takes one lock per imported type. With the new list options left out, the list SQL is unchanged, except that a list combining `dataFilters` with a stage filter applies the stage filter inside the scan (the fix above).

**Docs corrections.** Configuration said `stageConfig` "requires `features.stages`", and the JSON reference that `defaultStage` is required. Neither is so. Stages and move rules are checked whenever they are configured, `features.stages` only adds the default stage at create and the editor's Stages section, and only a sourced set is shape-checked.

**Tests.** 93 new integration tests cover the slot, the list options and the type-editor helpers, including a regression block that runs existing paths with nothing registered. There are 159 in all.

Frontend and backend, no schema change and no data migration. The minimum starter version is unchanged (v2.13.0). Host type-check against veradai: 0 errors frontend and backend.

---

## v1.39.0

**Isolation sections for in-process consumers** *(generated-output)*

The cognitive-db engine asked for a set of options (S1–S6 below) so it can write into and read from a chosen isolation section, the part of the data under one top-level record of an isolated node type. All of them are additive and for system callers only (`isSystem: true`). A request caller that passes one gets `BadRequest`, and a caller that passes none sees no change. See [Isolation Sections](/modules/structure/integration/#isolation-sections-v1390) for the full contract.

- **S1, labelled records.** `createNode` and `bulkCreateNodes` take `scopeRootId`, which labels a top-level record with a section without filing it in the folder tree. Labelled records:
  - count as inside the section for lists, `getNode`, searches and `getUsage`, but stay out of `getTree`;
  - are deleted and purged with their section root;
  - keep their label through moves, merges, duplicates and migrations.
- **S2, labelled links.** `createConnection` takes `scopeRootId` (a section id, or `null` for no section) and refuses an endpoint in a different section unless it is of a shared/global type. For `isUnique` connection types, uniqueness is checked within each section, and `promoteConnection` applies the same rule. The section is recorded in `metadata.isolationSection`.
- **S3.** Every `NodeConnection` read now returns the link's `rootNodeId`.
- **S4.** `expandGraph` takes `scopeRootId`: a section id walks only that section's links, and `null` walks outside every section.
- **S5.** New `getIsolationModel(userId)` returns `{ isolatedTypes, globalTypes, sectionRootIds }`.
- **S6.** `scopeRootId: null` on `listNodes`, `semanticSearchNodes` and `findSimilarNodes` returns rows outside every section.
- **Unique and max-limited connection types** are now re-checked under a transaction lock when a link is created or promoted, so concurrent creates can no longer slip past `isUnique` or `maxConnections`.

**Fix: moving an entry no longer damages the paths of the entries under it** *(generated-output)*

On Prisma 7 with the pg driver adapter, moving an entry that had children (or merging one, which re-parents its children) could write those children's stored path as empty or wrong. They then dropped out of path-based reads and deletes. The move now writes correct paths. A new migration, `1.39.0__descendant-path-repair.sql`, rebuilds the path, depth and root of any damaged rows from their parent chain when you run `npx structure update`. It only touches rows whose path doesn't match, and it is safe to run more than once.

**Other changes for callers that pass no new options:**

- **`getUsage` link counts.** For a section root, `getUsage` counts links by their source entry, as it did in v1.38.2.
- **Duplicates.** Copied links are stamped with the copy's section instead of none.
- **Lists with `includeChildren`.** The children follow the list's section filter.
- **Deleting a section root.** This also deletes the links labelled with it, but only those whose endpoints are both deleted or of a shared/global type.
- **Moving a section root.** Its labelled rows and links follow it to the new root.

**Tests.** The repo now has an `npm test` integration harness that renders the generated backend and runs it against an in-memory Postgres (PGlite with Prisma 7). There are 66 tests, including a check that every existing call behaves as before when the new options are left out. The harness is generic and is meant to move to the module starter.

Backend, plus `rootNodeId` on the frontend `NodeConnection` type. One data migration, no schema change. Host type-check against veradai: 0 errors frontend and backend.

---

## v1.38.2

**Storage usage** *(generated-output)*

- **`getUsage(userId, { nodeId? })`** on the structure service, and **`GET /api/v1/structure/usage`** (`?nodeId=` for one entry and everything under it), report the space a user's Structure data takes: entries and deleted entries (count and text size), connections, and on embeddings installs the text prepared for search, search vectors and text chunks. Sizes are the stored text in bytes; vector sizes are estimated (count × dimensions × 4). Figures not available on an install are `null`.
- **A Storage section** on the Structure settings page shows the total and each line.
- The response shape (`StructureUsage`, built from `ModuleUsageItem` lines) is meant to be mirrored by other modules — uploaded files belong to Document Management — so a combined figure can be added up elsewhere.

Frontend and backend, no schema change. Host type-check against veradai: 0 errors frontend and backend.

---

## v1.38.1

**Follow-ups from the cognitive-db engine: space scoping, connection rows, links about links** *(generated-output)*

- **A system caller's explicit space keeps shared types.** `listNodes` with `isSystem` and `scopeRootId` used a plain space match, which dropped shared/global types. It now keeps them, as `semanticSearchNodes` and `findSimilarNodes` already did.
- **Connections belong to their space.** `createConnection` stamps the connection row's `rootNodeId` from its endpoints, so under enforced spaces the owner still sees the edge in `getNode` and space-confined lists. Existing rows have no space; an app using enforced spaces can backfill them with one SQL statement that copies `rootNodeId` from each connection's `fromNodeId` endpoint (run it against your own database, after a backup).
- **Untyped lists leave out connection rows.** A `listNodes` call with no `type` or `types` no longer returns the edges (named "A -> B"), which flooded name and keyword searches for well-connected entries. Pass `includeConnectionRows: true` to get them back.
- **Links about links.** The connection-type editor can choose "A connection" as an endpoint type. Deleting a node, merging it away or deleting a connection now also sweeps the links about each swept link, up to four levels deep, and `expandGraph` never returns a connection row as a reached node. See [Integration › Connection Lifecycle Methods](/modules/structure/integration/#connection-lifecycle-methods-v1160).
- **Merge ids for undo.** `structure.node.merged` now carries the re-parented, re-pointed and dropped ids (capped at 1,000 each, with `idsTruncated`).
- **Documented:** `createConnectionType` defaults `bidirectional` to `true` (unchanged).

Frontend and backend, no schema change. Host type-check against veradai: 0 errors frontend and backend.

---

## v1.38.0

**Cleanup & security audit fixes — API keys, secrets, consistent write rules, data lifecycle** *(generated-output)*

Fixes the findings from the 2026-09-30 cleanup and security audit (no critical findings; the moderate and minor ones were listed on the [Roadmap](/modules/structure/roadmap/)). The fixes landed in batches, each reviewed adversarially, and the release was then reviewed again as a whole. Configuration ownership stays **per-admin** (a recorded decision): node types can be app-wide, while field templates, connection types, functions, groups and display templates stay owned by the admin who created them. Only the knock-on bugs of that model were fixed.

<Aside type="caution" title="Before you update">

**Requires starter template v2.13.0 or newer.** The generated code now imports the starter's web-session and step-up middleware, its personal-data export registry and `getApiErrorMessage`, so on an older starter the app fails to build. Run `npm run template:update` in the app first, then `npx structure update`.

Behaviour changes that can affect existing apps or integrations:

- **API keys.** Listing, creating and revoking keys needs a signed-in web session; a device token is refused. A new **write-scoped** key always expires: with no expiry it gets 365 days, and an expiry further out is refused (400). Read-only keys can still be non-expiring. Existing keys are unchanged.
- **Step-up confirmation** ("Confirm it's you", at the starter's hardened and strict security tiers; a pass-through at the standard tier) is now asked for when creating an API key, running a real config or node-type import (a config-import dry run is exempt), deleting a node type, connection type, field template or function, and permanently deleting entries. The starter's API client shows the prompt and retries; a script calling these routes must handle the step-up response.
- **The list status filter `all` no longer includes deleted entries.** Ask for `status: 'deleted'` explicitly; it lists only your own deleted entries.
- **Unsafe validation patterns.** A `validation.pattern` whose matching time can grow sharply with the length of the value is refused when it is saved (400 naming the field). A pattern already stored is logged and **not run**, so values pass without it until an admin replaces it. A value too long to test safely against its pattern is refused on request writes.
- **Field locks (`editableBy`) now apply on create, bulk create, duplicate and connection writes**, not only on update, for request callers. In-process module writes are unaffected. Computed and formula fields are exempt, and an empty locked field is given its configured default.
- **Connection field values are validated on request writes** (required, type, pattern, options), like node data. A connection `PATCH` replaces the whole data record, so it is validated as a whole.
- **Stricter value shapes on request writes.** A node-reference value must be a node id, and, when the field sets `allowedTypes`, an entry of an allowed type the caller can read. A `secret` value must be text. A field-group value must be a list of rows. Each is a 400; in-process module writes are coerced instead, with a logged warning.
- **HTTP creates need an enabled node-type row.** A type registered only in frontend code, or disabled, can no longer take new entries over HTTP; seed a database row for code-registered types. Connections can no longer be created or edited through the nodes API; use the connections API.
- **Config import.** Slugs must be lowercase letters, digits, hyphens or underscores (at most 100 characters), and each list holds at most 1,000 items. `POST /types/import` refuses a full config bundle (use `/config/import`). A replace-mode import refuses to remove an app-wide node type that any user still has live entries of, and an import cannot create an app-wide type whose slug another user already owns.
- **Smaller contract changes.** The favourite and pin toggles return only `{ id, isFavorite, isPinned }`. The `stageGroup` list filter now filters, and needs `type`. Restoring a deleted entry needs the type's delete permission. On a type with stage transitions, a non-admin cannot clear an entry's stage, and a non-admin's new entry can only start in the type's default stage (when it sets one). Public API create and update on a read-restricted type answer `{ id }` only. On a type with several parents that declare the same field id at different depths, the definition that wins can change: sealing and validation now resolve fields in the same order as the form.

</Aside>

**API keys and sensitive routes**

- **Key management needs a web session, and creating a key needs step-up.** A lower-trust device token could previously mint a long-lived key that kept working after the device was revoked.
- **Write-scoped keys expire.** The create dialog gets an **Expires** choice (30, 90 or 365 days, defaulting to 90, plus Never for read-only keys only), and the key list shows when each key expires.
- **Key lifecycle events.** Creating and revoking a key fires `structure.apiKey.created` / `structure.apiKey.revoked` (never the key or its hash), both declared as audit events, and the creation log records the scopes and expiry granted.
- **Sensitive routes.** Step-up on imports and on the config hard deletes listed above. `POST /nodes/bulk-delete` needs a web session, and the new permanent delete needs a web session plus step-up.

**Secrets**

- **The v1.37.3 secret guard closes its gaps.** A node-reference field with no `allowedTypes` (the default, meaning any type) now counts the secret fields of every type the user can read. The guard reads the type's resolved field set (own fields, attached field templates, inherited fields) plus built-in field configs; a field group holding a secret counts as secret; and a referenced secret field named `name`, `description` or `slug` is caught. It runs on node-type, field-template and connection-type saves and imports, a field-template save re-checks every type that attaches the template, and a change re-checks the types that depend on it (subtypes, and types whose computed fields reach a field that has just become secret). The client check in the type form agrees with the server.
- **Formulas and merge templates never see another entry's secret values.** A read through `nodes()` or a reference token is stripped of secret values before evaluation, failing closed while the entry's type is not loaded. This also closes the dynamic `pluck` residual noted in v1.37.3.
- **Secret values must be text.** A number, list or object sent to a `secret` field was stored unencrypted.
- **No filtering or sorting by secret fields.** A list filter or sort on a secret field, or on a field group holding one, is refused (400), and the list UI no longer offers those columns.
- **Connection hooks carry no secret values**, matching the node hooks.
- **The read-only view of a field group masks secret sub-fields.** It printed the rows as raw JSON.
- **An undecryptable secret survives a save.** It comes back to the owner as a marker instead of an empty string, keeps its stored value when the record is saved back, and shows as "cannot be decrypted" with a prompt to enter a new value. Previously the next save overwrote it with an empty value.
- **A missing `SECURITY_ENCRYPTION_KEY` answers 503 (`encryption_key_missing`)** instead of an opaque 500. Structure encrypts secret fields at every security tier, so the key is needed whenever a type uses a secret field.

**Consistent write rules**

- **Single and bulk create share one path.** Bulk-imported rows now get the `beforeCreate` filter and the type's default stage, and keep `stage`, `classification` and `observedAt`, which were dropped.
- **Field locks on every write path.** Only a change to a locked value is refused (on create: absent, empty or the field's default). The node form and the Add Connection dialog show locked fields read-only, and a required locked field with no default explains why that connection type cannot be added there.
- **Duplicate validates its overrides**, and connection writes validate their data and the targets of new node references.
- **Stage rules hold.** A supplied stage must be one of the type's configured stages, and with a transition map a non-admin can no longer skip it by creating into a later stage or clearing the stage first.
- **Deleting an entry checks its whole subtree.** If the subtree holds entries of a type the caller may not delete, the delete is refused, naming that type.
- **Side routes respect the type permissions.** Favourite and pin respect the read permission and return only the flags; `PATCH /nodes/:id/metadata` checks the edit permission for request callers; `GET /connections/:id` hides deleted connections and those with an endpoint the caller cannot read; updating or promoting a connection blanks an endpoint of a read-restricted type for a non-admin.
- **One field order everywhere.** Sealing, validation, locks and the public strip resolve a multi-parent type's fields in the same order as the form.

**Per-admin config knock-ons**

- **Field templates resolve for every user.** A template attached to a type every user sees now applies for everyone, so its fields render and their sealing, locks and validation hold. Creating, listing and editing a template stays with its owner.
- **Type deletes count every user's entries.** Deleting an app-wide node type or connection type, or removing an app-wide node type in a replace-mode import, is refused while any user has live entries of it, not just the deleting admin.
- **Admins review every user's proposals.** The proposal list carries `id` and `proposedBy`; approve and reject take an optional `proposalId`, and answer 409 when more than one pending proposal uses the slug. The review hooks carry the proposer as `userId` and the reviewer as `actorId`.
- **App-wide rows win slug clashes.** When a slug matches both an app-wide type and a user's own type, the app-wide row is used everywhere, and import or system approval no longer creates such a clash.
- **Erasing a template owner keeps the fields.** When an admin's account is erased, the fields of their field templates are copied into each type that attached them (and not inherited by subtypes), so those types resolve exactly as before.

**API and stability**

- **The public API works server-to-server in production.** Its route now accepts requests with no `Origin` header (curl, Zapier, backend jobs), which the starter's origin gate refused. Every call still authenticates with an API key.
- **Validation patterns are checked for catastrophic backtracking** on every save path, including field-group sub-fields, imports and proposals. At runtime a value is tested only up to a safe length: up to 100,000 characters for a pattern that runs in linear time (less when it has wide counted repeats — the server works the cap out from the pattern), and 1,000 (or the field's own maximum, up to 5,000) for one whose cost grows faster.
- **Data filters are scoped to the list.** The candidate scan honours the list's type, status, parent and space before its 5,000-row cap; a stored value that is not a number or date no longer fails the whole list (it simply does not match); filter values are checked against `valueType`; and `in` / `not_in` take at most 500 values.
- **Unique clashes answer 409**, naming what clashed (slug, a name already used under the same parent, reference number, public URL slug) on create, update, duplicate and move. Bulk responses show only client-safe error text per row.
- **Bounded reads.** A node read includes at most 5,000 children, a list with `includeChildren` at most 200 per parent, and duplicating with children is refused above 5,000 nested entries.
- **Bounded inputs.** Node and connection data are capped at 512 KB, settings and metadata at 64 KB, ids at 100 characters, and `sortOrder` at the database's integer limit. Settings writes validate the known keys and cap the stored settings at 64 KB. Through the HTTP route a user may hold at most 50 pending proposals, each size-bounded, and the admin proposal list returns at most 200.
- **`stageGroup` works.** It was accepted but never applied; it now filters a single type's entries to the stages in that group.
- **Public API `scopeRootId` is validated**: a repeated or over-long value is a 400 rather than silently ignored.

**In-app fixes**

- **Inline edits send only the changed fields.** Re-sending the whole record blocked edits once a locked field held a value, could overwrite another writer's changes, and could wipe a secret that failed to decrypt.
- **Errors show the server's reason.** Toasts and the import panels use the starter's `getApiErrorMessage` instead of the generic status text; settings saves and scope switches now report a failure (a failed switch reverts), and bulk actions list each row's failure reason.
- **Formula aggregates refresh after a change.** Formula traversal shares the cache of the Children, Path and Connections views, so a new child or connection is counted straight away instead of up to 30 seconds later.
- **A node opened under the wrong type's URL redirects** to its real type's page, rather than rendering with the other type's fields.
- **Node ids taken from field data are URL-encoded** in API paths, and the resolver skips values that are not ids.
- **Create buttons follow the type's state.** They are hidden for types that are disabled or registered only in code.
- **Field-group columns are not sortable** and show a row count; a failed sorted list offers **Clear sort**, and the sort resets when the type changes.

**Compliance and data lifecycle**

- **Deleted view and permanent delete.** The list's status filter gains **Deleted** (with a Deleted toggle when the status filter is hidden). There, the actions are **Restore** and **Delete permanently**, behind a confirm that says it cannot be undone. `POST /nodes/purge` takes optional `ids` (up to 500): your own deleted entries and their deleted descendants, or all of them. Like restoring, it needs the type's delete permission for every entry it would reach. An entry with a non-deleted entry under it is skipped; connections and chunks go with each purged entry; the response is `{ purged, skipped }`, and `structure.node.purged` fires. Delete confirms now say the entry moves to Deleted and can be restored, and the tree view falls back to the list outside the Active filter.
- **Type deletes clear their tombstones.** Entries already in Deleted no longer block deleting their node type or connection type, and are permanently removed after the delete (for an app-wide type, every user's).
- **Personal-data export includes Structure.** Six export handlers (seven on embeddings installs) cover entries and connections (secret values removed), settings, API keys (metadata only), the configuration the user created, reference counters and, on embeddings installs, retrieval chunk text; the entry, connection and chunk sections are paged and capped at 50,000 rows each, flagged when truncated.
- **Durable audit trail.** Sensitive events (type and config changes, imports, proposal reviews, bulk deletes, merges, purges, API-key lifecycle) are recorded in the starter's audit sink, with ids, slugs and counts only (never field data). A new `structure.nodeType.sharingChanged` hook records when a type starts or stops being shared.
- **Config export round-trips.** Exports include connection-type `contextHints` and `aiFields` and node-type `moduleId`, and an import update leaves a key the payload omits unchanged (except nullable columns an export leaves out when empty, which still clear).
- **Exports and destructive operations are logged for audit.**
- **Approved proposals keep only `proposedBy` and `approvedAt`**, so a proposer's source details no longer outlive their account on an app-wide type.

**Cleanup**

- **The v1.37.5 prime fallback is gone.** v1.37.5 kept the import-time prime as a fallback for starters below v2.7.0; it is removed, along with `primeStructureNavigation`. On such a starter the build failed before the fallback could run, and Structure now requires v2.13.0. The shell-init registration imports the starter's registry directly, `StructureInit` is the only primer, and `initStructureNavigation()` now also seeds the type and group cache.
- **Sidebar entry lists refresh after Structure's own writes** (debounced) rather than after every list fetch, and read preferences from the cached settings.
- **The "Show count badge" toggle works.** The nav link shows the type's number of active entries.
- **`sidebarNav.userOverridable` left unset now means on** everywhere, matching the admin form.
- **Node-detail sidebar sections** honour `collapsible` and `defaultCollapsed`.
- **Copy and dead controls.** The group icon picker is removed (the host sidebar has no slot for it); the sidebar options read "Recently updated" and "First N (list order)"; and the publishing panel says "Marked as published" rather than claiming the entry is publicly visible.
- **Unread config keys are marked deprecated** in the types: `rules.isHierarchical`, `rules.allowMultipleParents`, and `sidebarNav` `parentType`, `maxDepth`, `showEmpty`, `collapsible` and `defaultExpanded`.
- **Duplicated code folded** (connection mapping, the favourite and pin toggles, field change handlers, a node-fetch helper, the settings hooks), and stale comments rewritten.

Frontend + backend, no schema change. Host type-check against veradai: 0 errors (frontend and backend).

---

## v1.37.5

**Sidebar: stop duplicate registration, drive priming from the startup registry** *(generated-output)*

Fixes the repeated `[NavigationRegistry] Section "structure-group-…" already registered. Skipping.` console warnings and consolidates navigation priming.

- **Each sidebar section registers once.** `registerStructureNavigation` re-runs on every node-type sync, but the host navigation registry has no section-removal API (the `unregisterSection` probe was a silent no-op), so every pass re-registered the same section ids and the host warned. Section ids are now tracked for the module's lifetime and registered exactly once.
- **One priming path, via the startup registry.** On a modern host, *both* the unconditional import-time auto-prime and the shell-init `StructureInit` fetched and synced the registries, double-firing the whole registration. The import-time auto-prime is removed: `StructureInit` — registered on the host `shellInitRegistry` and mounted once post-login by the authenticated shell — is now the sole primer and primes **both** node types and groups. The import-time prime (with its auth-race retry) is retained only as the fallback the shell-init registration calls when the host has no shell-init registry (starter &lt; v2.7.0), so exactly one prime path runs on any host. *(That fallback is removed in [v1.38.0](#v1380), which requires starter v2.13.0.)*

Frontend-only, no schema change. Host-tsc clean.

---

## v1.37.4

**Resource & memory audit fixes** *(generated-output)*

A resource/memory audit of the generated code turned up one frontend leak and three heavy backend reads (the frontend cleanup surface — timers, subscriptions, observers, object-URLs, list virtualisation — came back clean). All four are fixed:

- **Memory leak — `similarityCache` is now bounded.** The `semanticSimilarity()` formula function memoised results in a module-scope map whose 60s TTL only gated *reuse* on read, so a successful result was retained for the whole SPA session — an unbounded leak for a type evaluating that function over many distinct node pairs. Now hard-capped (cleared past 200 entries), mirroring the sibling `parseCache`.
- **`getConnectionsForNodes` bounds its peak.** The batch edge read (for the cognitive engine's structural-gap diagnostics) loaded every edge over a vertex set of up to ~5000 nodes in one query + one decrypt fan-out; edge count is O(N²), so a dense graph could spike memory. It now processes the vertex set in chunks — bounding each query's bind list and the in-flight decrypt fan-out — while still returning **every** edge (the caller needs completeness), deduped by edge id and re-sorted. Verified to return the identical edge set as the single query.
- **`listNodes` custom sort no longer re-materialises the whole corpus.** With `dataSortField` set (and no `dataFilter`), the id scan had no cap, so every page turn pulled and in-memory-sorted the user's entire matching set. It is now capped at 5000 like the sibling `dataFilters` / search branches (`total` approximate beyond the cap, as there).
- **`getNodeContext` counts children with `groupBy`.** Child-type counts came from a `findMany` that materialised one row per child; a `prisma.groupBy` returns only the distinct types.

No schema change, no `structure update` needed. Host-tsc clean (frontend + backend).

---

## v1.37.3

**Security: computed fields can no longer launder a secret** *(generated-output)*

Closes a laundering vector: a `name` / `slug` / `description` `computedTemplate` or `calcConfig` formula (and any computed data field) is client-computed and stored **unencrypted**, then returned unredacted by the public per-type REST API — so a template referencing a `secret`-typed field would persist that secret's plaintext in the clear. This was documented but not enforced.

- **Enforced on every config-write path.** Saving, proposing, updating or **importing** a node type whose stored computed field references a secret token — `{secret}`, `{group.sub}` or `{ref->secret}` — is now rejected (`BadRequestError` server-side; the App Structure form blocks it with a clear message and jumps to the Fields tab). The secret is caught whether it is the type's **own** field, a field-group sub-field, an **inherited** (superClass) field, or one from an **attached field template**, and whether reached directly or via a one-hop node-reference deref. Detection reuses the merge-field token schema (each token already carries its field type), so it stays in lock-step with what the resolver actually reads.
- **Import & proposals covered too.** The JSON config import writes rows directly (bypassing create/update), so the guard runs as a second pass inside the import transaction — against transaction-visible rows, so a secret defined on a **co-imported** referenced/superClass type is seen before commit. `approveNodeTypeProposal` re-runs the guard before promoting a pending config to live (catching a parent that became secret since propose-time, and any pre-guard proposal).
- **Residual (documented):** a *dynamic* `pluck(nodes({refs}), 'aSecret')` — where the field name is a runtime string, not a token — is not statically caught; and traversal node-views (`children`/`scope`/`connections`) were already stripped of secret values in v1.37.2.

Frontend + backend, no schema change. Runtime-verified (token-detection suite) and adversarially reviewed across **three** find→verify passes — 8 findings fixed pre-release (attached-template secrets, import transaction-visibility, non-word field ids, the proposal-approval path, inherited/group-nested ref-derefs, a built-in id false-positive). Host-tsc clean (frontend + backend).

---

## v1.37.2

**Formula aggregation over children and connections** *(generated-output)*

Formula fields can now reduce a traversal to a single number, and reach a node's connection graph.

- **List-aware `sum` / `avg` / `min` / `max`.** These now aggregate over a list as readily as over loose arguments, so `sum(pluck(children('invoice'), 'amount'))` totals every child invoice and `avg(pluck(connections('review'), 'score'))` averages connected reviews. They follow spreadsheet `SUM`/`AVERAGE` rules — a **blank** item (a child missing that field) is ignored so it never sinks an average or a minimum, while a real `0` counts; over an empty list `sum` is `0` and `avg`/`min`/`max` are blank. *(This changes an existing edge case: a non-list `avg`/`min`/`max` whose operands are all blank now yields blank rather than `0`.)*
- **New `connections([type])` traversal keyword.** Returns the nodes on the **other end** of each active connection edge, optionally filtered to one connection type, each view carrying the edge's `connectionType` / `connectionId` as built-ins for `filter`. Self-loops and endpoints the reader can't access are skipped. It joins `children`/`parents`/`scope`/`nodes` as a fifth structural form and returns an empty list on a brand-new node.
- **Security — formulas can no longer read a secret through traversal.** Every traversal node-view is now stripped of sealed secret-field values (top level and inside field-group rows), so `pluck`/aggregation over `children`/`scope`/`connections` can never surface — or launder into a stored `name`/`slug` — a secret. (The own-node `{secret}` / `nodes()`-deref caveat is unchanged; see [Fields](/modules/structure/fields/#formula).)
- **`+` concatenation treats a `null`/`undefined` operand as `''`** (matching `concat`), so an empty aggregate renders blank in a name/slug formula rather than the literal `null`.

Frontend-only — no schema change, no `structure update` needed. Runtime-verified (aggregation + special-form + secret-strip suites) and adversarially reviewed (four-lens find → verify): four findings fixed pre-release. Host-tsc clean (frontend).

---

## v1.37.1

**Cross-module DX: `getConnection` + `relationClass`/`superProperty` on connection types** *(generated-output)*

Two cognitive-db engine asks. **Adds `relationClass` + `superProperty` columns** to `structure_connection_type` — run `npx structure update`.

- **`getConnection(id, userId): NodeConnection | null`** (serviceRegistry-exposed + `GET /connections/:id`, 404 on null) reads one connection by id — `userId`-scoped and `nodeType:'connection'`-guarded (an IDOR-safe read that can't return a regular node), returns `null` (not a throw) when absent, and unseals secret field values for the owner. It's the read-before-write half the engine's strength / pattern-status writes need: read → modify → `updateConnection` (which re-seals on write) — the same round-trip `getNodeConnections` already supports.
- **`relationClass`** (`'associative' | 'sequential' | 'hierarchical' | 'descriptive'`) and **`superProperty`** (a parent connection-type slug) on `ConnectionTypeConfig` — the connection-type analogue of a node type's `superClasses`, and where cognitive-pipeline's grown relationship vocabulary persists. Wired through the schema, zod validation, create/update, both row→config read mappings, JSON config import/export, and a relation-kind select + super-property input in the connection-type editor. Both clear to `null` (the admin controls, the API, and the engine can all unset them).

Additive; host-tsc clean (frontend + backend). Adversarially reviewed (one admin-form clear-path write-loss fixed pre-release).

---

## v1.37.0

**Sidebar entry lists, node pinning, list grouping & node-detail sidebar sections** *(generated-output)*

The cleanup audit surfaced three admin config surfaces that were saved but never acted on (`navigation.sidebarNav`, node-detail sidebar sections, `navigation.listPage.groupBy`). This release wires all three into real features. **Adds a `structure_node.isPinned` column** — run `npx structure update` to apply the schema.

- **Sidebar entry lists.** `navigation.sidebarNav` now works: a node type can list a few of its entries directly under its sidebar link — the user's favourites, most-recent, all (first N), or pinned — up to `maxEntries`. The base nav renders instantly and entries stream in, refreshing live when the underlying nodes change (a pin, a favourite, a create). Concurrent refreshes are generation-guarded so a slow refresh can never clobber a newer one or leak a stale item.
- **Node pinning.** A new first-class per-node pin (`isPinned`, mirroring `isFavorite`): `togglePin` service + `POST /nodes/:id/pin`, an `isPinned` list filter, a `structure.node.pinned` hook, and a pin toggle on the node detail header (shown for types whose sidebar can list pinned entries). The `'pinned'` sidebar filter lists a user's pinned entries.
- **Per-user sidebar overrides.** When a type sets `sidebarNav.userOverridable`, each user can choose their own entry filter and count from a "Sidebar" popover on the list page; the choice is stored per-type in `structure_settings.sidebarPrefs` and the sidebar re-renders on save.
- **Node-detail sidebar sections.** `NodeDetailPage` now renders module-registered sidebar sections (`useNodeSidebarSections`) in a right-hand column, honouring the type's `integrations.sidebarSections` enable/title/order config. (Visible once a module registers a section.)
- **List grouping.** `navigation.listPage.groupBy` groups the list view into sections by a field — status (title-cased), stage (resolved to its configured name), or a select field (resolved to option labels) — with a "Group entries by" control in the type editor. Drag-reorder is disabled while grouped, and grouping pulls the largest page the backend allows so sections stay complete.

Adversarially reviewed (five-lens find → verify): eight findings fixed pre-release (nav-refresh races, a blank-entry window on navigation, unreachable pin toggle, raw-slug group labels, page-local grouping, a group-key collision). Host-tsc clean (frontend + backend).

---

## v1.36.14

**Cleanup & security audit — secret-exposure fixes + dead-code removal** *(generated-output)*

A cleanup-and-security pass (five-area adversarial audit) fixed nine confirmed findings. This release covers the security fixes and the low-risk cleanups; the "dead config" items (unwired admin toggles) are being wired up separately.

- **Secret fields no longer render in cleartext on the read-only node view.** `NodeFieldRenderer` had no `secret` case, so an owner's decrypted API-key/token (getNode unseals secrets for the owner) printed verbatim in the Info tab. It now masks by default (`••••••••••`) with an Eye/EyeOff reveal toggle, mirroring the edit input.
- **Public API stops echoing sealed secret envelopes.** `GET /:type/:id` already redacted secret-type field values, but the sibling `GET /:type` (list), `POST /:type` and `PATCH /:type/:id` returned them verbatim as `{ __sealed: … }` envelopes. All three now run responses through `redactSecretValues`, matching the single-fetch contract.
- **`reorderNodes` now enforces the `edit` permission.** It was the only mutating node op with no `assertNodePermission` gate, so a non-admin could reorder `edit:'admin'`-locked nodes they own. It now resolves the distinct types among the reordered rows and asserts `edit` on each (and the `POST /reorder` route resolves the caller's role).
- **`visibleWhen` evaluation unified.** `NodeFieldRenderer` reimplemented the field-visibility evaluator inline twice; both now call the shared `isFieldVisible` (utils/field-conditions), so computed-template, formula and regular fields resolve conditions from one source.
- **Dead code removed.** Deleted the unused `useStructureNavigation` hook (it re-implemented nav-building with the pre-v1.34.21 `enabled`-truthy filter — a fixed bug a host could have re-imported), and trimmed `NodeTypeLabels` from ~18 keys to the six `getLabel` actually resolves (`labels` stays a free-form `Record<string,string>` at the schema level, so existing configs are unaffected).

Host-tsc clean (frontend + backend).

---

## v1.36.13

**Cross-module DX: `mergeNode` (node dedup / condensation) + exact-name `listNodes` filter** *(generated-output)*

cognitive-db's cross-item dedup and condensation levers need to fold a duplicate node into a canonical one, and to look up an entity by exact name confined to a scope. This release adds both — and clarifies that the *archive* and *re-parent* halves of the same cross-module ask already shipped as `archiveNode` / `moveNode`.

`structureService.mergeNode(fromId, intoId, userId, options?)` (serviceRegistry-exposed; no HTTP route) folds a duplicate node into a canonical one in a single transaction, then retires the duplicate. It:

- **Re-points** every connection touching the duplicate onto the canonical — dropping self-loops and collapsing edges that would duplicate one the canonical already has (bidirectional-aware).
- **Re-parents** the duplicate's direct children under the canonical. A child whose name clashes with an existing child is *suffixed* (`… (merged)`), never dropped, so the `@@unique([userId, parentId, name])` constraint can never abort the merge and no child is lost.
- **Backfills** only the canonical's *empty* `data` fields from the duplicate — the canonical always wins a populated field; secret values are re-sealed on write (a legacy plaintext value is never relocated in the clear).
- **Archives** the duplicate (or soft-deletes it with `retire: 'delete'`, which then cascades the duplicate's remaining subtree + every edge touching it or a descendant, mirroring `deleteNode`).

Every step is a toggleable option (`reparentChildren`, `mergeData`, `retire`). Endpoints must be same-owner, same-type, same-space, non-shared, non-deleted, and neither an ancestor of the other. Fires a typed `structure.node.merged` hook (`{ userId, fromId, intoId, node, reparentedChildren, repointedConnections, droppedConnections }`); returns the canonical node.

**Exact-name filter on `listNodes`.** `NodeListQuery` gains `name` (exact equality — served by the `(userId, parentId, name)` index when paired with `parentId`) and `nameInsensitive` (case-insensitive; LIKE metacharacters in the value are escaped, so a name like `node_1` matches literally instead of acting as a wildcard). `listNodes` also now honours an explicit `options.scopeRootId` for trusted (`isSystem`) callers — matching the retrieval arm — which is what confines exact-name dedup to a space.

**Already available (no new code):** the *archive* and *re-parent* halves of the handoff ask are `archiveNode` and `moveNode` (both serviceRegistry-callable, hierarchy- and space-enforced); the scope-confinement half is already served by `parentId` / `pathPrefix` on `NodeListQuery`.

Additive; host-tsc clean (frontend + backend). Adversarially reviewed (four lenses + per-finding verification, then a re-verify of the fixes): nine findings fixed pre-release, including a cross-space guard that would have rejected the common top-level dedup and an `ILIKE` wildcard footgun on the insensitive name filter.

---

## v1.36.12

**Cross-module DX: `getConnectionsForNodes` — batch edge read** *(generated-output)*

cognitive-db (v1.12.8) needs to load, in one query, every connection touching a set of vertices (up to ~5000) for its structural-gap diagnostics (dead-end / orphan detection) — the per-node path is an N-query storm.

`structureService.getConnectionsForNodes(nodeIds, userId, { status? })` (serviceRegistry-exposed) returns every connection where `fromNodeId` **or** `toNodeId` is in `nodeIds`, in a single query — `userId`-scoped (connections are never shared), status-filtered (default `['active']`), and `nodeIds: []` → `[]`. It returns the same `NodeConnection` shape as `getNodeConnections`, but **without endpoint hydration** (the engine reads only `fromNodeId` / `toNodeId`) and without `scopeRootId` (the caller passes an already-scoped vertex set). Secret connection-field values are unsealed for the owner, with field defs cached once per connection type (a no-op for the secret-free co-activation / discovered edges). Additive — the engine degrades safely (skips structural gaps) until its adapter wires this in. Host-tsc clean.

---

## v1.36.11

**Sidebar icons off the lucide barrel — resolve stored names via the starter's `IconRenderer`** *(generated-output)*

The generated `navigation.registration.ts` turned a node type's stored Lucide icon *name* into a component by importing lucide's full `icons` map (`import { icons as lucideIcons }`, ~1600 components) and indexing it — a dynamic index no bundler can tree-shake, so the whole barrel landed in the app's first-paint bundle. Structure is imported eagerly, so that pinned ~1600 icon components into every app's initial load. This was the module's **last** barrel pull: `NodeIcon` and the card / list / tree surfaces already resolve icons through the starter's DynamicIcon-based `IconRenderer`.

- The nav registration now resolves the stored name through `IconRenderer` (`@/components/icon-picker`) via a small `resolveNavIcon(name)` wrapper — one lazy chunk per icon actually used, not the whole set. Cached per name so component identity stays stable across re-registration (icons don't remount); `strokeWidth` 2 preserves lucide's default weight; an unknown / renamed name falls back to a Folder (matching the old `?? Folder`).
- **Requires starter ≥ v2.7.1**, which widened `NavigationItem.icon` from `LucideIcon` to the exported `NavIcon` (`LucideIcon | ComponentType<SVGProps<SVGSVGElement>>`) so a name-resolving component is assignable. On an older host the generated file would fail `type-check`.
- Validated type-clean in a real app (veradai — zero `tsc` errors across the frontend). Pick it up with `npx structure update` after `template:update` brings starter v2.7.1.

## v1.36.10

**Sidebar navigation primes on load — StructureInit via the starter's shell-init registry** *(generated-output)*

Structure builds the sidebar from your node types, which it fetches from the server. That prime ran at module *import* (app boot) — before login — so it 401'd, retried briefly, gave up, and never re-fired after auth: the sidebar stayed empty until you opened a Structure page or **Admin → App Structure**. Structure shipped a `StructureInit` helper for exactly this, but nothing mounted it (the app shell is starter-owned).

- A new `registrations/shell-init.registration.ts` registers `StructureInit` with the starter's **`shellInitRegistry`** (starter v2.7.0), so the authenticated shell (`DashboardPage`) mounts it once **post-login** and the sidebar primes on load. The import-time auto-prime stays as a fallback.
- **Guarded** — a dynamic, catch-wrapped import of the shell-init registry, so on an older host (starter &lt; v2.7.0) it degrades to a silent no-op.
- **Requires starter ≥ v2.7.0** for the shell-init registry. Pick it up with `npx structure update` (after `template:update` brings starter v2.7.0). Validated type-clean in a real app.

## v1.36.9

**Cross-module DX: `updateConnection` — a connection field-update capability** *(generated-output)*

cognitive-db (v1.12.1) needed a way to update an existing connection's `data`: working-memory consolidation strengthens a co-activation edge by raising `data.strength`, but Structure's connection adapter had `createConnection` / `getNodeConnections` / `promoteConnection` and no update path.

`structureService.updateConnection(connectionId, userId, { data?, metadata? })` — serviceRegistry-exposed, plus `PATCH /connections/:id` — replaces the connection's `data` and/or `metadata` JSON and returns the updated `NodeConnection`. **Replace semantics:** the caller does its own read-modify-write and passes the full merged object; Structure sets the field to exactly what is passed, never a deep-merge. It is `userId`-scoped and throws `NotFoundError` on a missing / foreign id (never a silent no-op), and touches only `data` / `metadata` — never `status`, the endpoints, or `connectionType`. Secret fields are **re-sealed on write** (`getNodeConnections` returns *unsealed* data, so a round-tripped value would otherwise persist a plaintext secret; already-sealed envelopes are skipped). Fires the new `structure.connection.updated` action hook (declared in the audit manifest, typed as `ConnectionUpdatedPayload`). Host-tsc clean; additive.

---

## v1.36.8

**`nodeFilterRegistry` list filters are live — the last Now/Next item, wired** *(generated-output)*

`nodeFilterRegistry` — the extension point for a module to add its own filter controls to a node type's list page — had a fully-built `useNodeFilters` hook with **zero call sites**: registered filters rendered nowhere and drove nothing (the same declared-but-dead trap the module has closed several times). It is wired now, end-to-end and pagination-safe.

`NodeListPage` renders each registered filter's `component` in the filter bar, beside the built-in field filters, driving the shared `useNodeFilters` value / `onChange` state. A **server-mode** filter implements `toDataFilters(value): DataFilter[]` — those merge into the list query's `dataFilters` and are applied in the database by the existing engine, so they filter across **every page**, not just the loaded one. A **client-mode** filter implements `filterFn` and refines the loaded page in memory. An active module filter also disables drag-reorder (a filtered list is not in stored order).

The server contract changed from the old `toQueryParams` (arbitrary `Record<string,string>`, which the backend had no way to apply) to **`toDataFilters` returning structured `DataFilter[]`** — the same shape the built-in field filters produce. So **no backend change was needed**: `buildDataFilterQuery` already applies any `DataFilter` (all thirteen operators, a parameterised `fieldId`, `.max(50)`-validated), across all pages. A pre-launch clean break — the old name had no consumers. Host-tsc clean. (The hook's `useNodeFilters` API surface stays the same except `serverQueryParams` → `serverDataFilters`.)

An adversarial review of the wiring caught and fixed five minor issues pre-release: client filter components now receive the *unfiltered* loaded page (so a facet filter can't strangle its own option list), `hasActiveFilters` counts only defined values (so it resets when a filter is cleared), a throwing module `filterFn` / `toDataFilters` is contained rather than blanking the list, the empty-state no longer shows "nothing here yet" copy while a filter is active, and the debounce expectation for server-mode filters is documented.

---

## v1.36.7

**Hardening — two fixes from the v1.36.6 adversarial review** *(generated-output)*

An adversarial review of the queue sweep caught two real defects before release; both are fixed here.

- **Drag-reorder could silently overwrite `sortOrder` from a non-stored order.** The reorder gate required a manual-sort type but not that the list was actually *displayed* in stored `sortOrder` — and `defaultSort` (which drives the query's sort) defaults to `name`, independent of the Manual Sorting toggle. So a manual-sort type left sorted alphabetically still showed drag handles, and a drag rewrote every sibling's `sortOrder` from the alphabetical array (the dragged row then snapped back on refetch, while the persisted order was clobbered). The gate now also requires the effective sort to resolve to `sortOrder` — the exact expression the query builds — matching what the reorder assumes.

- **The merge-token picker showed a stale field snapshot.** The v1.36.6 approach seeded the server's `resolved` field projection into form state and stripped it on submit — but `getEffectiveFields` *replaces* live fields with `resolved.fields` (`??`, not a merge), so a field added or edited in the same session didn't appear in the picker. It now merges the live own fields with the projection's inherited / attached-template fields (own wins on id collision), so session edits show immediately and inherited fields still appear. The form-state seed and submit-strip are gone.
