## v1.36.6

**Now / Next queue sweep — five admin & list fixes** *(generated-output)*

Five ranked Now/Next items cleared in one pass. All host-tsc clean; the drag-reorder and node-reference changes were mapped by exploration before editing.

- **Merge-field admin gaps closed.** The merge-token picker in `NodeTypeForm` now surfaces inherited superClass (and attached-template) fields, not just the type's own — `getMergeFieldSchema` resolves through the type's field projection, which the admin form previously never fed it, so inherited tokens silently vanished. And the **Card display** section gains **Title template** and **Body template** inputs, each with a `MergeFieldPicker`, writing `navigation.listPage.card.titleTemplate` / `bodyTemplate`. Both slots were already consumed by `NodeCard` but had no admin control (`titleTemplate` had zero occurrences in the admin form) — settable only by hand-editing JSON until now.

- **Sibling drag-reorder on node lists.** The grid and list views gain drag-to-reorder, wired to the existing `reorderNodes` path (`POST /reorder`) — a grip handle (top-left on grid cards, inline on list rows), pointer + keyboard sensors. It is deliberately gated: offered only on a **manual-sort** type showing **one unfiltered, unpaginated page** of a **single parent's** active siblings in stored order. The server assigns `sortOrder` by array position, so reordering a filtered or paginated subset would write misleading positions — this mirrors the "not draggable while filtered" guard the admin type list already uses.

- **PageHeader on the App Structure admin page**, and the **Registries tab marked read-only.** `NodeTypesAdminPage` opened straight into its tab row with no title; it now leads with a `PageHeader`. The Registry Explorer gained a line making explicit that it is a read-only view of what installed modules have registered — there is nothing to configure there.

- **`node-reference` allowed-types picker.** The two remaining raw comma-separated-slug text boxes (the field editor and the field-group sub-field editor) are replaced with the reusable `TypeListField` add-and-list picker, fed by `useNodeTypes()`. Every other type picker in App Structure moved to `TypeListField` in v1.34.18 / v1.34.23; the leftover text box read as a missing setting.

- **Corrected a false public-API comment.** The note in the public REST routes claimed per-field schema validation "is performed in the app UI, not enforced server-side here" — falsified since v1.33.31, when `validateNodeData` began running on every write path the public handlers delegate to. It now states the truth: an external integrator cannot post data that violates the type's field schema.

## v1.36.5

**One confirmation primitive for every destructive action — `useConfirm()`** *(generated-output)*

The admin UI carried three different confirmation shapes and two genuine gaps. The worst: an **API key was revoked on a single click with no confirmation** — irreversible, and it breaks every integration still holding that key. The built-in **bulk delete** and the saved-**filter-preset** delete fired the same way. Elsewhere a plain `Dialog` guarded some deletes (connection types, the node settings tab, the template editor) while an `AlertDialog` guarded others (node types, field templates, functions) — the same job done three ways.

This replaces all of it with **one primitive**: `useConfirm()` (`components/common/ConfirmDialog.tsx`), an imperative `await confirm({ title, description, confirmLabel, destructive })` that resolves `true` / `false`, backed by a single shadcn `AlertDialog`. Every destructive action now routes through it — the node card and detail-header delete, the settings-tab delete, built-in bulk delete, filter-preset delete, connection removal, and every admin delete (node types, connection types, groups, field templates, functions, template editor, and the API-key revoke) — plus the "stop sharing" primary-scope toggle and module-registered bulk-action confirms. The hand-rolled `Dialog` / `AlertDialog` confirmation blocks and their `useState` flags are gone.

It is a **self-contained hook**, not a context provider. Structure owns no single app-root to mount a provider above, `StructureInit` is optional, and its page components are themselves confirm-consumers (a component cannot consume a provider it renders in its own JSX). So each consumer calls `confirm()` and renders `{confirmDialog}` once — the same one-line call sites, without the fragile provider. The resolver settles exactly once, so Radix firing both the confirm click and its trailing `onOpenChange(false)` never double-resolves, and Escape / cancel resolve `false`.

The pass also **corrected inaccurate copy**: node deletion is a *soft, cascading* delete (the node and everything nested under it, restorable), but the settings-tab dialog claimed it was permanent and could not be undone. The new confirmations state the cascade plainly without over- or under-claiming a restore path. Adversarial review (completeness / primitive correctness / call-site behaviour) surfaced no confirmed defects; host-tsc clean (0 errors across the regenerated host).

---

## v1.36.4

**Cross-module DX: `structureService.getActiveScope(userId)` — the active-space decision, exposed** *(generated-output)*

Closes the one gap a security review of the retrieval arm flagged. Structure's own reads (list, tree, keyword search) confine to the user's active enforced space and **deny** — return nothing — when spaces are enforced but the user has *no* active space selected. A `serviceRegistry` retrieval consumer (cognitive-pipeline, ai-core, any RAG arm) that passes `scopeRootId` to the embeddings methods could match the *confine* case, but had no way to detect that *deny* state, so it would surface the user's content across every space instead of nothing.

`getActiveScope(userId, { scopeRootId? })` exposes what Structure's private `resolveActiveSpace` already computes, as a plain result — the enforced-scoping counterpart to [`getUnreadableTypes`](/modules/structure/cognitive/#read-enforcement-for-retrieval-consumers):

- `{ mode: 'none' }` — nothing enforced for this user: don't confine.
- `{ mode: 'deny' }` — a space is enforced but none is active: a user-facing read must return nothing.
- `{ mode: 'allow', scopeRootId }` — confine to `scopeRootId` (pass it straight to the embeddings methods / `expandGraph`).

It's the same cross-type resolution the tree and keyword search use, so a consumer stays in lockstep with Structure. Shared/global types stay space-exempt in every mode (a consumer confines only its non-global content); `scopeRootId` overrides the persisted setting and an explicit-but-invalid one throws `BadRequestError`; and it deliberately takes no `isSystem` — it reports the *user's* confinement, which a system bypass would erase. Purely additive: one new service method, one new exported `ActiveScope` type; host-tsc clean.

---

## v1.36.3

**Feature: module-registered bulk actions render in the list toolbar** *(generated-output)*

`nodeActionRegistry` has had a `'bulk-action'` location — collected, listed on the Registries admin tab, admin-configurable on a type's Integrations tab, and readable via `useBulkActions(nodeType)` — since it was introduced, but **nothing in the generated UI rendered it**: `useBulkActions` had exactly one occurrence in the repo, its own definition. That's the same declared-but-dead extension-point trap this module has closed twice before. It's wired now.

When rows are selected on a node type's list page, any registered `'bulk-action'` for that type appears in the **selection toolbar**, before the built-in Archive / Restore / Delete. Each button carries the action's `icon` (via the starter's bundle-safe `IconRenderer`), `label`, and `variant` (`default` → an outline button, to sit with the built-ins), ordered by `order` — with the admin's per-action `label` / `order` / `enabled` overrides from the Integrations tab applied, the same overrides the detail-header/context-menu locations already get (`getBulkActions` was extended to honour them, not just `enabled`).

Running an action honours the contract:
- **`bulkHandler(nodes, nodeType)`** is called **once** with the whole selection when present; without it, the single-node **`handler`** is **fanned out per node**, tolerating partial failure (a summary toast reports how many of N failed) — mirroring the built-in bulk endpoints' semantics.
- The selection is intersected with the **owner-filtered** `selectableNodes` the toolbar already computes, so a bulk action on a shared/global type never runs over another user's rows (the same boundary the built-in bulk ops use).
- A **`confirm`** block pops an `AlertDialog` (title / description / confirmLabel / destructive variant) before running; without it the action runs on click.
- After it completes the selection clears and the list refetches, since a module handler may change node data without invalidating Structure's queries.

A per-node `isVisible` does **not** hide the toolbar button (it's a whole-selection control), but it **is** honoured when the action runs — nodes it excludes are skipped, matching the single-node contract. `detail-header` and `context-menu` actions remain the consumer's to render (read them with `useNodeActions`). See [Registries → nodeActionRegistry](/modules/structure/registries-guide/#5-nodeactionregistry).

---

## v1.36.2

**Feature: NodeSelector inline quick-create, `onNodeCreated`, and a dialog variant** *(generated-output)*

`NodeSelector` is the module's **primary integration point** — every other module uses it to reference a Structure node — and until now it made each consumer build its own creation flow: `allowCreate` rendered a "Create new" group only when an `onCreateClick` callback was wired, and `createParentId` was accepted but ignored. Three related gaps close together, since they are one piece of work on one component.

**Inline quick-create.** With `allowCreate` on, picking `New {type}` now opens a minimal **name-only form inside the picker** — create-and-select without leaving the popover, no consumer wiring. On submit it creates the node (`createParentId` sets its parent), auto-selects it (`onChange` fires with the new id), and closes. A quick-created node is name-only, so a type with **required custom fields** rejects server-side — the toast shows why and the form stays open; delegate those to a full form via `onCreateClick`, which is now optional and, when present, preserves the old delegate-and-close behaviour.

**`onNodeCreated(node)`.** A new callback fires with the freshly-created node (already selected), so a consumer can react — link it, seed related data — without diffing `onChange`.

**Dialog variant.** A new `variant="dialog"` renders the same trigger + list + quick-create form inside a shadcn `Dialog` instead of a `Popover`. Use it when the selector itself lives inside another dialog or sheet, where a nested popover clips or fights the parent's focus trap — the case that previously forced consumers to hand-roll their own picker. An optional `dialogTitle` sets its heading (and accessible title). The trigger and panels were extracted so both variants share one implementation.

Fully backward-compatible: the default is still the popover, `onCreateClick` still delegates, and every existing prop behaves as before. See [Integration → NodeSelector](/modules/structure/integration/#using-nodeselector-to-reference-nodes).

---

## v1.36.1

**DX: typed action & filter hook contexts** *(generated-output, no runtime change)*

The cross-module hook contract becomes typed — the highest-leverage cross-module DX item on the roadmap, landing just before cognitive-pipeline is written against these hooks.

**Before**, every one of the ~50 action hooks the service fires through `actionRegistry.doActionAll` (and the 4 filter hooks it applies through `filterRegistry.applyAll`) passed an **inline object literal**, and the only exported context interfaces were `ApiKeyContext` and `NodeContext`. A consuming module wrote its handler against an untyped object and found out the shape at runtime; a payload's shape was whatever the last edit happened to pass.

**Now**, each hook has a **named, exported payload interface** in `structure.types.ts` — `structure.node.created` → `NodeCreatedPayload`, `structure.node.moved` → `NodeMovedPayload`, `structure.config.imported` → `ConfigImportedPayload`, and so on — collected into two maps keyed by hook name:

```typescript
import type { NodeCreatedPayload, StructureActionPayloads } from '../structure/structure.types.js';

actionRegistry.addAction('structure.node.created', 'my-listener',
  (p: NodeCreatedPayload) => { /* p.node, p.nodeType are typed */ });

// or index the map by hook name:
type P = StructureActionPayloads['structure.node.moved'];
```

Filters get the matching `StructureFilterHooks` map (`{ value, context }` per hook) and a shared `StructureFilterContext`.

**The producer is enforced too.** The service now fires through two thin typed wrappers — `fireAction<K>(hook, payload)` and `applyFilter<K>(hook, value, context)` — so a payload that drifts from its declared interface fails *this module's* build, not a consumer's. The wrappers forward identically to the registries (`void doActionAll` / `return applyAll`), so there is **zero runtime change**: this release is a pure typing refactor.

**What writing the contract caught.** Typing the call sites surfaced **16 producer sites** that were passing a raw Prisma row (`data: JsonValue`, `createdAt: Date`) where the contract is the domain type (`StructureNode`, `NodeTypeGroup`, `StructureTemplate`, `StructureFieldTemplate`, `StructureFunction`) — exactly the drift the roadmap flagged. Each now carries the codebase's standard `as unknown as <DomainType>` boundary cast (the same one the service methods already use on their return values), and `NodeStageChangedPayload.newStage` was corrected to `string | null` (a stage can be cleared). `ImportBundleResult` / `BundleCategoryStats` moved to `structure.types.ts` so `structure.config.imported` can carry the real result type. Host-tsc clean on both ends; the `auditEvents` contract and the `action`/`filter.registration.ts` hook tables were re-pointed at the interfaces.

---

## v1.36.0

**Feature: formula traversal, list functions, and formulas on built-in fields** *(generated-output)*

The formula field's expression language stops being an arithmetic calculator over one node's own fields and becomes the engine for **derived values across the graph**. Three things landed together, all client-side (the same client-computes / server-accepts trust model as `computedTemplate`, [D11](/modules/structure/decisions/)), and all closing [Now / Next](/modules/structure/roadmap/) item 1.

**Why.** A derived name cannot carry identity — that is what [v1.35.0](#v1350)'s reference numbers were for — but with a citable handle in place the name is now free to be *computed*, and the motivating case needs computation the old language could not express: a Matter whose parties are two people both surnamed Schulz plus an organisation has to render as **"Schulz B & Schulz J"**, not "Schulz & Schulz & ICL". That is filter-map-join over related nodes — unreachable by a substitution template, and there was not one collection or traversal function among the 35 built-ins.

**Node traversal.** Four language keywords resolve related nodes into **node-views** — one flat record per node, its built-ins (`name`, `nodeType`, `reference`, …) and its data fields on a single level:

| Form | Returns |
|---|---|
| `children([type])` | Direct children of the current node, optionally one node type |
| `parents([type])` | Ancestors, nearest parent first |
| `scope([type])` | Every node in the current space (the `rootNodeId` subtree) |
| `nodes(ids)` | Hydrates node-reference ids — e.g. `nodes({parties})` — into node-views |

Relationships here are modelled both **structurally** (`children`/`parents`/`scope`) and as **reference fields** (`nodes`), and traversal is applied uniformly across both. On a brand-new node there is no id yet, so the structural forms return an empty list; `nodes({parties})` still resolves, because the reference ids are already in the form data — so a name formula works at create time.

**List functions.** `map(list, expr)` and `filter(list, pred)` are higher-order **special forms**: the second argument is a per-item sub-expression, evaluated once per item with that item bound — `{field}` reads the item's field, `{_}` (or `{it}`) is the whole item. `filter` also takes a 3-argument field-name form, `filter(list, 'nodeType', 'person')`. Around them sit the pure helpers `pluck`, `join`, `first`, `last`, `count`, `reverse`, `unique`, `includes` and `sortBy` (a new `list` function category). The Matter name becomes:

```
join(map(filter(children(), {nodeType} == 'person'),
         concat({surname}, ' ', upper(substr({first_name}, 0, 1)))),
     ' & ')
```

**Formulas on built-in fields.** `name`, `description` and `slug` now accept a `calcConfig` formula alongside their `computedTemplate`, set under **App Structure › Fields › Built-in Fields**. If both are set the **template wins**. Like the merge template it resolves against the node's custom-field `data` only — never a sibling built-in — so `name`/`slug` formulas cannot oscillate, and the computed value is written on the create/edit form and stored, so read views show it without recomputing.

**Security and cost.** A node-view is carried **opaquely**: the language still has no member-access operator, so a record's fields are only ever read through a prototype-safe `readField` (own-key only) — inside `map`/`filter` via `{tokens}`, or by name via `pluck` / a 3-arg `filter`. The special forms are language keywords dispatched before the registry, so an admin or module function cannot shadow `map`/`children`/etc. Two new bounds join the existing step/depth/string caps: **`MAX_LIST_LENGTH` (1000)** on any traversed or mapped list, and **`MAX_FETCHES` (200)** on the backend node reads a single evaluation may trigger — and, closing a defect an adversarial review caught before ship, a `{ref->field}` dereference inside a `map`/`filter` body now counts against that same fetch budget, so `map(scope(), {owner->name})` over a large space can no longer fan out unbounded. A hardening pass also makes a `{__proto__}`/`{constructor}` token resolve to nothing rather than leaking an inherited member as a value.

**Admin.** The formula editor's function picker now lists the traversal and list keywords first; the built-in field editor gains a **Formula** input beside the merge template; both surface the same field-insert picker.

---

## v1.35.0

**Feature: reference numbers — a citable identity for an entry** *(generated-output, schema change)*

An entry can now carry a server-assigned, human-readable reference — `MAT-2026-0147` — issued once and never changed afterwards. **Off by default**, switched on per node type under **Basic → Reference numbers**. A type that leaves it off stores `NULL` and behaves exactly as it did before.

**Why a fourth identifier.** None of the three that already existed can be quoted. `name` is display text and is about to become formula-derived, so two unrelated matters both compute to "Schulz & Schulz", and adding a party silently rewrites a name something else already cited. `id` is a cuid — stable, but nobody reads one down a phone. `slug` is client-supplied and `@@unique([userId, slug])` with no de-dup helper, so a computed slug collides. The reference is the handle a human quotes; with it in place, the name is free to be computed.

**The format**, stored as `structure_node_type.referenceConfig` (JSON, defaults `{}`):

| key | default | effect |
|---|---|---|
| `enabled` | `false` | Master switch — anything other than `true` means the type issues none |
| `prefix` | *(none)* | Leading segment, e.g. `MAT`. Omitted from the reference when empty |
| `separator` | `-` | Joins the segments. An explicitly empty string is honoured (`MAT20260147`) |
| `padding` | `4` | Zero-pad width, clamped 1–12. Only ever **widens**: `0999 → 1000 → 10000`, because lpad-truncating collides |
| `includeYear` | `false` | Inserts the UTC year between the prefix and the number |
| `resetYearly` | `false` | Restarts the count each year. Ignored without `includeYear` — resetting with no year segment reissues last year's numbers |

`{ enabled: true, prefix: 'MAT', separator: '-', padding: 4, includeYear: true, resetYearly: true }` renders `MAT-2026-0147`; `{ enabled: true, prefix: 'INC', padding: 4 }` renders `INC-0031`. The form previews the current knobs live as you set them.

**Where it shows.** As a monospace badge beside the entry name in the detail header — a button rather than plain text, because copying it is the whole point — and it is matched by list search, so a number pasted out of an email finds the record. It is deliberately **not** stripped for a non-owner on a shared node type: a reference carries no user content, and a read-only viewer is precisely who needs to cite one.

**Storage.** `structure_node.reference` is `String?` with `@@unique([userId, reference])` — nullable so the column is additive on a populated table and so a disabled type stores nothing, and Postgres tolerates any number of NULLs in a unique index. The counter is its own model, `structure_reference_counter`, keyed `@@unique([userId, nodeType, period])`, where `lastValue` is the highest number **issued** and `period` is the four-digit year for a yearly-reset type and `''` otherwise. It is deliberately **not** a column on `structure_node_type`: a high-water mark is runtime state, and holding it in the type config would carry one environment's issued numbers into another through config export/import.

**Assignment is a single statement** — `INSERT … ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE … RETURNING` — run inside the same transaction as the node create. Two concurrent creates cannot receive the same number, and a failed create burns none. A duplicate that copies children takes one block allocation per node type rather than a round trip per row; a bulk import allocates per row inside that row's own transaction, so one failing row cannot abort the numbers already handed out.

**Immutable once assigned**, and the schemas are what make it so:

| path | reference |
|---|---|
| `POST /nodes`, public API create | Key stripped by the schema — never settable |
| Every update, bulk status and metadata path | No update schema carries the key at all |
| `POST /nodes/bulk` (the module's import door) | **Optional** `reference`, accepted only from an admin session or an in-process system caller |

That one exception exists so a migration keeps its existing numbers. An imported value is validated (`^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._/-]*$`, 100 characters) and pushes the type's counter past itself, so a later assignment cannot reissue it.

**Existing entries are numbered on enable, not by the migration.** `referenceConfig` is a new column defaulting to `{}`, so at the moment the v1.35.0 SQL runs on an upgrading host nothing has references switched on and its backfill matches zero rows. The moment that actually matters is the config flip: `updateNodeType` — and a JSON config import, which is a first-class way to switch them on — numbers that type's existing entries in `createdAt, id` order and seeds the counter above the highest value assigned. It runs on the caller's own transaction, so it commits, or rolls back, with the write that enabled it (an import's dry-run abort included). Only `reference IS NULL` rows are candidates, so enable → disable → re-enable never renumbers an entry that already has one.

<Aside type="caution" title="Schema change">
One column on `structure_node`, one on `structure_node_type`, and the new `structure_reference_counters` table. `npx structure update` generates and applies the migration. The column is nullable and the config defaults to `{}`, so every existing entry and type is unaffected until an admin switches a type on.
</Aside>

<Aside type="note" title="The year is UTC, in both places">
The runtime uses `getUTCFullYear()`, and the migration's `to_char("createdAt", 'YYYY')` is *already* the UTC year: `createdAt` is a `timestamp` **without** time zone written in UTC, and `to_char` over a plain timestamp performs no conversion. Adding `AT TIME ZONE 'UTC'` there would promote the value to `timestamptz` and make the year follow the session zone — that would be the bug rather than the fix. The two must agree, or an entry numbered by the backfill lands in a different period from the one it would have been given at create time.
</Aside>

**Also in this release: node revision history was built, then reverted before shipping.** A revisions model, a prior-state snapshot in `updateNode`, `getNodeRevisions` / `restoreNodeRevision`, routes, hooks and erasure were written in full on 2026-08-20 and then deliberately taken back out. Record versioning is likely field/type-based and belongs with document-management, which already owns file chain-of-custody and versioning; a second parallel history here would duplicate it, and the requirement is not yet understood well enough to build against (diffing a JSON `data` blob, restore versus view, per-field versus per-record). Nothing shipped, so there is nothing to migrate — it is recorded under [Parked](/modules/structure/roadmap/#parked--not-planned), and D8 is marked superseded on the [Decisions](/modules/structure/decisions/) page. The `auditEvents` contract half from v1.34.2 stays.

See [Configuration](/modules/structure/configuration/) for the full `referenceConfig` reference.

---

## v1.34.28

**Fix: hierarchy rules were not enforced on every node-creating path** *(generated-output)*

`bulkCreateNodes` never called `enforceHierarchyRules`, so `POST /nodes/bulk` ignored `allowedChildren`, `allowedParents`, `maxChildren` and `maxDepth` entirely. It ran its own per-row permission check, parent-state resolution and field validation — which is why the gap was easy to miss — but nothing checked whether the row was allowed to sit where it was being put. The named consumer of that endpoint is a bulk writer (cognitive-pipeline), which made it the most likely route for a node to land somewhere the type rules forbid. `duplicateNode` had the same hole on its re-parenting path.

- **`bulkCreateNodes`** now enforces per row, inside the existing `try`, so a failing row becomes that row's entry in `errors` and the batch continues — the partial-success contract is unchanged. Per-type rules are memoised alongside the existing field-def and permission caches, so a 500-row batch pays one extra type read rather than 500. The `maxChildren` count stays per row by necessity: rows commit sequentially and each must see what the previous ones created.
- **`duplicateNode`** enforces only when `targetParentId` is supplied. An in-place duplicate is deliberately left ungated — it copies a node beside itself, so `allowedParents`, `allowedChildren` and `maxDepth` are satisfied by construction, and enforcing the one rule it could breach (`maxChildren`) would start rejecting duplicates that succeed today. That call is recorded as an open question on the [Roadmap](/modules/structure/roadmap/#decisions-needed).
- **`moveNode`'s `maxDepth` check** now measures the deepest descendant of the moved subtree rather than the moved node alone — but only for **deepening** moves. A sideways or shallower move cannot newly breach the limit, and measuring there would reject an already-deep subtree on the very operation that might be fixing it.

Every other node-writing path was audited and left alone with reasons recorded: `restoreNode` and the bulk status methods change only `status`, `updateNode` cannot re-parent (its input carries no `parentId`), `importConfigBundle` writes type configuration and creates no nodes, and connections are edges governed by their own connection-type rules.

---

## v1.34.27

**Feature: shared node types, and the Scope section** *(generated-output)*

The node-type form's **Primary Scope** section is now just **Scope** (heading only — the *Use as the primary scope* toggle and its *Enforce isolation* child are untouched), and it gains a second option: **Shared across all users**, stored as `primaryScope.global`.

A shared type's entries are **read-shared**: every user reads the same rows, and the type is exempt from enforced-space confinement, so it stays visible in every space and is a valid connection target from any space. Use it for reference data the whole app draws on.

**Writes are never widened.** A user can only ever modify entries they own — edit, archive, restore, delete, duplicate, favourite, move, add-child, bulk — at **any** permission tier, with **no admin bypass**. The `permissions` tiers only narrow who may create their own entries; they never widen who may change someone else's. A non-owner gets a genuinely read-only view: the detail header carries a lock and a **Read-only** marker, and every write control is hidden rather than offered and left to fail.

**Sharing requires an app-level type row** (`structure_node_type.userId === null`). Turning the toggle on migrates the row; the flag on a per-user row is inert. This is the property that stops one account's configuration admitting another account's rows of a same-named type, and it is enforced through a single `resolveSharingTransition` decision point shared by the admin form, `PATCH /types/:slug` and JSON config import.

**Shared and primary scope are mutually exclusive.** The form hides the shared toggle once primary scope is on, and confirms before clearing a shared flag you had already set; the server resolves the same contradiction (`isPrimary` wins) for configurations that arrive by import, where the form then offers a clear-only control.

**What a second user does not get:**

| | Behaviour for a non-owner |
|---|---|
| Secret-typed fields | Stripped — the keys are **absent**, not blanked |
| `embeddingText` | Never returned |
| `settings` / `metadata` | Returned as `{}` |
| `isFavorite` | Forced `false`; the Favourites filter returns owned rows only |
| Chunks | `metadata` blanked, and the chunk **omitted entirely** when the source type declares any secret field |

**Edges are never shared.** Connections stay per-user rows: on a shared node you see only your own edges, and may create, promote, restore and delete your own. Deleting or restoring a shared node deliberately sweeps referencing edges across owners so the two stay symmetric.

**Guards that refuse rather than half-apply.** Turning sharing on is refused when another user already owns a type with that slug; turning it off is refused while other users hold live entries; `DELETE /types/:slug` counts entries across all owners for a shared type; and a replace-mode import fails naming the slug and count rather than sweeping a shared type away.

<Aside type="caution" title="Public API">
`features.publicApi.enabled` combined with sharing means every user's entries of that type are reachable through **one** user's API key — sanitised as above, but reachable. Deliberate, and worth understanding before combining the two toggles.
</Aside>

<Aside type="note" title="Known limitations">
`dataFilters` run against the raw `data` column before sanitisation, so an equality probe matches a sealed envelope's *shape*, not plaintext — legacy values written before a field was marked secret are the residual risk until the next write seals them. `restoreNode` does not revive descendants that `deleteNode` soft-deleted (pre-existing). The node-type config cache has a 15-second per-process TTL for the shared-slug list, bounded safe in both directions because newly-sharing is additive and un-sharing is refused while other users hold entries. Two admins racing to create the same shared slug can both pass the uniqueness check under Postgres READ COMMITTED; closing that needs a database-level constraint.
</Aside>

Every helper short-circuits when no type is shared, emitting byte-identical SQL and Prisma predicates — so an app that flags nothing shared behaves exactly as it did before. Validated with `check:templates`, `build`, `conformance:check` and `parity:check`, plus a host `tsc --noEmit` reporting zero errors under `features/structure` in both backend and frontend.

See [Shared types](/modules/structure/configuration/#shared-types-global-true) for the configuration reference and [Usage](/modules/structure/usage/) for what an admin sees.

---

## v1.34.26

**Fix: clearing an icon or colour never saved** *(generated-output)*

Reported as *"save on navigation groups doesn't seem to do anything"* — and it was not specific to groups. Clearing an **icon** or **colour** silently did nothing on **node types**, **connection types** and **navigation groups** alike. Setting a value worked; removing one did not.

**Why.** The admin forms mapped a cleared picker to `undefined` (`color || undefined`, `icon ?? undefined`). `undefined` is omitted from the request body, survives the `.partial()` Zod schema as "absent", and reaches Prisma — where **`undefined` means *leave this column alone***. So the update ran, returned 200 and toasted success, while the old colour stayed in the database. Reopening the form showed it again.

**Fix.** A cleared picker now sends an **empty string**, and the service translates that to an explicit `NULL` via a new `clearable()` helper. The three states are now distinct and each does what it says:

| submitted | meaning | result |
|---|---|---|
| `undefined` | field not part of this update | column untouched |
| `''` | user cleared it | column set to `NULL` |
| a value | user set it | column set to that value |

Applied to `updateNodeType`, `updateConnectionType` and `updateNodeTypeGroup` (icon + colour), and to the matching create paths so a never-set icon/colour is stored as `NULL` rather than an empty string.

<Aside type="note">
Verified against a real database before release: an `undefined` write left an existing colour (`#a855f7`) in place — reproducing the reported symptom exactly — while the new `NULL` write cleared it, with the original value restored afterwards.
</Aside>

---

## v1.34.25

**Fix: App Structure failed with "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'icon')"** *(generated-output)*

A regression from **v1.34.21**, reported from a live app. That release made a type appear in the sidebar unless explicitly switched off (`mainNav?.enabled !== false`) — which correctly admits types that have **no `navigation.mainNav` block at all**. But the line that builds the nav item still asserted one existed:

```ts
const mainNav = nodeType.navigation.mainNav!;   // ! was safe under the old filter
const iconName = mainNav.icon ?? nodeType.icon ?? 'Folder';   // threw here
```

So the first such type threw on `mainNav.icon`. Fixed by dropping the assertion and falling back per field (`mainNav?.icon ?? nodeType.icon ?? 'Folder'`, and likewise for label / order / showCount).

**Why a sidebar bug blanked the admin page.** The navigation registration runs from `nodeTypeRegistry`'s sync listener, and that sync happens **inside `useNodeTypes`' `queryFn`** — so the throw propagated out of the query itself. Every page reading node types saw a failed query; App Structure rendered its error state instead of the type list, and the sidebar registered nothing.

That coupling is now fixed too: **`notifySync` isolates its listeners**, so a throwing subscriber can no longer fail the fetch that triggered it. The registry's data is already updated and correct at that point — only the subscriber's side effect fails, and the other subscribers still run. A registration bug degrades the sidebar instead of taking down every page.

<Aside type="note">
Verified against a real app's data before release: all 24 enabled types run through the fixed registration with **zero throws**, and the four types with no `mainNav` config resolve their own icons and land in their configured group sections.
</Aside>

---

## v1.34.24

**AI context for connection types — closes the Phase 2 connection half** *(generated-output, schema change)*

Connection types can now describe themselves to AI the same way entry types have since v1.30.0. This is the deferred half of Phase 2 ("context / `aiFields` on connection types"), and it is what the v1.34.23 connection-type tabs were missing.

**New storage** on `structure_connection_type`, mirroring the node-type pair exactly:

- `contextHints` (`String?`) — plain-language notes about what the relationship means.
- `aiFields` (`Json`, default `{}`) — `{ fields?, promptSegments?, outputExamples? }`, the same `AiFieldsConfig` shape node types use.

Structure is **storage + exposure only**, as with node types: it persists and returns these, AI Core consumes them. Nothing in Structure reads them at runtime.

**Wired end to end** — the gap this release closes is that every one of these had to be done for the config to be real:

- `createConnectionType` / `updateConnectionType` persist both fields; `listConnectionTypes` / `getConnectionType` return them.
- The create/update **Zod schemas** accept them, so API and cognitive-pipeline writes are not silently dropped.
- The **config bundle** carries them: export reads through `listConnectionTypes`, and the import mapping writes both, so a bundle round-trips. Verified by diffing the schema's columns against what the import actually writes — 19 connection-type config columns, all covered.
- `ConnectionTypeConfig` gains `contextHints` / `aiFields` on both the frontend and backend type.

**The AI tab** appears in the connection-type editor between Fields and Display, and only when **AI Core is installed** (`useModuleInstalled('ai-core')`, same gate and same fail-closed behaviour as the node-type AI tab — if presence flips while the tab is open it falls back to Basic). It holds **AI Fields** (the shared field editor, ids namespaced `ai_field_N` so they never collide with the connection's own fields) and **AI Context** (the freeform hints).

<Aside type="caution">
**Schema change:** two columns are added to `structure_connection_type`. `npx structure update` generates and applies the migration; both columns are nullable/defaulted, so existing connection types are unaffected.
</Aside>

---

## v1.34.23

**Connection types get the node-type tab treatment** *(generated-output)*

The connection-type editor was a single long scroll. It now uses the same tab structure as the node-type form:

- **Basic** — slug, name, plural name, description, **Position** (the positional dropdown from v1.34.22), and **Enabled**.
- **Properties** — how it behaves (**Two-way**, **One per pair**, **Most per entry**) *and* what can be connected (**From** / **To** entry types). Endpoint rules live here rather than in a separate tab: a connection type's constraints are its properties.
- **Fields** — the shared field editor plus attached field templates.
- **Display** — icon and colour, i.e. how the type appears on connection badges, in the Add Connection dialog, and in lists.

**Two controls upgraded on the way through**

- **From / To entry types** were free-text boxes where you typed comma-separated slugs, with no validation and no idea what was available. They are now the same **Add + list** control (`TypeListField`) the node type's Hierarchy tab uses, listing your actual entry types.
- **Shared field templates** was a badge-cloud; it is now that same Add + list control, so attachment order is visible and adjustable.

`TypeListField` moved out of `NodeTypeForm.tsx` into `components/admin/TypeListField.tsx` so both forms share one implementation.

**Fix caught during the rewrite**

Clearing the From, To, or attached-templates list now persists. `updateConnectionType` passes these straight to Prisma, where `undefined` means *leave the column unchanged* — so sending `undefined` for an emptied list would have silently kept the old value. The form now always sends the array, and an empty array means "any type" / "none attached", which is what every read path already assumed.

**Import/export verified, not assumed**

Every configuration column on `structure_node_type` (34) and `structure_connection_type` (17) was checked against what the config-bundle import actually writes: full coverage on both, with the only gaps being proposal plumbing and the two columns set on the create branch. Nothing in this release or v1.34.22 adds a column — ordering lives in the existing `sortOrder` / `navigation.mainNav.order`, group membership in `groupSlug` — so exported bundles round-trip unchanged.

<Aside type="note">
The **AI** tab is not here yet. Connection types have no `aiFields` / context storage — that is the deferred Phase 2 connection-type half, and it needs a schema column, service and import/export work before a tab can do anything real. Building the tab first would have shipped a control that silently does nothing.
</Aside>

---

## v1.34.22

**Order by position, not by number — and Groups became two-way** *(generated-output)*

Admins no longer type sort numbers anywhere in App Structure. A new shared control pair (`components/admin/OrderControls.tsx`) replaces every raw order input:

- **`OrderButtons`** — first / up / down / last, for a list you can see. Used on the **Groups** list and the **Connection Types** list.
- **`PositionSelect`** — **First / After &lt;name&gt; / Last**, for a form where you are editing one item and cannot see the list. Used on the node type's **Sidebar position** (replacing the 0–100 slider), the **Group** form, the **Connection Type** form, and all eight module-integration order fields on the Integrations tab.

The stored `sortOrder` / `navigation.mainNav.order` numbers are unchanged — the controls compute them (midpoint insertion, stepping past the ends), so existing config and the JSON import/export format still work exactly as before.

**Groups now edit membership from both sides**

The Groups tab previously showed a **read-only** badge list of assigned types with a note telling you to go and set the group on each type. It is now a real editor:

- **Add type** picks any type into the group (showing which group it is currently in, if any); the **×** on a row removes it. Both write the same `groupSlug` on the node type that the type's own Basic tab writes — one setting, two places to edit it.
- Each row carries **OrderButtons** that set the type's position *inside* that group's sidebar section.
- A type's **Sidebar position** dropdown lists only the siblings that share its sidebar location — the other types in its group, or the other ungrouped types — so "After Evidence" always means what it looks like.

**Also**

- The admin **Components** tab is hidden (`SHOW_COMPONENTS_TAB`), parked until component code can be authored externally and bound to merge fields. Nothing is removed.

<Aside type="note">
Ordering is still stored as numbers, so a config bundle exported before this release imports unchanged. Two items that happen to share an order value sort by name, and moving either one re-separates them.
</Aside>

---

## v1.34.21

**Fix: node types marked "Show in sidebar" could be missing from the sidebar** *(generated-output)*

**The bug.** The admin form seeded its **Show in sidebar** toggle with `navigation.mainNav.enabled ?? true`, so a type whose stored config had no explicit `mainNav.enabled` **displayed as ON** — while the navigation registration filtered on `mainNav?.enabled` being *truthy*, and treated the same missing value as OFF. Any type that never went through a form save — created by **config import**, by seeding, or through the API — was therefore shown as "in the sidebar" in App Structure while being silently absent from it. Field-confirmed against a real app: 4 of 24 types had a null `mainNav.enabled`, every one of them assigned to a navigation group (so clearly intended for the sidebar), and none of them rendered.

- **Fixed:** the registration now filters on `mainNav?.enabled !== false`, matching the form's displayed default. A type is in the sidebar unless it has been explicitly switched off. Types already saved with `enabled: false` are unaffected.

**Two related navigation-wiring fixes found in the same pass**

- **Groups now sync into the registry whenever they are fetched.** `useNodeTypeGroups` mirrors its result into `nodeTypeRegistry` the way `useNodeTypes` already did for types. Previously only the boot-time prime populated groups, so if that prime lost its auth race, every grouped type registered against a sidebar section that was never created — and the host **auto-creates** a section for an unknown id, producing a stray heading like "Structure group resources" instead of the group's real name.
- **A type never points at a section that does not exist.** If a type's `groupSlug` has no matching group in the registry, its nav item is registered without a `sidebarSection` (so it appears in the main list) rather than referencing a section the module never registered. `syncGroups` also notifies sync listeners now, so groups arriving after types (they are separate requests) re-run the navigation registration instead of leaving grouped types flat until the next type sync.

**Also**

- The admin **Components** tab is hidden for now (`SHOW_COMPONENTS_TAB = false` in `NodeTypesAdminPage`). Nothing is removed — the component-template service, the tab's content, and every `*TemplateId` config keep working; the tab returns when component code can be authored externally and bound to Structure merge fields.

<Aside type="caution">
**Behaviour change:** a node type with **no explicit** `navigation.mainNav.enabled` now appears in the sidebar (previously hidden). This is the intended default — it is what the admin UI has always displayed — but if an app relied on importing types that stayed out of the sidebar without ever being switched off, set `navigation.mainNav.enabled: false` on those types.
</Aside>

## v1.34.20

**Performance pass on the generated code — 33 fixes from the 2026-08-18 audit** *(generated-output)*

An eight-dimension performance audit of the generated output (41 raw findings → 34 verified after adversarial checking) drove this release. Everything below is a change to the code the templates generate; no config or admin UI changed shape. Host-tsc validated in a real app (0 errors, frontend + backend). The full register lives on the [roadmap](/modules/structure/roadmap/#archive).

**Typing and rendering**

- **List-page field filters are debounced** (300 ms) like the search box, and `useNodes` forwards TanStack's **abort signal**, so a superseded filter request is cancelled instead of running to completion server-side. Typing an 8-character filter value used to fire 8 uncancelled JSONB-filtered queries.
- **Field editors stop re-serialising the form on every keystroke.** `useResolvedTemplate` / `useResolvedFormula` only stringify the data bag when the field actually has a template/formula; `NodeFieldRenderer` is `memo()`'d; `NodeInfoTab` and `NodeForm` build one shared `allData` object (not one per field) and pass it **only** to fields that depend on other fields, with stable per-field `onChange` handlers.
- **Tree view is virtualised.** `StructureTree` windows its flat row list with `@tanstack/react-virtual` (new frontend dependency) and renders `memo()`'d rows, so a 5,000-node tree no longer mounts 5,000 DOM rows or re-renders all of them on every arrow key. Tree search is debounced 300 ms; keyboard navigation scrolls the target row into view before focusing it.
- **The Add Connection dialog owns its own state**, so typing in a connection field no longer re-renders every connection badge behind it.
- **Lists keep the previous page visible** while the next one loads (`placeholderData: keepPreviousData` + a dimmed placeholder state) instead of flashing to a full-pane spinner on every page/filter/sort change.

**Backend queries**

- **List search uses the existing `searchVector` GIN index** (`@@ to_tsquery` with prefix terms) instead of an unindexable `jsonb_each_text` + `ILIKE` scan of every owned row. A self-healing fallback re-runs the old scan if the column/index cannot be provisioned on that host.
- **Node-type config is resolved once per request**, not seven times: a short-TTL, version-stamped cache behind `loadEnforcedScopes` / `unreadableTypesFor` / `getFieldDefs` / `loadTypeGraph` / `loadFieldTemplateFields`, invalidated by every type / field-template / import / proposal mutation. Transaction-client reads bypass it.
- **Sorting by a custom field** fetches only that field's value per row (`data->>'field'`) instead of every matching row's full `data` blob; the `dataFilters` id pre-query is capped at 5,000 like the search arm.
- **Hierarchy reads are bounded and narrower**: `getTree` selects only the columns the tree renders, `getNodeChildren` / `getNodeDescendants` take a cap, and connection endpoint rows omit `settings` / `metadata`.
- **Bulk archive / delete / restore** prefetch their rows and per-type permissions in two `IN` queries instead of 3–6 queries per id; **`bulkCreateNodes`** memoises per-parent state (one existence + path + `MAX(sortOrder)` lookup per distinct parent); **`duplicateNode`** inserts a subtree with chunked `createMany` instead of one INSERT per descendant (which risked the 5 s interactive-transaction timeout).
- **Public API**: `GET`/`PATCH /:type/:id` no longer hydrate every child row just to discard them (new `getNode({ includeChildren: false })`), and `GET /:type/schema` resolves referenced types concurrently off one cached type graph.

**Caching and bundle**

- **Mutations invalidate what they actually change.** Archive / restore / reorder use a narrower invalidation, and **toggling a favourite** patches the cached node via `setQueryData` instead of refetching seven query prefixes.
- **Card merge-field dereferences go through the shared query cache**, so N cards referencing the same node fetch it once rather than once per card.
- **The admin page is lazy-loaded** (`lazy(() => import(…))` in its registration) — ~6,600 lines of admin UI plus `@dnd-kit` no longer parse at boot for every user. The settings page and detail tabs stay eager (their hosts render them without a Suspense boundary).
- **The feature barrel no longer statically re-exports the routed pages**, which had silently defeated the `lazy()` route split.
- **Icons now go through the starter's shared icon picker** (`IconRenderer`, and `IconPickerDialog` in place of the module's own picker grid) at seven of the eight former `import * as LucideIcons` sites. The eighth — the navigation registration — needs a component *reference* for `navigationRegistry.registerMain` / `registerSection` rather than rendered JSX, so it resolves names against lucide's `icons` map directly, mirroring `IconRenderer`'s own resolution. That gives one icon owner for everything the module renders; it does **not** shrink the bundle on its own, because the starter's `IconRenderer` statically imports lucide's full `icons` map — that half is [logged as a starter upstream issue](/starter-template/upstream-issues/).
- **New `POST /types/reorder`** applies a whole admin drag-drop order in one request/transaction instead of one PATCH per node type.

<Aside type="caution">
**Type change:** `NodeTreeItem.node` is now a lightweight **`NodeTreeNode`** (the structural + presentation columns the tree renders, plus `data`) rather than a full `StructureNode`, and the vestigial `isExpanded` / `isSelected` fields were removed. Consumers reading `description` / `settings` / `metadata` off a tree node should fetch it with `getNode` instead. `resolveNodeColor`, `getNodeFieldValue` and `NodeIcon` accept the narrower shape.
</Aside>

<Aside type="note">
`@tanstack/react-virtual` is a new frontend dependency (declared in `module.ts`); `npx structure update` installs it.
</Aside>

## v1.34.19

**Pages tab: wired the dead/partial controls + a full clarity pass** *(generated-output)*

The node-type **Pages** tab got the same treatment as the other clarity-pass tabs — plainer wording, tighter grouping — plus a trust-fix pass that made three declared-but-dead/partial controls actually affect rendering. The wired-vs-dead classification came from a render-side wiring audit before anything was touched.

**Fixes (behaviour)**

- **Sort by** was broken. The form stored friendly values (`created` / `updated` / `manual`) that the backend list query rejects — its enum is `name` / `createdAt` / `updatedAt` / `sortOrder` — so picking anything other than **Name** returned a `400` and the whole list failed to load. A form→backend sort-value map now translates them (and tolerates legacy/unset values), so all four options work, including **Manual order** → `sortOrder`.
- **List-item layout** (`listItemTemplateId`) was **dead** — persisted and exported but no renderer read it. The compact-list row (`NodeListItem`) now honours the assigned template's **show icon / description / colour / fields**, mirroring how cards honour `cardTemplateId`. Unconfigured types render exactly as before (icon + name only).
- **Overview layout** widgets were **partial** — only the 1-/2-column layout applied; the template's per-widget config was ignored. `NodeOverviewTab` now honours **enable** (hide a widget), **order**, and **left/right column** placement. Types with no widget config keep the original auto-flow grid.

**Clarity pass**

- All three badge-clouds — **Filter by field**, **Table columns**, **Fields on the card** — became the reusable **Add + list** control (`TypeListField`) from v1.34.18, which adds the **ordering** they never had. The Table-columns list preserves each column's label / sortable / width when you reorder or remove.
- Plainer labels and help throughout (e.g. "Navigation style" → "Section navigation"; "Show filters" → "Show status filter", since it only gates the status dropdown; the "Single page (no tabs)" option relabelled "One section only" to match what it renders).
- Closed two option gaps: **Opens on** now offers **Connections**, and **Default view** now offers **Tree** — both already supported by the renderer.

<Aside type="note">
The wired-vs-dead classification came from a 4-agent audit that traced every Pages-tab config key to its render site (file:line evidence). An adversarial review then checked the change for regressions before ship — it confirmed the defaults reproduce the original rows, the sort map never emits a value outside the backend enum, and the columns adapter preserves per-column config. Three minor items were fixed: a path-spacing regression in the list row, `boolean`-type fields now render **Yes/No** like `toggle`, and the two-column split only activates when a widget is actually assigned to the **right** column (avoiding an empty column). **Host-tsc validated clean in veradai** — 0 `features/structure` errors, frontend + backend.
</Aside>

## v1.34.18

**Hierarchy tab redesign — Children-first, with auto-derived Parents** *(generated-output)*

The node-type Hierarchy tab was reworked around one clear model (Kim-driven): containment is defined from **one** side.

- **What entries can contain (Children)** — now an editable **"Add type" + list** (with ↑↓ reorder and remove) instead of a badge cloud. This is the **source of truth** for nesting; self-nesting is an option in the Add menu.
- **Parents** — **auto-derived, read-only**: it shows the types whose "Can contain" already lists this one, so you never enter the same relationship twice.
- **"Based on"** (renamed from "Type inheritance"; description made generic — no app-specific examples) moved to the **bottom in a collapsed accordion** — it's advanced/rarely-needed. It auto-expands and shows a count when a type already has base types.
- All the type pickers (Children, Based on, Scoped types) now share one reusable **Add + list** control.
- The old editable "Where entries can live" (`allowedParents`) picker is gone — Parents derives from Children instead. Existing `allowedParents` values are preserved and still enforced.

<Aside type="note">
A 3-lens adversarial review (correctness / data-integrity / UX) with per-finding verification ran before ship. It caught that a legacy `allowedParents` value is still enforced server-side but would become invisible/uneditable once its picker was removed — fixed by surfacing any such value read-only in the Parents section with a **"Clear legacy restriction"** button. Also fixed: the "Based on" accordion now opens (and badges a count) when inheritance is already configured, and the empty-Parents message no longer stacks two lines or misreads a self-nesting type. Data-integrity verified `allowedChildren`/`superClasses`/`scopedTypes` round-trip with no clobber. Module gates clean; **host-tsc validated clean in veradai** — 0 `features/structure` errors, frontend + backend.
</Aside>

## v1.34.17

**Per-type creation entry points (sidebar / header toolbar) + fixed quick-create** *(generated-output)*

Node types gain **creation entry points**: pick where a **"+ New"** appears for a top-level type.

- **Config:** new `rules.createFrom { sidebar?, headerToolbar? }`, gated by the top-level toggle. No migration — it rides the existing config blob.
- **Basic tab:** the old "Can be created standalone" toggle left the Hierarchy tab and became a **"Creating entries"** section — **"Can be created at the top level"** plus, when on, **Header toolbar** (defaults on) and **Sidebar** (opt-in) sub-toggles.
- **Header:** the existing "+ New ▾" dropdown now honours `createFrom.headerToolbar`.
- **Sidebar:** an opted-in type's row becomes an expandable group with an **All** sub-link (the list) and a **New** sub-link (quick-create).
- **Bug fix:** the pre-existing `?action=create` intent was dead — the header quick-create that shipped earlier never actually opened the create form (`NodeListPage` ignored the param). It's wired up now, so quick-create works from both surfaces.

<Aside type="note">
A 3-lens adversarial review (runtime / data-integrity / UX) with per-finding verification ran before ship. The data-integrity lens verified `createFrom` round-trips with no clobber. Four fixes were applied: the create sheet render is now gated on the create permission (a hard-load race could otherwise show the form to a non-permitted user; the reviewer's effect-level fix was rejected because it would also have broken permitted hard-loads — the URL-reflection effect strips the param during the loading render); the sidebar emits **two** sub-items because the host renders any parent-with-children as a *non-navigating* collapse group, so an "All" sub-item preserves the path to the list; and two wording fixes (a header-vs-sidebar description mismatch and an orphaned "standalone"). Known minor: using quick-create while on the same filtered list drops the filter query-params from the URL (state keeps them; self-heals on the next filter change). Module gates clean; **host-tsc validated clean in veradai** — 0 `features/structure` errors, frontend + backend.
</Aside>

## v1.34.15

**AI tab gating via the host module-status hook** *(generated-output)*

The node-type **AI tab** (AI Fields + AI Context) now decides its visibility through the starter's runtime module-status API — `useModuleInstalled('ai-core')` from `@/features/modules` (starter **v2.6.2+**) — replacing the deep import of `useModulesOverview` from `@/features/admin/hooks/useModulesAdmin` (an admin-only query, outside any public barrel). This closes the upstream issue the v1.34.10 code comment flagged: the starter now exposes a stable presence API.

`useModuleInstalled` is a presence check backed by the non-admin `GET /api/v1/modules/status`, with no fetch of its own; it **fail-closes to `false`** until presence is positively confirmed (so the tab never flashes) and on hosts below v2.6.2 (no provider) the tab simply stays hidden.

<Aside type="note">
**Behaviour change:** gating is now on **installed** (presence) rather than installed-*and*-enabled — an admin who has installed but *disabled* ai-core will now see the AI tab. Swap to `useModuleEnabled` (same barrel) to also hide it when disabled. **Requires the app on starter v2.6.2+** (`npm run template:update`); below that the `@/features/modules` import won't resolve. Host-tsc validated clean in veradai (v2.6.2+) — 0 `features/structure` errors, frontend + backend.
</Aside>

This closes the recorded "Hierarchy is a catch-all" backlog item; the tab now holds only inheritance + parent/child nesting + spaces. The **Allow moving between spaces** rule — previously a loose row — now sits under that same Spaces header. No config keys changed; `primaryScope`/`governance` were already seeded in the form so the whole-object save can't wipe them.

The displayed value and slider are clamped together so a legacy out-of-range order can't show a thumb/readout mismatch. Adds the `slider` shadcn component to the module (installed on `structure update`).

A 3-lens adversarial review (correctness · data-integrity/seeding · UX) with per-finding verification ran before ship: the seeding lens confirmed no whole-object-PATCH clobber (both `primaryScope` and `governance` are seeded), and three fixes were applied — the slider display/value clamp, filtering unnamed levels out of the **Default level** dropdown (a pre-existing orphan-default quirk in the re-added block), and the cross-tab pointer wording. Module `check:templates` + type-check + build clean; **host-tsc validated clean in veradai** — 0 `features/structure` errors (frontend + backend); only the 4 pre-existing cognitive-pipeline / document-management errors remain.

## v1.34.13

**Per-type Read enforcement — Structure-side half for the retrieval (RAG) arm** *(generated-output)*

Closes the v1.34.12 tracked follow-up. The serviceRegistry-only retrieval methods — the embeddings search arm and the `expandGraph` graph primitive — gain an opt-in `excludeNodeTypes` filter, so a consumer that serves results to an end user can drop read-restricted node types. This is the exact shape of the RAG `scopeRootId` half (v1.34.2), which confines results to one enforced space.

- **Embeddings arm.** All six methods (`semanticSearchNodes`, `findSimilarNodes`, `semanticSearchChunks`, `lexicalSearchChunks`, `hybridSearchNodes`, `hybridSearchChunks`) accept `excludeNodeTypes?: string[]` and AND a self-contained `"nodeType" NOT IN (…)` predicate — no cross-file helper. The chunk methods gate on the *source node's* type (chunk content **is** node text — the highest-sensitivity surface). `findSimilarNodes` additionally returns `[]` when the *source* node's own type is excluded, so a read-restricted node can't seed a similarity leak.
- **Graph arm.** `expandGraph` (`GraphExpandOptions.excludeNodeTypes`) drops reached nodes of excluded types — appended to the outer node join, so traversal still passes *through* them but they never surface.
- **Consumer contract.** These methods are system-tier and cannot compute the unreadable set themselves, so a new `structureService.getUnreadableTypes(userId, { isAdmin, isSystem })` returns the caller's read-restricted type slugs (admin / system → `[]`). A trusted consumer (cognitive-pipeline / ai-core) resolves it for the **end user** and passes it as `excludeNodeTypes`, exactly as it passes the active-space `scopeRootId`.

<Aside type="note">
Additive only — every new param is opt-in, no schema change, and no behaviour change for existing callers (an omitted or empty `excludeNodeTypes` is a no-op). This is the Structure-side half; the consuming engine still has to fetch the set and pass it, which stays the open coordination follow-up (tracked on cognitive-pipeline / ai-core). Module `check:templates` + type-check + build clean; **host-tsc validated clean in veradai** — 0 `features/structure` errors (frontend + backend); only the 4 pre-existing cognitive-pipeline / document-management errors remain.
</Aside>

## v1.34.12

**Per-type Read permission, enforced server-side** *(generated-output)*

Node types gain a **Read** permission (Anyone / Admin only) alongside create/edit/archive/delete, in the Permissions section. Admin-only read means entries of that type are hidden from everyone else — everywhere.

Enforcement is server-side across every read path, so a non-admin never sees a read-restricted type's entries:

- **Single-entry reads** (`getNode`, path / children / descendants, node context, connections) → 404 when the entry's type is read-restricted (its existence isn't revealed); read-blocked related entries (children, ancestors, connection endpoints, childCounts) are filtered out.
- **Lists** (`listNodes`, tree, keyword search) → read-restricted types are excluded from the query; hydrated children are filtered too.
- **Public API** (`GET /:type`, `/:type/:id`, `/:type/schema`) → 404 for a read-restricted type even when it's publicApi-enabled (indistinguishable from a non-exposed type); a writable-but-read-restricted type stays writable.

Admins and trusted system callers (e.g. cognitive-pipeline) read everything; the default (no Read set) is fully permissive, so existing apps are unaffected, and a config typo never locks anyone out (only exactly `admin`/`system` restricts).

An adversarial security review caught + fixed two issues before ship: a leak where `getNodeConnections` didn't gate the seed node's own type, and a public-API PATCH lockout on writable read-restricted types.

**Follow-up:** the semantic/hybrid embeddings search arm + `expandGraph` are serviceRegistry-only (system-tier) and get an opt-in `excludeNodeTypes` filter that RAG consumers pass — the same pattern as the RAG `scopeRootId` half. **✅ Shipped v1.34.13.**

Host-tsc validated clean in veradai (0 `features/structure` errors, frontend + backend).

## v1.34.10

**Node-type admin: new AI tab, Navigation consolidated into Basic** *(generated-output)*

Reorganises the node-type form (`NodeTypeForm`) so related settings sit together.

- **New "AI" tab** — shown only when **AI Core** is installed and enabled (detected via the module register's enablement state). It holds:
  - **AI Fields** — a field set defined with the same editor as custom fields but scoped to AI (Structure stores them; AI Core consumes them). Their ids are namespaced (`ai_field_N`) so they never collide with a type's regular fields.
  - **AI Context** — the freeform `contextHints` guidance passed to AI prompt assembly.
- **Sidebar Navigation moved into the Basic tab**, and the duplicate standalone "Navigation Group" control was removed — it now lives once, inside the Navigation section.
- **Node Context stays in Properties.** The structural context toggles (ancestor breadcrumbs, connections, child counts) feed `getNodeContext`, which cognitive modules consume generally — not just AI — so they stay reachable in every app; only the AI-specific hints moved to the AI tab.

**Correctness fixes caught by adversarial review before ship:**

- The form now **seeds `aiFields` / `contextConfig` / `contextHints`** from the saved type, so editing shows real values and the whole-object save no longer wipes stored config (including AI Core's `promptSegments`). This also fixes a pre-existing Context clobber.
- AI-tab visibility uses the module **enablement** state, so a *disabled* AI Core correctly hides the tab (rather than showing it whenever the code is present).

Also logged an [upstream issue](/starter-template/upstream-issues/) requesting a stable frontend "is-module-installed" API — the only current access is an admin-only internal hook.

Host-tsc validated clean in veradai (0 `features/structure` errors, frontend + backend).

## v1.34.9

**Fix: computed / formula fields no longer clobber each other in the same commit** *(generated-output)*

Follow-up to v1.34.8 (finding #6 from its adversarial review). Two computed or formula fields whose values changed in the same render commit could overwrite each other, because both field-change handlers rebuilt the next data object from a stale render-closure snapshot — the second write dropped the first field's value until an unrelated edit. Both handlers now read the latest value: the node form reads from the react-hook-form store (`getValues('data')`), and the detail tab builds from (and keeps in sync) its `pendingDataRef`, so every field written in a burst survives.

Host-tsc validated clean in veradai (0 `features/structure` errors, frontend + backend).

## v1.34.8

**Merge-field picker: 2-layer grouped + searchable, and built-ins now resolve in templates** *(generated-output)*

Closes the field-editing items of the admin-UI clarity pass. The flat merge-field combobox in the field editor (used for `computedTemplate` merge templates and `formula` fields) becomes a **grouped, searchable picker** (`Popover` + `Command`): tokens are organised into **Node basics** (name / description / slug), **This type** (own fields), and one group per **field group** and **referenced field**. Grouping is keyed on the stable field id, so containers with tricky labels never merge or collapse — only the inserted token matters, and that is always correct.

Built-in props (`{name}` / `{description}` / `{slug}`) now **resolve** in a custom field's `computedTemplate` / `formula`: they are merged into the resolution bag in the node form and detail view, mirroring what the node card already did. So a merge template like `{name} — {status}` works, and a type with no custom fields still has bindable tokens. (Connection fields are deliberately unchanged — their create form collects no name/description/slug to bind.)

**Correctness fixes caught by adversarial review before ship:**

- Computed fields no longer **auto-persist on mere view**. A read-only detail view resolves and displays the computed value but never writes it back — previously, once `{name}` resolved non-empty, simply opening a node's Info tab could fire `updateNode` + a `structure.node.updated` audit event with no user edit.
- Computed **built-in** fields (name/description/slug) resolve against custom-field data only, so two mutually-referential computed built-ins (e.g. name=`{slug}`, slug=`{name}`) can no longer oscillate into an infinite update loop.

Host-tsc validated clean in veradai (0 `features/structure` errors, frontend + backend).

## v1.34.5

**Node-type Fields tab restructured into three consistent list sections** *(generated-output)*

Continuing the admin-UI clarity pass. The Fields tab mixed a badge picker, a 3-column grid, and a card list; it's now three sections in one consistent list style, in a clear order:

1. **Built-in Fields** — Name / Description / Slug as a collapsible list (matching the custom-field editor); each row expands to its merge template + Editable-By.
2. **Custom Fields** — the shared `FieldSetEditor` (unchanged).
3. **Shared Field Templates** — attached templates as a **reorderable list**: each row shows its field count and expands to preview the fields; reorder ↑/↓ sets precedence, remove per row; an **"Add shared template"** dropdown offers the unattached templates — replacing the old badge multi-select and the flattened preview.

Inherited-fields preview and Conditional Colour follow. Behaviour is unchanged (same `builtInFieldConfig` and `fieldTemplateIds` data) — this is a layout + interaction-consistency pass. Host-tsc validated clean in veradai (0 `features/structure` errors).

## v1.34.4

**Fix: `useEffect is not defined` crash on the App Structure admin page** *(generated-output)*

The v1.34.2 import-dialog reset (a `useEffect` keyed on the dialog's open state) was added to `NodeTypesAdminPage` but `useEffect` was never added to that file's React import — so the admin page threw `ReferenceError: useEffect is not defined` at render. Added the import. Host `tsc` confirms this was the **only** `features/structure` error across v1.34.2 + v1.34.3 (backend and frontend otherwise clean). *(Reminder: the module's own build can't type-check generated code — run host `tsc` before loading the app; the dev server transpiles without type-checking, so a missing import surfaces only at runtime.)*

## v1.34.3

**Shared field editor extracted to its own component + type-picker "Add Field"** *(generated-output)*

Start of an admin-UI clarity pass.

- **`FieldSetEditor` is now a standalone component** (`components/admin/FieldSetEditor.tsx`), extracted from `NodeTypeForm.tsx` and renamed from `NodeFieldSetEditor` — it isn't node-specific, so the `Node` prefix was misleading. All three surfaces that author fields — node-type fields, connection-type fields, and field templates — import the one primitive (re-exported from the admin barrel). Pure refactor: no behaviour change, one-directional dependency (`NodeTypeForm` → `FieldSetEditor`).
- **"Add Field" is now a type picker.** Instead of always creating a text field and making you change its type afterwards, the button opens a dropdown of every field type, grouped by category (Text & input · References · Media · Layout · Custom), with the module name shown for module-provided types. Clicking a type creates a field of that type and expands it for editing. The empty-state "Add Your First Field" CTA works the same way; the in-editor Type select still lets you change a field's type later.

<Aside type="note">
No schema or API change. A dependency-classification pass plus an independent `noUnusedLocals` verification confirmed the extraction imports cleanly in both generated files (the shared editor's imports and `NodeTypeForm`'s pruned set — 0 unused / 0 missing). Module gates clean; **host tsc pending** (veradai `structure update` → v1.34.3, alongside v1.34.2).
</Aside>

## v1.34.2

**Finish Stage-1/2 partials: audit-event contract, config dry-run/diff, retrieval space opt-in** *(generated-output)*

Three loose ends from the generic-hardening plan, all additive.

- **Audit-event contract (half of S1.6).** The security manifest now declares an `auditEvents` array covering all 50 domain events the module fires through `actionRegistry.doActionAll`: 47 always-on (node / connection / type / group / template / field-template / function / settings lifecycle), plus the three opt-in embeddings events, gated to embeddings installs. A consuming audit/activity module reads the formal list via `securityRegistry.getAuditEvents()` instead of hard-coding hook strings. The hook-doc table was corrected to match the real fire sites — it had drifted (missing bulk archive/delete/restore, field-template, function and config-import rows, and a stale `node.moved` payload that predated the S1.5 `rootNodeId` fields). *(The other half of S1.6 — a `structure_node_revision` table + restore — is still open.)* **— superseded 2026-08-20:** revisions were built in full and then reverted before shipping, on the view that record versioning is likely field/type-based and belongs with document-management. See the v1.35.0 entry, [D8](/modules/structure/decisions/) and [Parked](/modules/structure/roadmap/#parked--not-planned).
- **Config-bundle dry-run / diff preview (S2.1).** `POST /config/import` accepts `dryRun: true`: it runs the real import transaction to compute an exact per-category diff, then rolls it back — nothing is written, no hook fires, and an invalid bundle still surfaces its rejection reason. Results now also carry a per-category **`unchanged`** count (rows that matched but were structurally identical). The admin import dialog gains a **Preview** button showing "N to create · N to update · N unchanged · N to delete" per category before you commit. *(Still open: per-definition config versioning.)*
- **Retrieval space confinement — Structure-side half.** All six embeddings search methods (`semanticSearchNodes`, `findSimilarNodes`, `semanticSearchChunks`, `lexicalSearchChunks`, `hybridSearchNodes`, `hybridSearchChunks`) accept an opt-in `scopeRootId` that confines results to one enforced space via a self-contained `rootNodeId` SQL predicate — no cross-file helper, so it sidesteps the v1.34.1 revert. The consuming engine (cognitive-pipeline / ai-core) still has to pass the active space; that coordination stays the open follow-up.

<Aside type="note">
Additive only — no schema change and no behaviour change for existing callers (every new field/param is opt-in). A 5-dimension adversarial review (cross-constant visibility, dry-run / transaction semantics, retrieval SQL, audit-event accuracy, dialog UX) verified each finding; the three confirmed were all doc/UX polish — a stale-panel-on-reopen bug in the import dialog (the controlled footer button bypassed the reset) and two out-of-date hook-doc rows — and are fixed. Module `check:templates` + type-check + build + conformance clean; **host tsc pending** (veradai `structure update` → v1.34.2).
</Aside>

## v1.34.1

**S1.5 + config-bundle host-tsc fixes** *(generated-output)*

Two cross-file type errors surfaced by the host `tsc` (the dev server transpiles without type-checking, so they were latent until a full type-check):

- **S1.5:** the embeddings/RAG methods (`semanticSearchNodes` / `findSimilarNodes`) live in a *separate* generated file (`structure-embeddings.service.ts`) that cannot see the main service file's scope helpers — so their opt-in / anchored scope references failed to resolve. Reverted: the whole vector/BM25 + chunk retrieval arm's scoping is now one coordinated follow-up (it needs cognitive-pipeline/ai-core to pass the space). The always-on **keyword** arm and the anchored **context** surfaces (`getNodeContext` / `expandGraph` / `getNodeConnections`) remain confined — the primary AI-context path is protected.
- **Config bundle (v1.33.34):** `ImportBundleResult` was defined in the hooks file but named in the service file → moved into the service file (the hook + admin page infer it).

Host-validated clean in veradai (0 `features/structure` errors, backend **and** frontend). No behaviour change.

## v1.34.0

**Enforced scoping — server-side "spaces" (Stage 1 / S1.5)** *(generated-output)*

`primaryScope` was client-side convenience only: the React list page applied a client `pathPrefix`, every other read ignored scope, and the client store could never be a security source of truth — so one space's data (a Matter/Project subtree) could reach a read for another, **including AI context**. This makes isolation a real, server-side boundary.

- **Opt-in per root type:** set `primaryScope.isolation: 'enforced'` (default `'filter'` = the old soft behaviour). With no enforced type, behaviour is unchanged.
- **Denormalised `rootNodeId` column** on `structure_node` (= the enforced-space root — first path segment, self for a root), maintained on create / bulk / duplicate / move with **zero extra queries** (derived from the materialised path). A guarded, idempotent backfill migration populates existing rows.
- **Two confinement modes, fail-closed:**
  - **Ambient** (list / tree / keyword search / `getNode` / node children·descendants·path / `listConnections`): confined to the user's **active space** (read server-side from the persisted `currentScopeNodeId`, validated). No valid space → empty / 404.
  - **Anchored** (`getNodeContext` / `expandGraph` / `getNodeConnections` / `findSimilarNodes`): confined to the **seed node's own space** — the AI-context leak fix. Correct for any caller (no setting/param needed).
- **Connection carve-out:** a connection between two *different* enforced spaces is rejected at creation; connections to scope-exempt reference nodes are allowed.
