- **Cross-space move:** moving a node *out of* an enforced space is rejected unless its type sets `rules.allowCrossSpaceMove` (default off; audited via the `structure.node.moved` payload, which now carries old/new `rootNodeId`). Filing *in* from a non-enforced area is always allowed.
- **Public API:** the per-type read routes accept `?scopeRootId=` (an enforced type requires it; absent/invalid → empty / 400). Trusted in-process callers pass `isSystem` to bypass ambient scoping.
- **Cross-module:** `rootNodeId` now rides node lifecycle payloads (`StructureNode.rootNodeId`) so cognitive-pipeline + document-management inherit the space.
- **Admin UI:** an **Enforce isolation** toggle on the primary-scope editor and an **Allow moving between spaces** toggle on the type's rules. Switching the active space now invalidates structure reads so they re-run against the new space.

<Aside type="caution">
**Behaviour change** (enforced types only): reads confine to the active space and fail closed; cross-space connections and out-of-space moves are blocked (opt-in). A 3-agent adversarial review hardened this — several read surfaces (plural-`types` list, node children/descendants/path, `listConnections`, `findSimilarNodes`) were confined after it flagged them. **Follow-ups:** the vector/BM25 **chunk** retrieval arm (`semanticSearchChunks` / `lexicalSearchChunks` / `hybridSearch*`) still needs the same opt-in confinement — coordinated so cognitive-pipeline/ai-core pass the space; and surfacing a failed scope-switch persist. Module build + `check:templates` + type-check + conformance clean; **host tsc validation pending** (veradai `structure update` → v1.34.0). Additive column + backfill — no breaking schema change.
</Aside>

## v1.33.35

**Config import dialog: "New Import" after success** *(generated-output)*

Follow-up to v1.33.34. After a successful bundle import the primary button still read **Import**, which looked like an invitation to re-run the same data. On success it now becomes **New Import** — clearing the JSON textarea and the results panel so the dialog is ready for a fresh file — alongside **Done** to close. On error, or before the first import, it stays **Import** so you can fix and retry. Closing the dialog (Done, Cancel, or the ✕) now also clears the JSON, so it always reopens empty.

## v1.33.34

**Server-side atomic config-bundle import/export (S2.1 core)** *(generated-output)*

The admin JSON config import was uneven: only **node types** imported server-side atomically, while field templates, groups, and connection types rode client-orchestrated per-item loops — and **functions never travelled at all**. A node-type failure mid-import could leave the other categories half-applied, `replace` mode only pruned node types, and the four per-view component-template ids were silently dropped. This replaces the whole path with one transactional endpoint.

- **New `GET /config/export` + `POST /config/import`** (both admin-only) and `structureService.exportConfigBundle()` / `importConfigBundle()`. The UI's Export/Import buttons now make **one** call each.
- **One transaction, all five categories** — field templates → groups → node types → connection types → **functions** — upserted in dependency order with server-side `fieldTemplateIds` id/slug remapping. **Any failure rolls the whole import back** (all-or-nothing); the dialog shows a **per-category created / updated / deleted** summary, or the rejection reason with nothing written.
- **Functions now round-trip** — formula named expressions (`structure_function`) were previously export/import-invisible, so an imported formula field that called one broke in a fresh space.
- **Component-template ids** (`cardTemplateId` / `listItemTemplateId` / `detailHeaderTemplateId` / `overviewTemplateId`) now round-trip on node-type export/import (they were dropped on both sides before).
- **`replace` mode is now consistent:** it prunes stale rows in **every category present in the bundle** (previously only node types). A category **absent** from the bundle is left untouched — a partial bundle never wipes what it omits. Connection types still spare `isSystem` rows; node types still spare `isSystem` + pending proposals.
- New **`structure.config.imported`** action hook (`{ userId, result, mode }`) fires once after commit.
- The narrower `POST /types/import` (node-types-only) endpoint is retained for backward compatibility.

<Aside type="caution">
**Behaviour change:** import is now **atomic** — an invalid row rejects the entire bundle (a clearer contract than the old silent partial-apply), and `replace` mode now deletes stale field templates / groups / connection types / functions, not just node types. No schema change. Module build + `check:templates` + conformance clean; **host tsc validation pending** (veradai `structure update` → v1.33.34).
</Aside>

## v1.33.33

**Admin node-type list search + quieter config import** *(generated-output)*

Quality-of-life for apps with many node types.

- **Search box** on **App Structure › Types** (matches name / slug / plural). While a query is present the list renders flat and non-draggable (a `PlainTypeItem`) — reordering a filtered subset would write misleading `sortOrder`s — and clearing it restores the drag-to-reorder list. *(Collapsible grouping-by-group is the next increment.)*
- **Quieter JSON-config import:** it no longer probes `GET /connection-types/:slug` per slug (which returns 404 — and the error middleware logs it — for every *new* connection type). It now loads the existing slugs once and diffs in memory. The 404s in the log during an import were benign existence checks; this removes the noise.

<Aside type="note">
Frontend-only; module build + `check:templates` clean; host tsc pending (`structure update` → v1.33.33).
</Aside>

## v1.33.32

**Server-side node-type permission enforcement + `'system'` tier (Stage 1 / S1.3)** *(generated-output)*

The node-type `permissions` block (`{ create, edit, archive, delete }`) was config-only and enforced solely in the UI — a direct API, public-API, or `serviceRegistry` call ignored it. Now it is enforced **server-side, in the service methods**.

- A shared `assertNodePermission` gate runs in `createNode`, `updateNode`, `deleteNode`, `moveNode`, `duplicateNode`, `archiveNode`, `restoreNode`, and the bulk variants — so callers that bypass route middleware are covered.
- The tier is extended to **`'anyone' | 'admin' | 'system'`**. `'system'` = only a trusted in-process caller (passing `isSystem`, e.g. the cognitive-pipeline engine) — no user or admin via the UI/API.
- `attachRole` is added to every node-write route to thread `isAdmin`; the public API passes `isAdmin: false`.
- This is the **simplified input-scope model** — no separate `inputScope`/`global` config fields were added (they duplicated `permissions` and structural scope membership; see [Design Decisions](/modules/structure/decisions/#resolved-follow-ups-2026-08-09)).

<Aside type="caution">
**Behaviour change:** a write to a node type whose `permissions` restrict that action now returns **400** for a non-admin (default `'anyone'` is a no-op, so unconfigured types are unaffected). Module build + `check:templates` clean; **host tsc validation pending** (veradai `structure update` → v1.33.32). Follow-up: an admin `permissions` editor in `NodeTypeForm` (settable via JSON import today).
</Aside>

## v1.33.31

**Server-side field validation on the write path (Stage 1 / S1.1)** *(generated-output)*

The first item of the [Generic Platform Hardening](/modules/structure/build-plan/#generic-platform-hardening--stages-13-2026-08-09) plan. The request Zod schema types a node's `data` as an open bag, so until now `required` / `pattern` / `min-max` / `enum` / type were enforced **only** in the `NodeForm` React component — a direct API, public-API, or cognitive-pipeline write persisted invalid values verbatim.

- A pure `validateNodeData` runs over the resolved field defs (`getFieldDefs` — own + `superClass`-inherited + shared-template fields) in `createNode`, `updateNode`, and `bulkCreateNodes`. The **public API and cognitive-pipeline** writes inherit it via the service.
- Enforces: **required** (unconditional fields only — a field with a `visibleWhen` is skipped, matching the client's `getMissingRequiredFields`), **type** (number / boolean / toggle), **enum membership** (select / multiselect against `options`), **pattern** and **length/range** (`validation.min`/`max`/`pattern`/`message`). Unknown data keys are ignored; computed/derived and structural field types are skipped.
- On update only supplied keys are value-checked (and a required non-conditional field cannot be cleared to empty).

<Aside type="caution">
**Behaviour change:** a write with invalid custom-field `data` now returns **400** instead of silently persisting. This is the intended hardening (pre-launch clean break). First cut is **top-level fields**; `requiredWhen`, field-group sub-field validation, and a `tsx` test suite are documented follow-ups. Module build + `check:templates` clean; **host tsc validation pending user-side** (veradai).
</Aside>

## v1.33.30

**Tree-view search + keyboard navigation** *(generated-output)*

Two tree-view enhancements:

- **Search** — a search box on the tree view filters to matching nodes, keeps their ancestors, and auto-expands those ancestors so matches stay visible (shows a "No matches" state when nothing matches).
- **Keyboard navigation** — `StructureTree` now renders a flat list of the visible rows with proper tree semantics: `role="tree"`/`treeitem`, `aria-level` / `aria-selected` / `aria-expanded`, a roving tabindex (one row tabbable at a time), and arrow-key handling — **Up/Down** move, **Right** expands or steps into children, **Left** collapses or steps to the parent, **Home/End** jump, **Enter/Space** select.

<Aside type="note">
Host-tsc-validated against a real app (frontend, 0 structure errors). Internal refactor of `StructureTree` from recursion to a flat render — no API change (same props; still controlled when `expandedIds` + `onToggleExpand` are supplied).
</Aside>

## v1.33.29

**Detail/overview template configs wired + tree expand-state persists** *(generated-output)*

Two follow-ups.

**Component-template configs now take effect.** The overview and detail-header Component templates (the reusable render templates assigned to a type via `overviewTemplateId` / `detailHeaderTemplateId`) carried config that the admin editor saved but the renderers ignored — the controls did nothing. Now:

- `NodeOverviewTab` reads the assigned overview template's `layout` (single / two-column widget grid), falling back to the legacy `widgetLayout` then two-column.
- `NodeDetailHeader` honours the assigned detail-header template's `showIcon`, `showDescription` (a new description line under the title), `showFavorite`, and `showActions` toggles.
- `NodeDetailPage` honours `showBreadcrumb` (it owns the breadcrumb bar).

**Tree expand-state persists.** The tree view's expand/collapse state is now saved **per node type** in `structure_settings.treeExpanded` and rehydrated on mount, so the tree reopens where you left it (`NodeTreeView` is now controlled; local state drives it for instant feedback).

<Aside type="note">
Host-tsc-validated against a real app (frontend, 0 structure errors). Still open: per-widget `column` placement on the overview grid.
</Aside>

## v1.33.28

**Bulk operations multi-select UI** *(generated-output)*

The frontend half of bulk operations — pick several nodes and act on them at once:

- **Selection checkboxes** on the grid, list, and table views (an overlay on grid/list cards, a leading column in the table). Each stops propagation so ticking it doesn't open the node.
- A **selection toolbar** above the list: a select-all checkbox over the visible page, a live "N selected" count, and — once something is selected — contextual **Archive / Restore / Delete** actions (shown per the current status filter) plus **Clear**.
- Selection clears automatically on search / filter / status / type / view change and after a bulk action.

Wired to the v1.33.27 endpoints via three new `useNodeMutations` mutations (`bulkArchiveNodes` / `bulkDeleteNodes` / `bulkRestoreNodes`) that report succeeded/failed counts and refresh the node caches. Declares the `checkbox` shadcn component.

<Aside type="note">
Host-tsc-validated against a real app (frontend, 0 structure errors). Builds on the v1.33.27 endpoints. **Still open** (roadmap): per-node permission filtering (server-side ownership already scopes every op), rendering module-registered `nodeActionRegistry` bulk actions, and bulk *move*.
</Aside>

## v1.33.27

**Bulk archive / delete / restore endpoints** *(generated-output)*

The backend half of bulk operations (the list-page multi-select UI lands next). Three new endpoints act on a bounded list of node ids with **partial-success** semantics — a failure on one id is collected, not fatal to the batch:

- `POST /api/v1/structure/nodes/bulk-archive`
- `POST /api/v1/structure/nodes/bulk-delete` (soft delete — reversible)
- `POST /api/v1/structure/nodes/bulk-restore`

Each returns `{ succeeded: string[], errors: { id, error }[] }`. Internally each id runs through the existing single-node method, so **cascade and the per-node hook are preserved**, and one summary hook fires per operation: `structure.nodes.bulkArchived` / `bulkDeleted` / `bulkRestored` (each `{ userId, nodeIds }`) — bringing the action-hook total to **49**. Bounded to 500 ids. Exposed cross-module via the service registry. Bulk **move** is deferred (it needs a target-parent picker in the UI phase).

<Aside type="note">
Host-tsc-validated against a real app (backend, 0 structure errors). No schema change.
</Aside>

## v1.33.26

**Tree view on list pages** *(generated-output)*

List pages gain a fourth view — **tree** — alongside grid, list, and table. It renders a node type's hierarchy (parent → child) as an expandable tree; clicking a node opens its detail page.

- A lazy `NodeTreeView` wrapper fetches the hierarchy via `useNodeTree` (`getTree`) **only when the tree view is active**, so grid/list/table loads pay nothing for it.
- Wires the previously built-but-unrendered `StructureTree` component (it self-manages expand/collapse).
- The pager is hidden in tree view (the tree shows the full bounded hierarchy, not a page).

<Aside type="note">
Host-tsc-validated against a real app (frontend, 0 structure errors). Follow-ups tracked in the roadmap: drag-and-drop reparenting, expand-state persistence, keyboard navigation, inline create, and tree search/filter.
</Aside>

## v1.33.25

**Saved filter presets on list pages** *(generated-output)*

Save the current list filters as a named preset and re-apply them in one click. A **Presets** popover in the filter bar lets you:

- **Save** the current search + status filter + field filters under a name
- **Apply** a saved preset (it also updates the URL, per v1.33.24)
- **Delete** a preset

Presets are scoped **per node type** and persist in the user's existing `structure_settings` blob under a `filterPresets` key — **no new table or migration**. A new `useUpdateStructureSettings()` hook shallow-merge-patches the settings blob and refreshes the shared settings cache.

<Aside type="note">
Host-tsc-validated against a real app (frontend, 0 structure errors). Backend and schema are unchanged — `structure_settings.settings` is a generic JSON blob. Builds on v1.33.23 (type-aware filters) and v1.33.24 (URL persistence).
</Aside>

## v1.33.24

**List filters, search, and status persist in the URL** *(generated-output)*

The list page now round-trips its active state through the URL query string, so a filtered/searched list **survives refresh and back/forward, and is shareable**:

- `?q=` — search text (debounced)
- `?status=` — status filter (omitted at the `active` default)
- `?filters=<json>` — the active field filters (select / text / number-range / date-range)

`NodeListPage` initialises this state from the URL on mount and writes it back with `useSearchParams(..., { replace: true })`, so typing does not stack history entries. View selection is intentionally **not** in the URL — it keeps its own user-preference persistence.

<Aside type="note">
Host-tsc-validated against a real app (frontend, 0 structure errors). Builds on the v1.33.23 type-aware filters.
</Aside>

## v1.33.23

**Type-aware list-page filters (text / number / date ranges)** *(generated-output)*

List-page field filters now go beyond `select`. Each filterable field renders a control suited to its type, driving the existing server-side `dataFilters` engine:

- **select** → `equals` (dropdown, unchanged)
- **text / textarea / email / url** → `contains` (search box)
- **number** → a min–max range (`between`, or a single-sided `gte` / `lte`)
- **date / datetime** → a from–to range

`DataFilter` gains an optional `valueType` (`'text' | 'number' | 'date'`), so `buildDataFilterQuery` casts comparison operands correctly — `::numeric` by default (backward-compatible), `::timestamptz` for dates so a date range orders correctly instead of failing a numeric cast. The admin **Filterable fields** selector (a type's **Pages** tab) is widened to offer select, text, number, and date fields.

See [Usage → List Page](/modules/structure/usage/) and [Roadmap → Now / Next](/modules/structure/roadmap/#now--next).

<Aside type="note">
Host-tsc-validated against a real app (backend + frontend, 0 structure errors). Backward-compatible: existing `equals` filters and any `DataFilter` without `valueType` behave exactly as before. **Deferred:** saved filter presets, URL-persisted filter state, and rendering custom `nodeFilterRegistry` filter components.
</Aside>

## v1.33.22

**Field templates in config import/export** *(generated-output)*

Shared field templates (`structure_field_template`) are now part of the admin JSON import/export, so a config file is fully self-contained — previously only the *references* (`fieldTemplateIds`) round-tripped, not the templates themselves.

- **Export** adds a `fieldTemplates` array alongside `nodeTypes` / `connectionTypes` / `groups`.
- **Import** creates/updates field templates first (matched by slug), then **remaps** `fieldTemplateIds` on node and connection types to the target app's ids. A reference resolves whether you write it as the template's **slug** (recommended for hand-authored configs — you don't need to know the generated cuid) or as an exported id; unmatched references are left as-is (dangling tolerated).

See [JSON Reference → Field Template JSON](/modules/structure/json-reference/#field-template-json).

<Aside type="note">
Host-tsc-validated against a real app (frontend, 0 structure errors).
</Aside>

## v1.33.21

**Per-node classification picker + group icon pass-through** *(generated-output)*

- **Per-node classification override picker.** The node **Settings** tab now shows a Classification selector for node types that define `governance.classification.levels` — previously the per-node override was API-only. Choosing a level sets `structure_node.classification` (and fires the `classificationChanged` hook); "Inherit type default" clears the override. The picker only appears for classified types.
- **Group icon forwarded to the sidebar.** `registerSection` now passes the group's icon (resolved from its Lucide name, the same way nav items resolve theirs) to the host navigation — via spread, so it stays inert on hosts whose `SidebarSection` has no icon slot yet. *(Requires host support to render, same pattern as group colour.)*

<Aside type="note">
Host-tsc-validated against a real app (backend + frontend, 0 structure errors).
</Aside>

## v1.33.20

**Navigation: entry-count pass-through + dead `sidebarNav.style` removed** *(generated-output)*

- **`mainNav.showCount` is now wired through to the host.** `registerMain` passes a `showCount` intent to the host navigation (via spread, so it stays inert on hosts whose `NavigationItem` doesn't support it yet). The host renders the live count badge — the toggle is no longer a dead control on the module side. *(Requires host support for a nav count badge to render, same pattern as group colour.)*
- **Removed the dead `sidebarNav.style` (`'none' | 'list' | 'tree'`) config field.** It had no admin editor and no renderer — the `StructureTree` component is present but unmounted (roadmap-deferred), and it only ever set a default nothing read. A future tree view will add a real list-page view switcher rather than resurrect the knob.

<Aside type="note">
Host-tsc-validated against a real app (backend + frontend, 0 structure errors).
</Aside>

## v1.33.19

**Node-form required enforcement + conditional `requiredWhen`** *(generated-output)*

- **Required is now enforced on the node create/edit form.** Previously the asterisk was cosmetic — a blank required custom field submitted anyway; only the built-in name was checked. Submitting with a blank required field is now blocked and the missing fields are listed above the footer.
- **New `requiredWhen` field config** makes a field conditionally required — same shape as `visibleWhen` — enforced on submit and reflected by the required asterisk (e.g. an obligation deadline required only when `compliance_status` is "not-yet-due"). Configure it in the field editor next to Visible When (inherited by node-type fields, connection-type fields and field templates).
- **Shared evaluators.** Visibility and required logic now live in one `utils/field-conditions.ts` (`isFieldVisible`, `isFieldRequired`, `getMissingRequiredFields`), reused by the field renderer and both the node and connection forms. The connection form's check now also **skips fields hidden by `visibleWhen`** — previously a conditionally-hidden required field could permanently disable the Add button.

<Aside type="note">
Client-side enforcement (what the field config describes); server-side field validation stays parked. Host-tsc-validated against a real app (backend + frontend, 0 structure errors).
</Aside>

## v1.33.18

**Connection uniqueness respects `bidirectional`** *(generated-output)*

- A `unique` connection type that is also `bidirectional` now enforces **one connection per unordered pair** — `createConnection` and `promoteConnection` reject an existing reverse edge (B→A) as well as A→B. Previously the check was ordered-only, so a bidirectional + unique type (e.g. a matter–case-law likeness score, which must be one-per-pair) could hold both A→B and B→A. Directional (`bidirectional:false`) types keep the ordered check, so A→B and B→A stay distinct.
- The admin **Bidirectional** toggle no longer claims connections become "one-way only" when off. Graph traversal (`getNodeConnections`, `expandGraph`) is **undirected regardless** of the flag; `bidirectional` governs the unique-pair semantics, not navigation. The toggle copy and the config / JSON-reference / usage docs now say so.

<Aside type="note">
Host-tsc-validated against a real app (backend + frontend, 0 structure errors).
</Aside>

## v1.33.17

**Workflow → Stage rename + transition enforcement** *(generated-output + schema change · breaking API rename)*

The `workflow` concept is renamed to **`stage`** everywhere, and stage-transition constraints are now actually enforced.

- **Rename** — `workflowState` → `stage` (per node), `workflowConfig` → `stageConfig` (per type), the config types `WorkflowConfig`/`WorkflowState`/`WorkflowStateGroup`/`WorkflowTransition` → `StageConfig`/`Stage`/`StageGroup`/`StageTransition`, the feature toggle `features.workflowStates` → `features.stages`, the list-query params `workflowState`/`workflowStates`/`workflowGroup` → `stage`/`stages`/`stageGroup`, the inner JSON keys `states`/`defaultState` → `stages`/`defaultStage`, and the action hook `structure.node.workflowStateChanged` → `structure.node.stageChanged` (payload keys `oldState`/`newState` → `oldStage`/`newStage`).
- **No data move** — the DB columns are kept (`stage @map("workflowState")`, `stageConfig @map("workflowConfig")`); a one-time, idempotent, exception-safe migration (`1.33.17__stage-rename.sql`) renames only the JSON keys inside the stored `workflowConfig` / `features` blobs.
- **Transition enforcement (net-new)** — `updateNode` now rejects a stage change the `stageConfig.transitions` map does not permit, and rejects a target stage not present in `stageConfig.stages`. Both are **opt-in**: an unset from-stage rule (or an empty transitions map / empty stages list) imposes no constraint, so types without a configured stage machine are unaffected. A new admin **Allowed Transitions** editor (per from-stage allowed-next chips) exposes it, and the editor now round-trips `transitions` instead of silently dropping it.

<Aside type="caution">
Breaking, pre-launch clean break: consumers (veradai, cognitive-pipeline, AI Core) must update the renamed API query params, request/response field (`stage`), config JSON keys (`stageConfig`, `stages`, `defaultStage`, `features.stages`), and the `structure.node.stageChanged` hook payload. An app that re-imports its node-type config JSON must rename those keys in the JSON, or the re-import will clear its stage config.
</Aside>

<Aside type="note">
Host-tsc-validated against a real app (backend Prisma client + frontend, 0 structure errors). The rename preserves the search-result projection by aliasing `"workflowState" AS "stage"` in the raw-SQL column lists.
</Aside>

## v1.33.16

**Meta-audit fixes — 28 findings resolved** *(generated-output + schema change)*

Fixes for the [2026-08-07 four-dimension audit](/modules/structure/roadmap/#archive): 1 critical, 13 moderate, 14 minor across security, production, performance and standards.

- **Security** — the public API `GET /:type/:id` no longer returns **decrypted secrets** (secret-type values are stripped before responding; a read-scoped key could previously read stored credentials). `duplicateNode` seals secret `dataOverrides` at rest; `updateNode` redacts secrets from its action-hook payload; the formula string-length cap is enforced centrally in `evalCall` (string dev-functions like `concat` could bypass the `+`-only guard → OOM). `structure_function` and `structure_field_templates` are declared in the security manifest, `structure_function` gains an erasure deletion handler, and template / field-template mutation logs carry `audit: true`.
- **Production** — empty-array data filters no longer 500 `listNodes` (`IN ()` guarded); merge-field resolution no longer corrupts values containing `$` sequences; `NodeListPage` and the children tab are paginated (items past the first 50 were unreachable); the Edit toggle works for single-tab and sidebar node types; a default (`{}`) `workflowConfig` no longer crashes the admin form; phone placeholders, `useCurrentScope` restore, seeded-type loading flicker, the date renderer, the node-selector search/empty state, and field-id generation after a delete are all fixed.
- **Performance** — `bulkCreateNodes` caches field defs per type (was two full-table queries per row); `listNodes` clamps pagination in-service; merge-field derefs pre-warm concurrently; `observedAt` is restored to search result columns; eight redundant leftmost-prefix `@@index([userId])` / `@@index([nodeId])` indexes dropped.
- **Standards** — the node-selector clear button and icon-only buttons (favourite, actions, secret show/hide) are keyboard / screen-reader accessible; a dead `showColor` prop and an aesthetic sticky-header opacity class removed.

<Aside type="note">
Schema change: drops 8 redundant indexes (additive migration). Host-tsc-validated against a real app (backend + frontend, 0 structure errors); the formula evaluator was re-checked against its 56-case development scratch suite (see v1.33.15 — the suite is a pre-release validation harness, not a shipped artefact).
</Aside>

## v1.33.15

**Formula / calculation field type** *(generated-output + schema change)*

A new admin-authored **`formula`** field type — a read-only field whose value is a safe expression over merge-field tokens, evaluated client-side (like `computedTemplate`, the value is client-computed and server-accepted).

- **Safe evaluator** — a dependency-free tokeniser → parser → tree-walking evaluator (`utils/formula.ts`). Operators (`+ - * / % ^`, comparisons, `&& || !`, ternary) plus function calls. No member access and no code execution: calls resolve only to registered functions by name, so JS globals/prototypes are unreachable; evaluation is bounded by input length, AST size, call depth, a work-step budget and an output-size cap.
- **Two-tier function library** —
  1. **Dev-registered functions** (`formulaFunctionRegistry`): built-in maths (`round`, `min`, `max`, `clamp`, `sqrt`, `pow`, `log`, `sum`, `avg`, …), text (`len`, `lower`, `concat`, `contains`, `replace`, …), logic (`if`, `coalesce`), and **similarity** (`levenshtein`, `stringSimilarity`, `jaccard`, `numericSimilarity`). Other modules can register their own by importing `formulaFunctionRegistry` (e.g. a colour module adding `deltaEOK`).
  2. **Admin-defined functions** (new `structure_function` table): reusable named expressions authored in **App Structure › Functions** (e.g. `discountedPrice(p, d) = p * (1 - d)`), callable from any formula and from other functions.
- **Node-to-node semantic similarity** — `semanticSimilarity(a, b)` returns the cosine similarity (0–1) of two nodes' embeddings, off the module's existing vector search.
- **Config** — a typed `NodeTypeField.calcConfig` (`formula` + optional `precision` / `prefix` / `suffix`). The field editor gains a formula box with field- and function-insert pickers, plus the new Functions admin tab.

<Aside type="note">
Schema change: adds the `structure_function` table (additive, Prisma-expressible — no raw migration). The `semanticSimilarity` fn also adds a `POST /similarity` endpoint + an embeddings-service method, both graceful no-ops on non-embeddings installs. Host-tsc-validated against a real app (backend + frontend, 0 structure errors); the evaluator was validated against a 56-case scratch suite during development and reviewed adversarially for sandbox-escape, DoS, NaN-comparison and update-loop defects. That suite is **not** shipped — the only formula artefacts the module emits are `utils/formula.ts` and `registrations/formula-functions.registration.ts`.
</Aside>

<Aside type="caution">
Like `computedTemplate`, a formula's result is persisted **unencrypted**. Don't reference a `secret`-typed field from a formula (or merge template) — the field-token picker excludes secrets, but a hand-typed `{secret}` would store a plaintext derivation of the sealed value.
</Aside>

## v1.33.14

**Colour inputs use the starter's shared colour picker** *(generated-output change)*

- The **node-type**, **group**, and **connection-type** colour fields now use the starter's shared **`QuickColourPicker`** (`@/components/colour-picker`) instead of the module's own bundled `ColorPicker`. Configured with hex swatches + a custom-hex chip + clear, so stored values stay `#RRGGBB` and the module's existing inline-style colour rendering is unchanged.
- Removed the module's `components/common/ColorPicker.tsx` (and its barrel exports + `module.ts` file-map entry) — colour selection is now the shared starter component, so modules don't each ship their own.
- The compact per-row colour swatches (conditional-colour rules, workflow states, classification levels) keep their native `<input type="color">` — a full swatch-row picker doesn't fit those dense inline rows.

<Aside type="note">
Requires the starter to provide `@/components/colour-picker`. Values are kept as hex (not theme tokens/palette) so they stay CSS-valid for the module's inline-style rendering — no render-site changes. Host-tsc-validated (frontend).
</Aside>

## v1.33.13

**Connections get full node-field parity + shared field templates** *(generated-output + schema change)*

Connections are nodes — connection types now use the full node field system instead of the old options-only subset:

- **Full field editor** — the connection-type editor uses the same `NodeFieldSetEditor` as node types (sections, every field type, validation, secret config, merge-field computed values). The create-connection dialog renders fields with the full `NodeFieldRenderer`.
- **Shared field templates** — connection types can attach `structure_field_template` sets via a new `fieldTemplateIds`, merged into their effective fields (own fields win on id clashes), exactly like node types.
- **Secret fields are sealed** — connection data now encrypts `secret`-type values on create and decrypts them for the owner on read (new `getConnectionFieldDefs`; `createConnection` seals, `getNodeConnections` / `listConnections` / `promoteConnection` unseal), matching node behaviour. Read-only displays mask secrets.
- **Sort order** — the editor now exposes the existing (previously unsettable) `sortOrder`.

<Aside type="note">
Schema change: adds `fieldTemplateIds Json @default("[]")` to `structure_connection_type` (additive, defaulted). `ConnectionTypeConfig.fields` is now `NodeTypeField[]` and `ConnectionTypeField` is a deprecated alias of `NodeTypeField` (existing connection-field data is a subset and upgrades cleanly). Host-tsc-validated against a real app with the Prisma column regenerated — backend + frontend, 0 structure errors.
</Aside>

## v1.33.12

**Admin UI (batch 4 cont.): group colour wired up** *(generated-output change)*

- **Group colour** — the App Structure › Groups editor now has a **Colour** picker, and the colour is passed through `registerSection` so a group's sidebar section renders a colour swatch beside its label. Previously the colour was stored end-to-end but had no editor and was never sent to the nav registry — dead config.

<Aside type="note">
Rendering the swatch requires the host starter template to carry `SidebarSection.color` (a small `navigation.registry` + `NavMain` change, starter ≥ v2.5.10). Until the app is on that starter version, the colour is stored and registered but simply not rendered — the registration uses a spread, so there is **no type error** on older starters. Group `icon` remains stored-but-unrendered under the same host constraint. Host-tsc-validated (frontend).
</Aside>

## v1.33.11

**Admin UI (batch 4): Context section + working Labels editor** *(generated-output change)*

- **Context section** (Properties tab) — new controls for `contextConfig` (**Include ancestors** + **ancestor depth**, **Include connections**, **Include child counts**) and a **Context hints** textarea. All are consumed by `getNodeContext()` when assembling a node's context payload (breadcrumbs, related data, AI prompts) but were previously unsettable in the UI.
- **Labels editor + wired overrides** — a new **Labels** section exposes overrides for the **search placeholder**, **empty-state title/message**, and **archive/restore** actions. `getLabel()` was fully built and exported but **never called** — the list / card / detail / settings components hardcoded or inlined the same strings, so an override did nothing. This batch routes those affordances through `getLabel()`, so overrides now take effect. Defaults are unchanged (getLabel's fallbacks reproduce the previous strings).

<Aside type="note">
Only label keys with a real rendered affordance are exposed (search, empty-state, archive, restore). The create/delete affordances are left as-is — their `getLabel` fallbacks ("Add New X" / "Delete X") differ from the current default copy ("New X" / "Create X" / "Delete"), so wiring them would silently change default wording. Group `color` / `icon` are deferred: they need a starter-template change (the host `SidebarSection` registry accepts no styling field). Host-tsc-validated (frontend).
</Aside>

## v1.33.10

**Admin UI (batch 3): sidebar-link overrides + working page-section reorder** *(generated-output change)*

- **Sidebar link overrides** — Properties › Sidebar Navigation now exposes `mainNav.label` (override the plural name), `mainNav.order` (sidebar position, 0–100), and `mainNav.icon` (override the type icon). All three are already consumed by the navigation registration but were previously unsettable.
- **Page Sections reorder now works** — the detail-page section list had grip handles that did nothing (`pageNav.tabOrder` was never written). Replaced with working **up/down arrows** that reorder and persist `tabOrder`, which the detail page already honours. The per-section enable toggles are unchanged.

<Aside type="note">
Additive admin-UI only. `sidebarNav.style` and its sub-options were deliberately **not** surfaced — they have no consumer in the module templates (ordering/labels come from `mainNav` + the registration), so a control would be dead config. Host-tsc-validated (frontend).
</Aside>

## v1.33.9

**Admin UI (batch 2): field validation message + secret-field config** *(generated-output change)*

More field-builder completeness — settings the backend accepts but the editor couldn't set:

- **`validation.message`** — a "Validation message" input (text / textarea / number fields), shown when a min/max/pattern rule fails. Previously the *rule* was editable but not the message the user sees.
- **Secret-field config** — a `secret`-type field now exposes **Length / Prefix / Charset** (alphanumeric · hex · base64) controls that drive `generateSecret()`. Previously the whole secret format was only reachable via the Import Fields JSON dialog (the type fell back to length 32 / alphanumeric). The "Default value" control is also hidden for secret fields, since their value is auto-generated.

<Aside type="note">
Additive admin-UI only. Host-tsc-validated (frontend) in a real starter app.
</Aside>

## v1.33.8

**Admin UI: Field Templates folded into App Structure; "Templates" tab renamed "Components"** *(generated-output change)*

- **Field Templates is no longer a separate admin sidebar page** — it's now the **Field Templates** tab inside **App Structure** (alongside Structure Types, Connection Types, Groups, Components, Registries, API Keys). The standalone `adminPagesRegistry` entry is removed and `FieldTemplatesAdminPage` renders in-tab.
- **The "Templates" tab is renamed "Components"** — the reusable card / list-item / detail-header / overview *view* templates, clearer alongside the new Field Templates tab.

<Aside type="note">
Admin-UI structure only — no data or API change. Host-tsc-validated (frontend) in a real starter app.
</Aside>

## v1.33.7

**Admin UI (batch 1): node-type & connection-type enable/disable, max-children, description field, field default value** *(generated-output change)*

First batch closing admin-config gaps — settings the backend already accepts/enforces but the admin form couldn't set:

- **Enable/disable node types & connection types.** An "Enabled" toggle in the node-type **Basic** tab and the connection-type editor (both default on), plus a "Disabled" badge on each admin list row. Disabled types/connections are already hidden from navigation, creation, and listings server- and client-side — now they're toggleable without deleting (and the admin lists still show them so they can be re-enabled).
- **`rules.maxChildren`** — a "Maximum children per node" input in the **Hierarchy** tab (server-enforced on create; previously only `maxDepth` was editable).
- **`features.description`** — a "Description Field" toggle in **Properties › Entry Customisation**, gating the entry description field (defaults **off**, matching the runtime).
- **Field default value** — a type-aware "Default value" control in the field builder (switch for toggle fields, number for number fields, text otherwise), pre-filling the field on new entries (previously only settable via the Import Fields JSON dialog).

<Aside type="note">
Additive admin-UI only — no schema or behaviour change to existing data (defaults chosen to match current runtime). Host-tsc-validated (backend + frontend) in a real starter app. First of several admin-config-coverage batches.
</Aside>

## v1.33.6

**Filtered-search hygiene: pgvector iterative scans + Lakebase BM25 prefilter** *(generated-output change)*

Completes the search arm's handling of heavily-filtered queries — the final item of the retrieval work brief.

- **pgvector 0.8 iterative index scans on the semantic arm.** When pgvector ≥ 0.8 (feature-detected on `extversion`), vector searches (`semanticSearchNodes` / `semanticSearchChunks` / `findSimilarNodes` and the semantic arm of both hybrids) set `hnsw.iterative_scan = strict_order`, so a heavily-filtered query keeps scanning the HNSW index until its `LIMIT` is filled instead of post-filtering a fixed candidate set — better recall, with no change to distance ordering. A no-op on `lakebase_ann` (storage-resident, documents no equivalent), which keeps the existing over-fetch + `ef_search` tuning.
- **Lakebase `lakebase_bm25.prefilter` on the keyword arm.** BM25 queries (`searchNodes`, `lexicalSearchChunks`, and the lexical arm of both hybrids) set `lakebase_bm25.prefilter = on` so the strict, always-`userId`-scoped `WHERE` filters are applied inside the bm25 scan rather than after top-K — cheaper and more accurate on large per-user corpora. Gated on the same bm25 feature-detection (the GUC ships with the `lakebase_text` extension that provides the index). The two previously non-transactional keyword methods now run their bm25 branch in a transaction so the `SET LOCAL` is scoped to the query and never leaks onto the pooled connection.

<Aside type="note">
Both are `SET LOCAL` session tunings, feature-detected and Lakebase/pgvector-version gated — non-Lakebase and pgvector < 0.8 installs are unaffected (over-fetch + `ef_search` remains the baseline). Validated end-to-end against a live Neon Lakebase (pgvector 0.8.0): every search path runs the preambles without error and returns correct rankings, and the BM25 self-heal still recovers through the new transaction wrap.
</Aside>

## v1.33.5

**Lakebase BM25 for chunk + hybrid search; self-healing BM25 fallback** *(generated-output change)*

Extends the v1.33.4 `lakebase_bm25` ranking from `searchNodes` to the **embeddings-tier** search surface, and makes every BM25 fallback self-healing.

- **BM25 for `lexicalSearchChunks` + `hybridSearchNodes` / `hybridSearchChunks` (feature-detected).** When a `lakebase_bm25` index is available, chunk keyword search ranks with **BM25** (`<@>` / `to_bm25query`, negated so higher-is-better — same `{ chunk, score }` contract) instead of `ts_rank_cd`, and the **lexical arm of hybrid** fusion orders by BM25 (`<@> ASC`). Only the rank *position* feeds reciprocal-rank fusion, so the fused score is unchanged regardless of the lexical backend. `ensureInfrastructure` provisions both the node (`searchVector`) and chunk (`contentVector`) `lakebase_bm25` indexes at runtime, gated **independently** on the access method existing (non-Lakebase apps keep GIN + `ts_rank_cd` / RRF unchanged).
- **Self-healing fallback (also applied to `searchNodes`).** The query-time fallback now **resets the memoised infrastructure promise** when a BM25 query throws, so the *next* call re-runs feature-detect — recovering automatically from a transient DB error (BM25 returns next request) or a genuinely dropped index (`ensureInfrastructure` recreates it via `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`). Previously a single transient blip pinned `ts_rank_cd` for the whole process lifetime.

<Aside type="note">
No schema change. Requires Neon Lakebase for the BM25 path; every other install keeps GIN + `ts_rank_cd` (lexical) and RRF (hybrid), unchanged. Validated end-to-end against a live Neon Lakebase — chunk BM25 ranking (higher term-frequency / shorter document ranks first), hybrid RRF ordering, and the self-heal (drop index → fall back to `ts_rank_cd` → next call recreates the index and restores BM25).
</Aside>

## v1.33.4

**Lakebase BM25 keyword ranking + runtime column self-heal** *(generated-output change)*

Completes the v1.33.0 `lakebase_bm25` fast-follow and makes keyword search self-provisioning.

- **BM25 ranking for `structureService.searchNodes` (feature-detected).** When a `lakebase_bm25` index over `searchVector` is available, `searchNodes` ranks with **BM25** (`<@>` / `to_bm25query`, IDF + document-length normalised) instead of `ts_rank_cd`; otherwise it uses the always-present GIN + `ts_rank_cd` path. Both are exposed as `score` where **higher is better**, so the `{ node, score }` contract is unchanged. `ensureKeywordInfrastructure` provisions the `lakebase_bm25` index at runtime, gated on the access method existing (non-Lakebase apps skip cleanly). A **query-time fallback** demotes to `ts_rank_cd` if the runtime-only index ever disappears, so keyword search degrades rather than errors.
- **Runtime column self-heal.** `ensureKeywordInfrastructure` (node `searchVector`) and `ensureInfrastructure` (node `searchVector` + chunk `contentVector`) now run `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS "<col>" tsvector` before the trigger/backfill/GIN — so the first search provisions **column → trigger → GIN → backfill** with no app intervention, even if the Prisma migrate that adds the `Unsupported("tsvector")` column never landed. Idempotent; a no-op once the column exists.

<Aside type="note">
No schema change. Requires Neon Lakebase for the BM25 path; every other install keeps GIN + `ts_rank_cd` (unchanged). **Ownership note:** the `searchVector` / `contentVector` columns are declared in `schema.prisma` as `Unsupported("tsvector")`, so Prisma also creates them via a bare `ADD COLUMN`; the self-heal's `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` is a deliberate belt-and-suspenders. On the drift-immune scaffold (module-starter v1.31.9+) this is safe — the schema-vs-schema migrate diff never re-adds an unchanged column, and `migrate deploy`'s "already exists" auto-baseline covers a failed-then-retried column migration. Validated end-to-end (self-heal + BM25 ranking + fallback) against a live Neon Lakebase database.
</Aside>

## v1.33.3

**Pre-migrate extension declaration via `onPrepareDatabase`** *(install-time only — no generated-output change)*

Adopts the scaffold's new **`onPrepareDatabase`** lifecycle hook (module-starter scaffold **v1.31.3+**) to declare the Prisma datasource extensions **before** `prisma migrate dev` runs, rather than only in the post-migrate heal. This closes the ordering gap where an *installed-but-undeclared* extension (e.g. Neon Lakebase) made the migrate fail on **drift** before the heal could declare it — so `npx structure init` / `update` now applies the schema in a **single pass** on a fresh Lakebase-enabled app, with no manual `prisma migrate dev` re-run.

- **`onPrepareDatabase`** (runs before the migrate, on both install and update) declares pgvector — plus the Lakebase pair when a read-only `pg_extension` probe confirms it is installed — via the starter's `ensure-pgvector --lakebase` primitive.
- The **post-migrate embeddings heal repeats the same declaration idempotently** as a fallback for older scaffolds that never invoke the hook; it is a no-op once the datasource is fully declared.
- Extracted a shared `declareDatasourceExtensions` helper — the declaration logic itself (the `--lakebase` / `--no-lakebase` argv-authoritative `pg_extension` probe) is unchanged from v1.33.1.

<Aside type="note">
Requires the module-starter scaffold **v1.31.3+** for the pre-migrate hook; this release ships on scaffold **v1.31.7** (TypeScript 7 toolchain). On older scaffolds the module degrades cleanly to the post-migrate declaration. No template or generated-output change — install-time logic only. Resolves the [module-starter upstream issue](/module-starter/upstream-issues/) (fixed scaffold-side in v1.31.3).
</Aside>

## v1.33.2

**Generated frontend imports migrated to react-router v8** *(generated-output change)*

Aligns the module's generated frontend with the starter's **react-router v8** upgrade, which consolidates `react-router-dom` into `react-router`. All generated components, pages, hooks, and route config now import `Link`, `useNavigate`, `useParams`, and the `RouteObject` type from **`react-router`** — no `react-router-dom` remains. Verified against `react-router@8`, which re-exports each of these at the top level. Apps on the react-router-v8 starter pick this up on their next `structure update`.

## v1.33.1

**Automatic Prisma extension declaration in the embeddings heal** *(install-time only — no generated-output change)*

The embeddings install/reconfigure heal (`healEmbeddingsSetup`) now **auto-declares** the app's Prisma datasource extensions instead of only advising. It shells out to the starter's declaration-only `ensure-pgvector` primitive so a future `prisma migrate dev` builds a shadow database that includes the extensions — closing the drift / **P3006** gap that could silently stop the module's schema columns from applying (the exact failure hit on veradai enabling Lakebase against structure v1.33.0).

- **pgvector: advise → auto-run.** When the datasource doesn't yet declare pgvector, the heal runs `npm run ensure-pgvector` (at the app root) rather than printing manual steps. It falls back to the previous advisory on older starters that predate the primitive.
- **Lakebase pair — opt-in, DB-gated.** A read-only probe (`SELECT … FROM pg_extension` via `prisma db execute`) confirms both `lakebase_vector` + `lakebase_text` are installed *before* the heal passes the opt-in `-- --lakebase`. Declaring an extension makes `migrate dev` try to `CREATE` it, which fails without the Lakebase preload — so the live DB is the authority, never a guess.
- **argv beats the environment.** The heal passes an explicit `--lakebase` / `--no-lakebase` flag (gated on the script supporting them) so its probe result wins over any ambient `ENSURE_PGVECTOR_LAKEBASE` env the child would inherit.
- **Silent when healthy.** Skips the probe and the shell-out entirely once both extensions are already declared.

<Aside type="note">
No template or generated-output change — this is module install-time logic only, so a host `tsc` pass is N/A. Requires the starter's `ensure-pgvector` (v1.65.0) for the pgvector auto-run and **v2.4.2** for the `--lakebase` / `--no-lakebase` flags; older starters degrade to the manual advisory. Hardened by an adversarial multi-lens review and validated against veradai's live schema (probe exit-code semantics, `prisma validate`, npm flag forwarding). See the starter's [Ensure pgvector](/starter-template/scripts/#ensure-pgvector).
</Aside>

## v1.33.0

**Always-on keyword search, plus a Lakebase `lakebase_ann` upgrade for vectors** *(reworks the v1.32.0 lexical arm)*

Keyword search is now a first-class, **always-available** surface — no embeddings, no per-type config. Structure owns **storage only**; it never calls an LLM or embedder. This reworks the v1.32.0 lexical arm: the search text is now auto-indexed from every node, and the vector tier gains a Neon Lakebase acceleration path.

- **Always-on keyword search — `structureService.searchNodes(userId, query, options?)`.** Ranks with `websearch_to_tsquery` + `ts_rank_cd` over a **trigger-maintained `tsvector` column** (`structure_node.searchVector`) built from `name` + `description` + every string value in the node's `data` JSON (via `jsonb_to_tsvector`), served by a GIN index. `options` is `{ topK?: number (default 20, max 200); nodeTypes?: string[]; status?: string[] (default ["active"]) }` and it returns `{ node, score }[]` where `score` is `ts_rank_cd` — higher is better, **not** a 0..1 similarity — and a blank query returns `[]`. This is the **ranked upgrade** of the `listNodes` `search` substring filter, and it needs **no embeddings opt-in**.
- **Dropped `searchText` + `cognitiveIngest.textTemplate`.** The v1.32.0 `structure_node.searchText` column and the per-type `features.cognitiveIngest.textTemplate` merge template are **removed** — keyword search now **auto-indexes** `name` + `description` + `data` with no config knob. `cognitiveIngest` is back to `{ enabled?: boolean }`.
- **Removed `structureEmbeddings.lexicalSearchNodes`** — superseded by the always-on `structureService.searchNodes`. Still on `structureEmbeddings` (embeddings-tier, unchanged surface, now over the new `tsvector` columns): `lexicalSearchChunks`, `hybridSearchNodes`, `hybridSearchChunks`, and the semantic methods. Hybrid still fuses keyword + semantic via RRF and needs embeddings.
- **Lakebase `lakebase_ann` for the vector columns (feature-detected).** When Neon Lakebase is available the module now creates `lakebase_ann` indexes for the embedding columns — pgvector-compatible (same `<=>` operator, **no query change**), storage-resident (survives Neon scale-to-zero), with far faster builds than HNSW. It is **transparent**: `CREATE EXTENSION` fails cleanly when the Lakebase preload libraries are not loaded, so non-Lakebase hosts are unaffected and **HNSW stays the automatic fallback**. `lakebase_bm25` for the keyword tier is a **fast-follow** (not in v1.33.0); keyword search ships on GIN + `ts_rank_cd` for now.
- **`getCognitiveCapabilities().lexical.enabled` is now always `true`** (was embeddings-gated), reflecting that `searchNodes` is unconditional. `hybrid` and chunk keyword search still ride the embeddings opt-in. `observedAt` is unchanged from v1.32.0.

<Aside type="note">
Migration `1.33.0__search-vectors.sql` adds the `searchVector` / `contentVector` `tsvector` columns, their maintaining triggers and GIN indexes, and (feature-detected) the `lakebase_ann` vector indexes — guarded and exception-safe like its sibling migrations, mirrored by the install heal and runtime `ensure`. After `npx structure update` to 1.33.0 the host **must run `prisma migrate dev`** to apply the new `searchVector` / `contentVector` / `observedAt` columns; the triggers + GIN self-heal at runtime on the first search once the columns exist. All raw SQL is parameterised. Validate with a host `tsc --noEmit -p` after install.
</Aside>

<Aside type="caution">
**Lakebase Prisma-drift caveat.** Enabling Lakebase is a Neon **management-API** operation (add `lakebase_vector` + `lakebase_text` to `settings.preload_libraries`, restart the compute, then `CREATE EXTENSION lakebase_vector CASCADE; CREATE EXTENSION lakebase_text CASCADE` — Postgres 16+, reversible). The extensions must then be **declared in the app's Prisma datasource** (`extensions = [vector, lakebase_vector, lakebase_text]`) or Prisma treats them as drift and blocks `prisma migrate` / `db push`. **As of v1.33.1 the embeddings heal declares them automatically** (via the starter's `ensure-pgvector --lakebase`, gated on a live `pg_extension` probe) — see below.
</Aside>

## v1.32.1

**Fix: `StructureFieldTemplate` import in the generated service** *(bug fix)*

Surfaced by host `tsc` validation in a real app: the shared-field-template service methods (`listFieldTemplates` / `getFieldTemplate` / …, added v1.29.0) annotated with `StructureFieldTemplate` but the generated `structure.service.ts` never imported it from `structure.types.ts`, so the file failed to compile with 8× `TS2552`. Added it to the type-import block. Verified the full structure feature (backend + frontend) type-checks with **0 errors** on Prisma 7.9.1, including the v1.32.0 lexical/hybrid/graph/`observedAt` code.

## v1.32.0

**Lexical + hybrid search, a graph-expansion primitive, and bi-temporal `observedAt`** *(cognitive-retrieval surface)*

Structure gains a keyword (lexical) and hybrid retrieval surface alongside the existing semantic methods, an N-hop graph-expansion query primitive, and a bi-temporal time column — all **storage-only**. Structure never calls an LLM or embedder: callers supply the query text and, for hybrid, the query embedding.

- **Lexical full-text search on `structureEmbeddings`.** `lexicalSearchNodes(userId, query, options?)` and `lexicalSearchChunks(userId, query, options?)` run `websearch_to_tsquery('english', …)` ranked by `ts_rank_cd` over functional GIN indexes on `to_tsvector('english', …)`. `score` is `ts_rank_cd` — higher is better, **not** a 0..1 similarity — and a blank query returns `[]`. Node search falls back to `name || description` when `searchText` is unset; chunk search joins `structure_nodes` for parent status/type scoping, mirroring `semanticSearchChunks`.
- **Hybrid search via Reciprocal Rank Fusion.** `hybridSearchNodes(userId, query, queryEmbedding, options?)` and `hybridSearchChunks(...)` run the lexical and semantic arms and fuse them in SQL — `score = Σ weight_arm / (rrfK + rank_arm)` (`rrfK` default 60; per-arm `lexicalWeight` / `semanticWeight`, both default 1). The caller supplies **both** the query text and its embedding. Each arm over-fetches 3× `topK`; a node present in only one arm contributes 0 from the other; a blank query degrades cleanly to the semantic arm.
- **Graph-expansion primitive — `structureService.expandGraph(userId, seedIds, options?)`.** Returns the N-hop neighbourhood over connection-nodes (`hops` default 1, max 6) as `{ nodeId, hopDistance }[]`, computed in a single recursive CTE. Undirected (matches either endpoint), userId-scoped, `origin`- and `connectionTypes`-filterable; each reached node carries its **minimum** hop distance. Seeds are excluded and only active, owned nodes are returned. A strict query primitive — orchestration, reranking and budgets live upstream; graph distance is a structural (never temporal) relevance signal.
- **Bi-temporal `observedAt` column.** `structure_node.observedAt` (`DateTime?`, **ungated**) records when a fact was true in the world, distinct from `createdAt` (when it was written). Nullable, engine-populated, indexed `[userId, observedAt]` — for time-scoped `WHERE` filters **only**, never a ranking or decay signal.
- **`cognitiveIngest.textTemplate` + `searchText`.** A node type's optional `features.cognitiveIngest.textTemplate` merge template (over `{name}`, `{description}` and `{fieldId}` tokens from the node's data) resolves into the new `structure_node.searchText` column on create/update, opting custom-field content into the lexical index. Own-config only (not hierarchy-resolved), so keep it on each type. When unset, the lexical GIN index falls back to `name` + `description`, so keyword search works out of the box.
- **`getCognitiveCapabilities` reports two more keys** — `lexical: { enabled }` (mirrors `embeddings.enabled`; the lexical/hybrid methods ride the same opt-in) and `graph: { enabled: true }` (always available — connection-nodes are core). Full shape: `{ embeddings: { enabled, dimensions }, chunks: { enabled }, lexical: { enabled }, graph: { enabled } }`.

<Aside type="note">
Additive and opt-in. The lexical/hybrid methods and `searchText` ride the existing `enableEmbeddings` opt-in; `observedAt` is **ungated**. Module migration `1.32.0__lexical-search.sql` adds the functional GIN indexes (`structure_nodes_searchtext_gin_idx`, `structure_chunks_content_gin_idx`), guarded and exception-safe like its sibling migrations and mirrored by the install heal and runtime `ensureInfrastructure`. All raw SQL is parameterised. Validate with a host `tsc --noEmit -p` after install.
</Aside>

<Aside type="tip">
GIN + `ts_rank_cd` is the **shipped, portable baseline** — it works on any Postgres 16+. Neon Lakebase's `lakebase_bm25` (true BM25 + top-K pushdown) and `lakebase_ann` are a **documented follow-up**, gated on a Neon-branch enablement test (`shared_preload_libraries` + compute restart) and on a materialised `tsvector` column — not defaulted to until that test is run.
</Aside>

## v1.31.0

**Field templates get the full visual field builder** *(item 6 follow-up)*

The **Field Templates** admin page now authors a template's fields with the **same visual builder** as a node type's Fields tab — add / reorder / configure every field type (select options, node-reference, chained-select, field-group sub-fields, merge templates, conditional visibility) — instead of the JSON textarea that shipped as the v1.29.0 MVP.

- **Extracted a shared `NodeFieldSetEditor` component** from the node-type field editor; it is now used by **both** `NodeTypeForm` and `FieldTemplatesAdminPage` — one source of truth for the field-array editor.
- The node type's own **Fields** tab is **unchanged in behaviour** — it renders the same editor through the extracted component.
- In the field-template context the per-field **Sensitivity** picker is hidden (a template has no owning type's classification levels) and the merge-template token picker is computed from the template's own fields.

<Aside type="note">
Frontend-only — no schema or API change. The extraction is a faithful, byte-diff-verified move of the existing editor (only `value`/`onChange`/sensitivity parametrisation differs from the original inline code). Validate with a host `tsc --noEmit -p` after install, and click through both the node-type **Fields** tab and the **Field Templates** editor.
</Aside>

## v1.30.1

**Security fix — secret fields nested inside a `field-group` are now encrypted at rest**

A `secret`-type field defined **inside a `field-group` repeater** was stored in **plaintext** (`node.data[group][i][subId]`). The server's field walker (`getFieldDefs`) only descended into top-level fields, so `getSecretFieldIds` never saw a nested secret and `sealSecretFields` never reached it. Top-level secret fields were unaffected.

- **`getFieldDefs` now recurses one level into `field-group` sub-fields**, tagging each with its parent `group` id (keyed `group.subId` so a nested sub-field never collides with a same-named top-level field). This applies to own, attached-field-template, and inherited sub-fields.
- **`sealSecretFields` / `unsealSecretFields`** now seal/unseal secret values inside field-group row arrays (immutably, idempotently), on create, update, and bulk-create. The unseal fast-path detects nested envelopes too.
- **`editableBy` enforcement** in `updateNode` now also rejects direct writes to `system`/`admin`-locked **sub-fields** (`data[group][i][subId]`).
- Reconciles the walker inconsistency with the public-API schema descriptor (`toMergeFieldDescriptors`), which already recursed.

<Aside type="caution">
**Existing data with a nested secret sub-field is plaintext at rest until it is next written.** Re-saving an affected node (create/update/bulk) seals it. There is no automatic migration — sealing happens on write. Rotate any secret that was stored plaintext if that matters for your threat model.
</Aside>

<Aside type="note">
Backend-only. No schema change. Nested groups-within-groups are not walked (the field editor supports one level). A known unrelated edge remains: `duplicateNode` merges caller `dataOverrides` without sealing (pre-existing; affects top-level secrets too).
</Aside>

## v1.30.0

**AI fields — per-node-type AI configuration, stored by Structure for AI Core** *(Veradai-Driven Build Plan — Phase 2, node-type half)*

A node type can now carry an `aiFields` config — structured prompt material that **AI Core** consumes for prompt assembly. Structure's role is **storage + exposure**: it persists `aiFields`, exposes it on `/types` reads, and provides the tab slot; AI Core (which self-registers its AI tab via `StructureModuleRegistration.tabs`) owns editing and consumption.

- **`aiFields` config on node types.** `{ promptSegments?, outputExamples? }` — added via the same mirror-site pattern as `contextHints`/`governance` (schema column + both type copies + `NodeTypeProposal` + the 8 CRUD mapping sites + Zod), so it round-trips through create/update/list/get/export/import/propose.
- **Aligned to AI Core's contract.** `promptSegments[].type` is `'context' | 'task' | 'external'` — identical to AI Core's `ConsumerSegment.type`, and each segment carries `content: string`, so a consumer maps a structure prompt segment straight onto an AI Core run segment. `outputExamples` (`{ label?, input?, output }`) guide the model; the consumer decides how to render them.
- **Storage-only.** Structure never interprets `aiFields` and builds no editor UI for it — that lives in AI Core's self-registered tab (a node type's AI tab is present iff AI Core is installed; there is no module-presence check in Structure).

<Aside type="note">
Additive — a `structure_node_type.aiFields` JSON column (created by `prisma migrate dev` on `structure update`, no `migrations.ts` entry). The `AiFieldsConfig` shape is the proposed Structure↔AI Core contract; it is additive/extensible, so the shape can evolve without breaking stored data. **Deferred (the other half of Phase 2):** context/`aiFields` on **connection types** (connection types have no context surface yet). Validate with a host `tsc --noEmit -p` after install.
</Aside>

## v1.29.0

**Shared field templates — define a field set once, attach it to many node types** *(Veradai-Driven Build Plan — item 6)*

A reusable **field template** is a named set of field definitions you author once and attach to any number of node types. Its fields merge into each attaching type's effective fields — and, critically, are sealed and permission-checked exactly like the type's own fields.

- **New `structure_field_template` entity + CRUD.** `{ name, slug, description?, fields }` with a full admin API (`/api/v1/structure/field-templates`, admin-gated writes) and a GDPR account-erasure handler. A dedicated **Field Templates** admin page authors them (name + description + a JSON field array).
- **Attach per node type.** A `fieldTemplateIds` config on the node type (App Structure → a type → **Fields** tab → **Field Templates** — a badge multi-select). The Fields tab shows a read-only preview of the fields each attached template contributes, with provenance.
- **Merge precedence: own › template › inherited.** A type's own fields shadow template fields, which shadow superClass-inherited fields (id-shadowed). Template fields are **not** inherited via `superClasses` — they apply to the type that attaches them.
- **Secrets and permissions are honoured.** Template fields flow through **both** the read path (`resolveTypeInGraph` → `resolved.fields`, tagged `fieldTemplateId`) **and** the write-path walkers (`getFieldDefs`) — so a `secret`-type field in a template is encrypted at rest and an `editableBy: 'admin' | 'system'` template field is enforced on update, not silently skipped. Template-provenance fields are stripped on write (never persisted into a type's own `fields`).

<Aside type="note">
Additive. Introduces the `structure_field_template` table and a `fieldTemplateIds` JSON column on `structure_node_type` — both created by `prisma migrate dev` on `structure update` (no `migrations.ts` entry needed). Authoring is JSON-based for now; a visual field-set editor is a fast-follow. Validate with a host `tsc --noEmit -p` after install.
</Aside>

## v1.28.0

**Merge-field exposure — metadata API, a reusable resolver, and a merge-field-driven card** *(Veradai-Driven Build Plan — Phase 5)*

Structure now **exposes** its merge fields so an external visual builder (e.g. useFoundry) can bind components to a node type's fields. Structure owns the data + exposure; presentation lives in the consuming builder.

- **Merge-field metadata API — `getMergeFieldSchema(nodeType)`.** Returns the flattened set of **bindable tokens** for a node type as `MergeFieldToken[]` — each with `path` (bare id), `insertText` (`{…}`), `label`, `type`, `kind`, and optional `via` / `multiple` / `inheritedFrom`. Full parity with the resolver: built-ins (`name`/`description`/`slug`), top-level fields, `field-group` subfields (`{group.sub}`), and one-hop node-reference deref targets (`{ref->field}`). Built on a single, pure `flattenMergeFields(fields, refFieldsBySlug)` core.
- **Reusable resolver.** The template resolver moved into a new React-free `utils/merge-fields.ts` and is now **exported**: `resolveTemplateAsync(template, data, cache?, fetchNode?)` (the node-fetch is injectable for non-React callers) plus a `resolveMergeFields(template, node)` convenience that merges `name`/`description`/`slug` into the data bag. `useResolvedTemplate` is unchanged and now wraps the shared resolver.
- **Merge-field-driven card.** `CardTemplateConfig` gains `titleTemplate` / `bodyTemplate` slots; `NodeCard` resolves them against the node's data (with `name`/`description`/`slug`), falling back to `node.name` for the title. `NodeCard` **now also resolves `cardTemplateId`** → its referenced `structure_template` row, so a shared card template's config actually applies at render time.
- **Admin merge-template pickers upgraded.** The "Insert field…" pickers (built-in name/description/slug + per-field) now list field-group subfield and node-reference deref tokens, not just flat `{field_id}` — powered by the same `getMergeFieldSchema`.
- **Public `GET /:type/schema`.** The API-key-authed public REST surface gains a schema endpoint returning `{ type, fields, referencedTypes }` — secret-filtered lean field descriptors (the flattener's inputs). Reference targets that are not themselves exposed are redacted, preserving the API's no-existence-leak rule.

<Aside type="caution">
**Behaviour change:** `NodeCard` now applies a node type's `cardTemplateId` (the referenced reusable card-template row). Previously the pointer was selectable in admin but **ignored** at render time — a card type that set `cardTemplateId` will now pick up that row's `titleTemplate` / `bodyTemplate` / `show*` config (inline `templateOverrides.card` and `navigation.listPage.card` still take precedence). Review node types that set `cardTemplateId` before upgrading.
</Aside>

<Aside type="note">
Additive — no schema change. The card-template slots ride in the existing `templateOverrides` / `structure_template.config` JSON. Validate with a host `tsc --noEmit -p` after install.
</Aside>

## v1.27.0

**Search over custom field values + bulk connection creation** *(two as-pulled build-plan items)*

- **Keyword search now scans custom `data` field values.** `listNodes`' free-text `search` matched only name + description; it now also matches the values of custom fields — via a guarded `jsonb_each_text` query folded into the search `OR`. Values only (not field keys); nested groups match as their JSON text; the row's `data` type is guarded so a non-object value can't fail the query. Best-effort (capped at 5,000 data-matches, unindexed) — structured querying stays the `dataFilters` engine's job, and retrieval/ranking stays cognitive-pipeline / AI Core. *(One quirk: `%` / `_` in the query act as SQL wildcards against data values.)*
- **Bulk connection creation.** A `bulkCreateConnections` service method (partial-success, like `bulkCreateNodes`) + `POST /connections/bulk` — the primary path for cognitive-pipeline to persist a batch of discovered connections. Each row runs through `createConnection` for full validation (endpoint ownership, type allowlist, unique / maxConnections); a failed row is collected per-index, not fatal. Reachable via the service registry (`serviceRegistry.get('structure').bulkCreateConnections(...)`). `skipHooks` suppresses the per-connection hooks and fires one `structure.connections.bulkCreated` summary instead, avoiding a per-row hook fan-out on large batches.

<Aside type="note">
Backend-only, additive — no schema change. Validate with a host `tsc --noEmit -p` after install.
</Aside>

## v1.26.0

**Collapsible fields + the repeater `allData` fix** *(Veradai-Driven Build Plan — Phase 4)*

- **`defaultCollapsed` field property.** Any field — including a whole `field-group` repeater — can render collapsed by default: a label + chevron trigger, with the input revealed on expand. The "hidden until revealed" complement to `visibleWhen`. Set via a **Collapsed by default** toggle in the field editor; honoured by `NodeFieldRenderer` in both edit and read-only modes (shadcn `Collapsible`).
- **Fixed: `visibleWhen` / `computedTemplate` now work inside `field-group` repeaters.** The renderer previously dropped `allData` when rendering a repeater's sub-fields, so a sub-field's conditional visibility and computed templates silently did nothing. Sub-fields now receive `{ ...topLevelData, ...rowData }` (the row wins on an id collision), so both work — a sub-field can reference a sibling in the same row **and** a top-level field.

<Aside type="caution">
The repeater fix is a **behaviour change for existing data.** A `field-group` sub-field configured with a `visibleWhen` (previously always shown) will now correctly **hide** when its condition is false, and one with a `computedTemplate` (previously never computed) will now **auto-compute and write** its value on first edit. Review any node types whose repeater sub-fields use `visibleWhen` / `computedTemplate` before upgrading.
</Aside>

<Aside type="note">
Frontend-only, additive — no schema change. `defaultCollapsed` lives on the frontend `NodeTypeField` (a pure render concern; it rides in the type's `fields` JSON and the server never reads it). Validate with a host `tsc --noEmit -p` after install.
</Aside>

## v1.25.0

**Data classification (generic node-type governance) + `isExportSafe`** *(Veradai-Driven Build Plan — Phase 3)*

The first entry in a generic, extensible per-type **governance** block: sensitivity / privilege classification. Structure stores and evaluates the classification; a consuming module's export pipeline enforces the gate.

- **New `governance.classification` config.** Define ordered sensitivity levels per node type (`{ value, label, rank, color? }`, lower rank = less sensitive) and a `default` level, on the node type's **Hierarchy** tab. Shaped as a generic `governance` container so further rule types (retention, access) can slot in beside `classification` later.
- **Per-field sensitivity.** A `sensitivity?` level on any field (`NodeTypeField`), for field-level redaction — set via a Sensitivity picker in the field editor; it survives type-hierarchy resolution into `resolved.fields`.
- **Per-node override.** A first-class `structure_node.classification` column (nullable; `null` inherits the type default), accepted on create / update (in-app and the public REST API) and returned on reads. A change fires a `structure.node.classificationChanged` action hook for audit / automation.
- **Evaluators.** `getEffectiveClassification(node, type)` (per-node override → type default → unclassified) and `isExportSafe(node, type, level)` (a node is safe when its effective classification rank ≤ the export threshold's rank). Both are exported from the feature root (backend **and** frontend) as pure functions for consumers to call. An unclassified node is unrestricted (fail-open); an explicitly-set level that is not in the configured levels, or an unknown threshold, fails closed.

<Aside type="note">
Generated **frontend + backend + schema** change — adds the `governance` JSON column on node types and a `classification` column on nodes. After `npx structure update`, run the Prisma migration so the new columns land, then validate with a host `tsc --noEmit -p` (0 errors in `features/structure`).
</Aside>

<Aside type="note">
Classification levels are **per node type** (they fit the existing per-type config model). For an app-wide scheme, configure consistent levels across the types that need it — `isExportSafe` evaluates each node against its own type's levels. **Deferred:** a per-node classification **picker** in the UI (the override is API-only for now); export-pipeline enforcement (a consuming module's job by design); and governance is **not** inherited through `superClasses` (own-config only, like the hierarchy rules).
</Aside>

## v1.24.0

**Primary-scope selector + server-side parent enforcement** *(Veradai-Driven Build Plan — Phase 1)*

Two halves of one deliverable: a configurable "current scope" that filters the app to one primary node's subtree, and the first server-side enforcement of the node-type hierarchy rules the admin UI has always let you configure.

**Primary scope**

- **New `primaryScope` node-type config.** Mark a type as the app's primary scope (`isPrimary`) and choose which node types the selected instance filters (`scopedTypes`). Configured on the node type's **Hierarchy** tab.
- **Header scope picker.** A `ScopeSelector` (registered via `headerToolbarRegistry`, `type: 'custom'`) appears in the header once a primary type exists; it lists instances of the primary type and remembers the choice per user (persisted to `structure_settings`).
- **Scoped list queries.** When a scope is selected, `NodeListPage` injects `pathPrefix` so a scoped type's list shows only nodes under the chosen instance's subtree (materialised path). **`scopedTypes` is an admin choice:** empty / `*` scopes *every* type; a specific list scopes only its members, leaving global/reference types (e.g. legislation) unscoped. The primary type's own list is never filtered by its own selection — you still browse all instances to pick one.
- Built for a single primary scope now, with the config shaped so multiple/secondary scopes can slot in later.

**Parent enforcement (the trust-fix half)**

- `createNode` and `moveNode` now **enforce** the node-type `rules` that were previously stored, shown in the admin UI, and silently ignored: a type marked *not standalone* (`isRoot: false`) requires a parent; a parent must be in the child's `allowedParents` (when set); a child must be in the parent's `allowedChildren` (when set); `maxChildren` and `maxDepth` are honoured.
- **Opt-in per rule — backward compatible.** An unset rule imposes no constraint, so a node type that never configured hierarchy behaves exactly as before. Only types with rules actually configured are affected.

<Aside type="note">
Generated **frontend + backend + schema** change (adds the `primaryScope` JSON column). After `npx structure update`, run the Prisma migration so the new column lands, then validate with a host `tsc --noEmit -p` (0 errors in `features/structure`).
</Aside>

<Aside type="caution">
Because enforcement now applies to rules that were previously unenforced, **existing data that violates a type's configured rules will surface an error on its next move** (e.g. reordering an `isRoot: false` node that already sits at the root) — the intended effect of turning enforcement on. Review your configured `rules` against existing placements after upgrading. Known Phase-1 gaps (follow-ups): `bulkCreateNodes` (the `POST /nodes/bulk` / AI-ingest path) does not yet run enforcement, and `moveNode`'s `maxDepth` check covers the moved node's own new depth, not each descendant of a moved subtree.
</Aside>

## v1.23.4

**Remove the vestigial `routes.registration.ts`**

`registrations/routes.registration.ts` was a **comment-only** file — no imports, no registry calls (routes are configured via `module.ts` `protectedRoutes` and the `structureRoutes` export in `routes.config.tsx`). It was emitted into every host app but did nothing, and was the one *true* positive the scaffold **v1.30.0** conformance linter surfaced once its C2 barrel check was fixed to resolve the real feature barrel. Removed from the templates; `npx structure update` drops it from existing installs as an orphan, and `conformance:check` is now clean.

## v1.23.3

**Computed built-in fields now populate — closing the `builtInFieldConfig` trust-fix**

v1.23.0 made computed built-in fields (name / description / slug carrying a `computedTemplate`) render read-only, but nothing ever produced the derived value — the field just stayed empty/unchanged. This wires it up, mirroring how custom computed fields already work.
